## C4-72 — official Hangover recipe removes C4-71 locale init fault; exposes distinct interval-tracker AV

**Official recipe, raw provenance:** `AndreRH/hangover@hangover-11.9` tree has one workflow, `.github/workflows/deb.yml`; `fex-pe` (`:85-132`) shallow-inits `fex`, copies `.packaging/ubuntu2204/fexpe/`, Docker-builds, extracts `/opt/fex/build/Bin/libwow64fex.dll`. Its gitlink is `86ff33bbe299cd8959a6610198c169b67ec419db` (same source as the vendored tree). `fexpe/Dockerfile:6-9` selects `/opt/bylaws-llvm-mingw-20240929-ucrt-ubuntu-20.04-x86_64/bin`, configures `Release`, `ENABLE_JEMALLOC_GLIBC_ALLOC=False`, `ENABLE_ASSERTIONS=False`, `toolchain_mingw.cmake`, `ENABLE_LTO=False`, `MINGW_TRIPLE=aarch64-w64-mingw32`, `TUNE_CPU=none`, `BUILD_TESTING=False`, builds `wow64fex` with make, then strips; no BUILD.md exists. Raw sources saved `scratch/c4-72-official/{deb.yml,fexpe-Dockerfile,foundation-ubuntu2204-Dockerfile,tree.json}`.

C4-72 runner is x86_64 Ubuntu 22.04 Docker because the pinned compiler is Linux x86_64; no alternate compiler. Recipe takes parameterized paths/mode/flags, uses official **Release** flags and sole authorized addition `-g` in Release C/C++ flags; no strip. Real final build: `41,525,248B`, SHA `4251c74b…72c3f6`, full live DWARF sections; cache confirms every official toggle. Receipts/commits: HANDOFF §C4-72.

**Live source-DLL result:** source DLL replaces stock only after hash-verified backup; canonical `timeout 240 pe10_probe.py fex` reaches FEX but does **not** return 42. C4-71's `moneypunct_byname<char,true>::init` (`+0x1696e4`, far `0x97`) is absent: recipe match kills that init fault. New terminal event: `elr=libwow64fex.dll+0x56c0`, `far=0x001911802E73B528`, AV / ret `3221225477`; terminal LR `+0x1697d0`. New DWARF symbol chain: `+0x56c0` → `FEXCore::IntervalList<u64>::Insert` lambda, `FEXCore/Utils/IntervalList.h:60`, faulting `ldr x14,[x12,#8]`; LR → `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` lambda, `Source/Windows/Common/InvalidationTracker.cpp:43`. **Close:** stock Hangover-11.9 DLL restored byte-identically; stock canonical `pe10 fex ret=42`, pe8/pe9 both backends/ramfb PASS. No V2 swap, net-zero. Installed DLL: stock. ## C4-71 — symbol DLL clears loader preflight; new FEX initialization fault

**Verdict: NEW-FAILURE-MODE; C4-67 FF/2 neither REPRODUCED nor ABSOLVED.** C4-70’s fixed 16MiB FS read cap is now `GAIA_NT_FS_FALLBACK_MAX_BYTES`/`NT_FS_FALLBACK_MAX`, decimal-validated at build time and defaulting to 64MiB. Audit found a coupled hazard: FS fallback previously bump-allocated the *ceiling* before any read; every absent FEX path probe leaked 64MiB because territory free is intentionally no-op. `FsSize()` now reads directory metadata first; misses/over-cap entries allocate nothing and valid entries allocate/read their exact size. This is the permanent staging shape: 64MiB acceptance ceiling, exact-byte territory consumption. Commits `b9d7146`/`e1be587`, both `GAIA_NO_RUN=1` verified.

**Source-DLL canonical boot** (C4-69B unstripped `42,377,216B`, SHA `030fc…51708`; corrected staging): `libwow64fex.dll` maps at `0x48d30000`, `starting FEX` prints, and zero `territory EXHAUSTED` lines occur. Initial arena fault/retry: `elr=0x48d3f438` / `+0xf438` → `FEXCore::LookupCache::FindBlock` (`FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/LookupCache.h:179`). Terminal distinct failure: `elr=0x48e996e4` / `+0x1696e4`, `far=0x97`, `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` / `NtRaiseException`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`; symbol: `std::__1::moneypunct_byname<char,true>::init(char const*)`, no DWARF line. `x0=+0x34c430` is `FEXCore::IR::IRRegClasses` data. PE-VAs were resolved with base `0x180000000` and the unstripped DLL; supplementary references: `+0x116f94` `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` (`Module.cpp:1009`), `+0x1074f4` `ConstrainedRAPass::Run` (`RegisterAllocationPass.cpp:661`), `+0x1d33c` libc++ `__tree::__find_equal` (`__tree:1874`). This is not the C4-67 FF/2 detector (`addr=0`, `State.rip=0`, V2 `ret=52`): V2/hello9 stopped; instrumentation targets N/A.

**Close:** stock Hangover-11.9 restored byte-identically; canonical stock `pe10 fex` re-proves `ExitStatus=0x2A`/`ret=42`; pe8/pe9/ramfb PASS; V2 swap untouched/net-zero; vendored FEX clean. Installed: **stock Hangover-11.9**. Receipts + commits: HANDOFF §C4-71.

## C4-70 — SELF-BUILT SYMBOL DLL: loader-preflight failure, before FEX execution

**Verdict: NEW-FAILURE-MODE; FF/2 neither REPRODUCED nor ABSOLVED.** C4-69B’s unstripped source DLL (`42,377,216B`; SHA-256 `030fc721f3231e998525c157647d0a50308643f2e6c9565771c336e961a51708`; FEX `86ff33bbe299cd8959a6610198c169b67ec419db`) replaced the consumed `stock/wow64-chain/libwow64fex.dll` only after copying stock Hangover-11.9 (`4,177,920B`; `d29099e1459471e5c1bbeb1a29d4273ac92657b6863c8d790ea4ab4557185490`) to `stock-hangover-11.9/`. FS capacity was independently calculated before write: existing `next_free=90390` sectors + source `82768` sectors = `173158` / `88,656,896B`; 64MiB’s 131072 sectors fails, 128MiB’s 262144 passes. `GAIA_DISK_MIN_SIZE_BYTES` now parameterizes the 128MiB policy; disk bytes hash-identically matched the source DLL after `GAIA_NO_RUN=1` populate.

**Actual canonical boot** (`pe10 fex`, `hello8_x86.exe`, timeout 240): `libwow64fex.dll: error: section raw data out of file bounds` → `NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=0xC000007B` → `pe10 ret=3221225595`; no `starting FEX`, no `CHALICE IMAGE: name=libwow64fex.dll`, no image-range REGION/LR/x30/dispatcher address. Thus `llvm-addr2line` has zero legitimate fault-PC inputs; no FEX function/line list exists. This is not an unexamined PE defect: `scratch/c4-70_source_dll_pe_validation.log` independently walks all 14 headers; every `rawptr + min(rawsize,vsize)` fits the full on-disk source length. The first named source location is floor parser `zoneA/pemod.c:198`, its bounds check; causal precondition is `zoneA/ntfile.c:104,117-119`, fixed `NT_FS_FALLBACK_MAX=16MiB` passed to `FsRead`; `zoneA/fs.c:103` truncates `e.size` to `maxlen`. The 42MiB file therefore reaches `PeMapImage` as a 16MiB prefix; DWARF trailing sections trip the correct guard.

**Stop/revert:** task’s different-init-failure rule applies. V2 `hello9c` and real `hello9` not run; C4-67’s FF/2 verdict unchanged; instrumentation targets N/A. Restored stock cache + disk payload byte-identically, reverted inherited C4-68 V2 `build.sh`/`shell.c` swap net-zero, then real stock canonical `pe10 fex` proved `ExitStatus=0x2A` / `ret=42`; pe8, pe9 (`wowbox64` + stock FEX), ramfb gates PASS. Close installed DLL: **stock Hangover-11.9**. Receipts: `scratch/c4-70_{install_size_and_build,source_dll_pe_validation,pe10_fex_canonical_{full,stdout},restore_stock_build,pe10_fex_stock_{full,stdout},pe8_gate,pe9_gate,ramfb_gate}.log`. Commits: `151583a`, `a9c1813`, `b212460`, `42989b5`, docs commit. Next authorized atom: parameterize/raise `NT_FS_FALLBACK_MAX`, retain unstripped source only for offline symbols, then rerun the ladder.

## C4-67 — Call-shape bisection: FEX's native call-dispatch machinery kills ANY indirect near call (`FF /2`, `CALL r/m32`) — memory-operand (`FF15`) or register-operand (`FF Dx`) alike, same-image or cross-image target alike — while a direct near call (`E8`, `CALL rel32`) survives clean. Fork (A) (DLL code pages never registered as guest-executable) independently REFUTED by Step-1 recon: kernel32.dll's and kernelbase.dll's own FULL ranges are confirmed live-registered with `InvalidationTracker` (`rc=0`) in every hello9 receipt on disk, well before the fatal call. Measurement atom, no fix.

**Task**: C4-64/65/66 established every guest-visible datum at hello9's fatal `FF15` cross-image call is correct (push executes, operand relocated, both IAT levels bound, real code at target) yet `State.rip` still zeros (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`) — the kill is inside FEX's own handling of this branch. Two forks named: (A) DLL code pages never registered as guest-executable with FEX (a floor-fixable `BTCpu*`-notify donor-contract gap, C4-46/53/54 precedent) vs (B) a genuine vendored-blob bug (needs JIT tracing). This atom bisects the CALL SHAPE itself (direct/indirect, memory/register operand, same-image/cross-image target) via three new minimal guest binaries, and separately checks fork (A) by direct recon (no build needed for that half).

**Step 1 — recon, no build** (`ntfile.c`/`ntboot.c`/`kmain.c`/`pemod.c` grep, `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp` read, vendored tree UNCHANGED): exactly two `BTCpu*`/notify call sites exist on this floor, both already fixed-and-proven-live by prior atoms — `NtMapViewOfSection`'s `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` (C4-46/48), firing UNCONDITIONALLY for every dynamically-mapped guest x86 (Machine `0x14C`) image other than `ntdll.dll` (which has its own dedicated `BTCpuProcessInit`→`HandleImageMap` path, C4-29); and `NtProtectVirtualMemory`'s direct `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` call (C4-53, bypassing the C4-52-diagnosed swallowing wrapper). Grepped `scratch/c4-6{1,4,5,6}_*full.log` for `base=0x5D09`/`base=0x5E1C`: **`CHALICE C4-46: BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection name=syswow64/kernel32.dll base=0x5D090000 size=0x119000 rc=0x0`** and the matching `kernelbase.dll base=0x5E1C0000 size=0x435000 rc=0x0` line appear in EVERY one of those four receipts — `rc=0`/`STATUS_SUCCESS` confirms `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`(`Module.cpp:1009`)→`HandleImageMap`(`Module.cpp:175`)→`InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` genuinely ran and inserted BOTH DLLs' ENTIRE ranges (not a stub/partial page) into `XIntervals` before hello9's fatal call ever executes. hello9's own crash target (`0x5D09D7AC`, kernel32.dll's `GetStdHandle` thunk, C4-65/66) sits well inside the registered `[0x5D090000,0x5D1A9000)` range. Six OTHER exported `BTCpu*` notify APIs in the same vendored file (`BTCpuFlushInstructionCache2`/`Heavy`, `BTCpuNotifyMemoryDirty`/`Alloc`/`Free`, `BTCpuNotifyReadFile`, `BTCpuNotifyUnmapViewOfSection`) are never called anywhere on this floor — a real, separate, still-open donor-gap class, but none of them apply to a freshly-mapped image that has never since been protected, freed, or read via `NtReadFile`, so none are implicated in this specific crash. **Fork (A) verdict: REFUTED for this fault** — the DLL code pages ARE registered as guest-executable, confirmed live via receipt grep, not inferred from source-reading alone.

**Step 2 — test matrix, three new guest binaries** (`chalice/hello9{d,c,b}_x86.c`, `mkpe32.sh` extended to build all 5 `*_x86` artifacts together; every opcode verified via `objdump -d` on the actual build artifact BEFORE any boot, per this floor's own measure-don't-guess doctrine):
- **V1, `hello9d_x86.c`**: `static int helper(void){return 51;} long PeMain(void){return helper();}` — zero imports. Disasm: `e8 08 00 00 00` (`CALL rel32`, direct, same-image). Baseline: block-linking, same-image.
- **V2, `hello9c_x86.c`**: `static int helper(void){return 52;} static int(*fnptr)(void)=helper; long PeMain(void){return fnptr();}` — zero imports, one `.reloc` HIGHLOW entry for `fnptr`'s own absolute initializer (the same reloc mechanism C4-65 already proved this floor applies correctly). Disasm: `ff 15 00 40 40 00` (`CALL dword ptr [0x404000]`, memory-indirect, SAME-image target). Isolates the memory-indirect FORM while holding target class constant vs V1.
- **V3, `hello9b_x86.c`**: `long PeMain(void){ void*target; long dummy; asm("movl __imp__GetStdHandle@4,%0":"=r"(target)); asm("pushl $-11\n calll *%1\n movl %%eax,%0":"=r"(dummy):"r"(target):...); return 53; }` — `-lkernel32`, pulls the real Import Directory entry for `GetStdHandle`. Disasm: `a1 40 20 40 00` (`mov eax,[0x402040]`, the exact bound IAT slot address the Import Table's own `addr` field names) then, separately, `ff d6` (`call esi`, register-indirect — `target` gets moved `esi<-eax` by an intervening spill/reload at `-O0`, the compiler's own register choice, not requested) — genuinely register-indirect, NOT memory-indirect, confirmed by the absence of any `ff15`/`ff25` opcode at the call site itself. Build-time design note: a typed `extern void *x asm("__imp__GetStdHandle@4")` C declaration was tried FIRST and REJECTED after `objdump -d` showed it compiles to a DOUBLE indirection (`movl 0x402000,%eax; movl (%eax),%eax`) — lld's MinGW auto-import runtime-pseudo-reloc mechanism for untyped/undecorated data-symbol externs, patched only by a real Windows loader at process-load time, which this floor's `PeMapImage` never performs; the naked inline-asm symbol reference (used in the final version) produces a clean single load instead, confirmed via the same tool.

**Boots** (established swap method, `build.sh`/`shell.c` two-line diff per `git show 19e4484`'s own precedent; SERIAL, one checkpoint commit per swap, `timeout 240 python3 scratch/pe10_probe.py fex` each):
```
V1 (hello9d, checkpoint e2b1645): NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x33 -> pe10 ret=51
  0 occurrences of elr=0x26D0C02B0 / rip=0x0 anywhere in the 12442-line receipt. PASS.
V2 (hello9c, checkpoint 03fcca8): NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0 -> pe10 ret=3221225477
  10x pc=0x26D0C02B0, 22x rip=0x0 -- byte-identical fault CLASS to hello9's own C4-58..66 crash. DIES.
V3 (hello9b, checkpoint 2103a5c): NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0 -> pe10 ret=3221225477
  9x pc=0x26D0C02B0, 22x rip=0x0 -- same fault CLASS. DIES.
```
Receipts: `scratch/c4-67_v{1,2,3}_receipt{,_full}.log`.

**Verdict, read honestly against the task's own 4-bucket matrix**: the literal result (V1 PASS, V2 DIES) already satisfies the task's own "memory-indirect form broken generally → (B), JIT tracing next" bucket — but V3 (register-indirect, cross-image, via the SAME real bound `GetStdHandle` IAT slot C4-57/65/66 already proved correctly and durably bound) ALSO dying means the bucket as pre-named is too narrow: the true discriminator is not "memory operand" (V3 has none at the call site) and not "cross-image target" (V2 has none, and still dies) — it is the **`FF /2` indirect-call opcode class itself**, `CALL r/m32` in either addressing submode, versus the **`E8` direct-call opcode**, `CALL rel32`, which alone survives clean. This is consistent with, and sharpens, fork (B): a genuine vendored-blob dispatch bug scoped to indirect near calls, independent of operand addressing mode and independent of same-vs-cross-image targeting — and it is fully consistent with Step 1's own direct refutation of fork (A) (the crash target's own page is already correctly registered as executable; a registration gap cannot explain a fault that also fires for a SAME-image, ALREADY-registered-since-Phase-0 target like V2's).

**Named next atom (C4-68)**: JIT/blob tracing of FEX's own indirect-call (`FF /2`, `CALL r/m32`) dispatch path in the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` BLOB (not the `FEX/Source` tree — C4-49/50/58-66's own established precedent that the source tree can mispredict the real compiled blob) — scoped now to indirect-call handling specifically, no longer needing to check registration (Step 1 closed that line) or operand addressing mode (V2/V3 both die identically) — find where `CALL r/m32`'s JIT translation/dispatch diverges from the already-proven-correct `CALL rel32` path V1 exercises successfully.

**Canonical-state restore + gate ladder**: all three TEMP swaps reverted in one commit (`dc6c8d9`), `git diff f9d4fe9 -- build.sh shell.c` confirmed EMPTY (net-zero); PE-timestamp/build-artifact churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit and after every gate/boot; `hello9{,d,c,b}_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked (rebuilt via the now-5-binary `mkpe32.sh`), standard convention. `pe10 fex` re-run TWICE post-revert (`scratch/c4-67_pe10_fex_canonical_{prefix,final}_receipt{,_full}.log`) — both `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` HOLDS both times**. **Gates** (`scratch/c4-67_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` GATE PASS. `pe9_gate.py` GATE PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`). `ramfb_gate.py` GATE PASS, 7/7 pattern checks. No regression anywhere.

**Commits** (source-only, each stage separate): `2730b33` (3 new call-shape-bisection guest binaries + `mkpe32.sh` extension, verified opcodes) → `e2b1645`→boot(V1) → `03fcca8`→boot(V2) → `2103a5c`→boot(V3) → `dc6c8d9` (revert all three, net-zero) → canonical reconfirm + gate ladder → this documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-67), final commit. Vendored `FEX/` tree read-only throughout. Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-66 — kernel32.dll's OWN internal Import Directory slot for kernelbase!GetStdHandle (the SECOND indirection behind hello9's `FF15` call — real Windows-shaped: kernel32.dll's exported `GetStdHandle` is itself a thin thunk, `8bff 55 8bec 5d ff25 <imm32>`, tail-jumping through kernel32's own IAT into kernelbase's real implementation) measured genuinely UNBOUND at bind-time (`0x0003FCAA`, not a valid code VA), FIXED (generalized floor-side i386 IAT binder — every mapped PE32 image's own imports now bound, not just the boot EXE), engagement CONFIRMED live (the exact measured slot now holds the exact correct kernelbase VA) — but the hello9 crash is UNCHANGED, proving this indirection was never the actual blocker either; C4-65's own verdict (fault fully inside FEX's native call-dispatch, upstream of even the FIRST indirection) stands, now with the LAST floor-satisfiable alternative directly ruled out by fix-then-reboot rather than inference.

**Task**: C4-65 proved hello9's OWN IAT slot (bound by C4-57's binder, pointing AT kernel32.dll's exported `GetStdHandle` thunk code, `0x5D09D7AC`) stays correctly bound through the fatal call — but never checked what THAT thunk code itself does once (if) reached. Disassembly of the real, vendored `chalice/stock/i386-windows/kernel32.dll` (`rva=0xd7ac` for `GetStdHandle`, `rva=0xf9bc` for `WriteFile`) shows both are NOT full implementations — they're 12-byte thin thunks: 2-byte hotpatch NOP-equivalent (`8bff`), `push ebp`(`55`), `mov ebp,esp`(`8bec`), `pop ebp`(`5d`), then `jmp dword ptr [imm32]`(`ff25 <imm32>`) — the `imm32` operand is the ABSOLUTE VA of a slot inside kernel32.dll's OWN Import Directory, holding kernelbase.dll's real `GetStdHandle` address. Grepped `ntfile.c` end to end: `nt_iat_bind_walk` (C4-57) only ever walked the BOOT EXE's Import Directory — nothing on this floor ever bound kernel32.dll's (or kernelbase.dll's, or ntdll.dll's) own imports from each other. If hello9's `FF15` call DID correctly reach kernel32's thunk (decodes fine, real code, matches C4-65's own "slot correctly bound" finding), the thunk's own SECOND `jmp [slot]` would still branch to garbage if THIS slot were unbound — same zero-`rip`-shaped fault signature as C4-58 through C4-65's own already-documented crash, but from a completely different, previously-unchecked address, undetectable by C4-65's own probe (which only ever watched the boot EXE's own slot).

**Salvage note**: the Step-1 diagnostic itself (`nt_c466_diag`, `ntfile.c`) was written and wired by a PRIOR, STALLED lane — found uncommitted in the working tree, its own embedded comment block carrying the full task context quoted above. This atom hardened it (an in-bounds guard, `slot_addr < k32base || slot_addr >= k32base+k32size`, before the `rd32(slotp)` deref — kernel32's own IAT necessarily lives inside its own mapped image; an out-of-range operand would mean unrelocated, preferred-base-relative, and NOT safe to dereference blind), committed the checkpoint, then built/booted/completed it.

**Step 1 — live diagnostic results** (`scratch/c4-66_diag_receipt{,_full}.log`, fires once at the SAME C4-57 bind-event gate, both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped):
```
CHALICE C4-66: k32_thunk_bytes@k32base+0xd7ac= 0x000000008B55FF8B 0x0000000025FF5DEC 0x000000005D0CD664 0x0000000090909090
CHALICE C4-66: k32_own_iat_slot_addr=0x000000005D0CD664 k32_own_iat_slot_live_value=0x000000000003FCAA
CHALICE C4-66: kernelbase_GetStdHandle_code@kbbase+0x4acb0= 0x0000000089559066 0x00000000084D8BE5 0x000000000018A164 0x00000000F9830000
```
Decoded byte-for-byte (`rd32` is little-endian, so each printed dword's bytes read low-to-high left-to-right once unpacked): thunk bytes = `8b ff 55 8b ec 5d ff 25 <64 D6 0C 5D> 90 90 90 90` — an EXACT match to the predicted `8bff 55 8bec 5d ff25 <imm32>` shape, confirming real, genuine kernel32.dll export-thunk code, not a decode error. `slot_addr=0x5D0CD664` — the hardening guard did NOT fire (no "OUTSIDE k32 image" print), proving this address IS inside kernel32.dll's own mapped range `[0x5D090000, 0x5D090000+k32size)` — i.e. CORRECTLY RELOCATED (kernel32.dll's real `ImageBase` is `0x7B800000`, verified via `struct.unpack` against the stock DLL's own Optional Header; the live value's own arithmetic — raw file operand `0x7B83D664` (backed out: `slot_addr - (k32base - 0x7B800000)`) plus the real `k32base - preferred` delta — reproduces `0x5D0CD664` exactly). Traced WHY, since this floor's own `NtMapViewOfSection` (`ntfile.c`) intentionally passes `apply_relocs=0` for every guest-mapped DLL (C3c-2q's own established convention — the GUEST'S OWN ntdll32 loader unconditionally self-relocates any module not at its preferred base regardless of this floor's own signal, so applying relocs floor-side would double-patch): kernel32.dll's own map call precedes kernelbase.dll's by several guest syscalls (real Wine's own `LdrLoadDll`/`build_module` chain, already proven guest-executed and correct for THIS exact dependency graph by C4-42 through C4-45), giving the guest's OWN x86 relocation-processing code (real `ntdll32.dll` binary, genuine machine code) ample opportunity to run over kernel32.dll's image before this atom's diagnostic (fired at the LATER kernelbase.dll map event) ever reads it — the relocation gap theory is REFUTED for this specific operand, by the SAME mechanism (guest self-relocation) C4-65 already established handles the boot EXE's own operand, just executed by a different piece of guest code (the loader, not `PeMain`). `k32_own_iat_slot_live_value=0x0003FCAA` — NOT a valid kernelbase code VA (independently confirmed: `kernelbase_GetStdHandle_code@kbbase+0x4acb0` decodes to a genuine x86 function prologue, `66 90`(2-byte hotpatch NOP) `55`(`push ebp`) `89 e5`(`mov ebp,esp`) `8b 4d 08`(`mov ecx,[ebp+8]`) — real, executable code, at `kbbase+0x4acb0 = 0x5E1C0000+0x4ACB0 = 0x5E20ACB0`, the value the slot SHOULD hold once bound) — matches the task's own predicted "unbound" signature ("small RVA-like/garbage/0") exactly. **STOP LAW check**: "if slot value live-reads a correct kernelbase code VA, stop after docs — no fix" — `0x0003FCAA ≠ 0x5E20ACB0`, does NOT apply; proceeded to Step 2.

**Step 2 — FIX** (`ntfile.c`/`pemod.c`/`pemod.h`, gated on Step 1 confirming unbound): generalized the C4-57 binder end to end. `nt_iat_bind_walk(exe_base, exe_size, k32base, k32size, kbbase, kbsize)` (fixed boot-EXE-only caller, fixed kernel32-then-kernelbase guessed resolution, no ordinal support) replaced by `nt_iat_bind_image(img_base, img_size)` (any image, any resolution target, name+ordinal) plus a new helper `nt_image_find_by_basename` (case-insensitive match of an import descriptor's raw DLL name against the LIVE image registry's own trailing path component — handles the registry's synthetic `syswow64/` prefix transparently, no separate lookup table) and a new pemod.c export-parser sibling `pe_find_export_by_ordinal` (same header-dispatch/error-contract as the existing `pe_find_named_export`, indexes `AddressOfFunctions[ordinal - Base]` directly instead of scanning the name table). `nt_iat_bind_maybe` now binds the boot EXE (as before) THEN loops the entire `nt_images[]` registry at the SAME one-shot gate, calling `nt_iat_bind_image` on every OTHER currently-mapped entry — `nt_iat_bind_image`'s own PE32-magic check silently skips anything that isn't a genuine i386 image (the native ARM64 wow64-support DLLs — `wow64.dll`/`wow64win.dll`/`win32u.dll`/the CPU backend — all real, all present in the registry, all correctly excluded with ZERO hardcoded name list), and its own empty-Import-Directory check silently skips anything with nothing to bind. IRON LAW held: no hardcoded addresses or counts anywhere in the new code — every loop terminates on the descriptor/thunk arrays' own null terminators (`IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR`/`IMAGE_THUNK_DATA32` null-entry convention) or the registry's own `used` flags, identical discipline to the pre-existing C4-57 code.

**Fix engagement, live-confirmed exactly as diagnosed** (`scratch/c4-66_fix_receipt{,_full}.log`): kernel32.dll's own import pass (`binding IAT for image base=0x5D090000`) resolves against BOTH kernelbase.dll and ntdll.dll (its real two dependency DLLs) and includes: `CHALICE C4-66: bound GetStdHandle -> 0x000000005E20ACB0` — the EXACT VA this atom's own Step-1 diagnostic independently derived as correct, written into the EXACT slot address (`0x5D0CD664`) Step 1 measured — 917/929 name imports bound, 12 forwarder-shaped failures (kernelbase re-exports functions like `HeapAlloc`/`EnterCriticalSection`/`QueryPerformanceCounter`/`ExitThread`/`HeapFree`/`HeapReAlloc`/`HeapSize`/`LeaveCriticalSection`/`QueryPerformanceFrequency`/`QueryUnbiasedInterruptTime` as PE forwarders to a further DLL, `pe_find_named_export`'s pre-existing `-2` return, "not chased further", C4-51's own established convention — an OFFLINE python re-derivation of the exact same import/export tables from the stock DLLs, done as a cross-check, independently found the identical 10 forwarder cases, confirming this is genuine forwarder shape, not a resolution bug). kernelbase.dll's own import pass (`base=0x5E1C0000`) resolves against ntdll.dll: 429/432 bound, 3 similar forwarder-shaped gaps.

## C4-65 — BOTH the RELOC gap and the IAT gap this atom set out to diagnose are REFUTED by direct, continuous live measurement, not by re-reading `pemod.c`/`ntfile.c` and trusting the code looks correct. hello9_x86.exe's own `FF15 5c 20 40 00` (`CALL dword ptr [0x40205c]`) instruction operand, read live from the mapped guest image right after `PeMapImage` returns, is `0x4766205c` — NOT the raw file value `0x40205c` — proving `pemod.c`'s existing `apply_relocs=1` `IMAGE_REL_BASED_HIGHLOW` walk (already correctly implemented, this atom traced it end to end) DOES reach and correctly patch this exact instruction. A NEW per-syscall extension of C4-64's own probe (watching the live dword at that exact, now-correct address on every single dispatch) proves the slot holds the file's raw unbound thunk RVA (`0x2068`) only until C4-57's existing IAT binder fires (well before `PeMain`/`exe_entry` ever executes), then transitions EXACTLY ONCE to the real resolved `GetStdHandle` VA (`0x5D09D7AC`) and stays there, unchanged, across ~5800 consecutive probe lines — including the precise syscall boundary C4-64 pinned as the last dispatch before the zeroing, and every zero-`rip` line through the terminal crash. A correct `CALL dword ptr [0x4766205c]` at that exact moment therefore MUST compute `0x5D09D7AC`, never `0` — yet FEX's own tracked `State.rip` still legitimately zeros there, byte-identical to C4-64's own receipt (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`). Verdict: **neither theory (a) nor (b) applies** — this floor's own guest-memory image is proven correct and durably stable at the moment of use; the discrepancy is fully inside FEX's own native call/branch-dispatch machinery (vendored `libwow64fex.dll`), reading or computing a value that demonstrably does not match what this floor's memory genuinely holds. C4-66 (the gated fix) NOT ATTEMPTED — no floor-satisfiable target exists to fix, per this atom's own explicit stop condition.

**Task**: diagnose which of two named gaps explains hello9's `State.rip=0` zeroing event (C4-64's own pinned syscall boundary) — (a) a RELOC gap (the `FF15` operand `0x40205c` is preferred-base-absolute; if the floor never applies `.reloc`, the guest dereferences the wrong, unrelocated address) or (b) an IAT gap (even if correctly relocated, the slot at `0x4766205c` holds the file's unbound `0x2068` thunk RVA unless something binds it, and unless that binding survives to the moment of use). Read the floor's PE32 mapper (`ntboot.c`/`pemod.c`/`ntfile.c`) for how the boot EXE gets mapped and whether any reloc/import processing runs, checked for IAT-binding precedent (found: C4-57's own binder), and instrumented a narrow live diagnostic per the task's own suggestion (dump guest dwords at `0x40205c` and `0x4766205c` after map) — extended into a continuous per-syscall watch once the first snapshot proved inconclusive on its own.

**Step 0 — precedent check, `ntboot.c`'s own Phase-0 exe map call**: `nt_boot_process` maps the boot EXE via `PeMapImage(exe_bytes, exe_len, "pe5-exe", nt_territory_alloc, 0 /*do_imports*/, 1 /*apply_relocs*/, &exe_base, &exe_size, &exe_entry)` — `apply_relocs=1`, NOT 0 (the `apply_relocs=0` convention is `NtMapViewOfSection`'s own, `ntfile.c`, for DLLs the guest's real Wine loader would otherwise self-relocate a second time — `pemod.c`'s own comment trail, HANDOFF scar #6 — the boot EXE itself has no such guest-side loader pass, per C4-56/57's own established mechanism, `do_imports=0`/direct Eip-seed bypasses `LdrInitializeThunk` entirely, so `ntboot.c` keeps `apply_relocs=1` deliberately, same as its own ntdll bootstrap map two lines above). `pemod.c`'s `PeMapImage` reloc walker (traced end to end, source read, this atom): handles BOTH `IMAGE_REL_BASED_DIR64` (`u64b val=rd64(target); wr64(target,val+delta)`) AND `IMAGE_REL_BASED_HIGHLOW` (`u32b val=rd32(target); wr32(target,val+(u32b)delta)`) — the C4-5 atom's own comment names this explicitly ("same apply_relocs semantics as DIR64"). On its face, this predicts relocation SHOULD already be correctly applied to hello9_x86.exe's own code at map time — this atom's whole job was to verify that prediction empirically rather than take it on faith, since the established C4-64 crash contradicts it at first glance (a correctly-relocated, correctly-bound call should never read 0).

**Step 1 — offline static confirmation of the INPUT (no boot)**: `python3 struct` walk of `hello9_x86.exe` on disk (rebuilt fresh via `mkpe32.sh` this atom, confirmed byte-for-byte consistent with C4-64's own already-committed static disasm): `.reloc` directory `rva=0x4000 sz=0x14` — raw bytes `00100000 14000000 1730223048305c3062300000` decode to ONE block, `page_rva=0x1000`, `block_sz=0x14` (6 entries of 2 bytes): `type=3(HIGHLOW) off=0x17`, `type=3 off=0x22`, `type=3 off=0x48`, `type=3 off=0x5c`, `type=3 off=0x62`, `type=0(ABSOLUTE, padding terminator) off=0x0` — 5 real fixups + 1 padding entry, matching `nentries=(block_sz-8)/2=6` exactly, zero malformed-block risk. RVA `page_rva+0x17=0x1017` is EXACTLY the `FF15` instruction's own 4-byte operand position (`entry_rva(0x1000)+opcode(0x15,2 bytes: ff 15)+0x17=0x1017`, confirmed against the raw `.text` bytes: `text[0x17:0x1b]=5c204000`, i.e. `0x40205c` LE). The other four entries (`0x1022`,`0x1048`,`0x105c`,`0x1062`) were also checked and match the remaining absolute-address references in `.text` — a second `FF15 60204000` call (`GetStdHandle`+`WriteFile`, the two real imports), a `LEA ECX,[0x402000]` (`0x1022`), and the two `FF25` IAT-thunk `JMP` stubs at the end of `.text` (`0x105c`/`0x1062`) — every absolute reference in the file is covered, nothing left un-relocatable. Arithmetic: `delta = alloc − image_base`; this run's own `alloc` (`exe_base`, live-measured) `= 0x47660000`, `image_base` (file's own `OPT_IMAGEBASE32`) `= 0x400000` → `delta = 0x47260000`; `0x40205c + 0x47260000 = 0x4766205c` — matches the correct, expected, delta-shifted IAT slot address (`exe_base+IAT_dir_rva(0x205c)`) EXACTLY. Every static input checks out; nothing here explains the crash — the only way to settle it is to read the ACTUAL post-map guest memory, live.

**Step 2 — live diagnostic #1, Phase-0 map-time snapshot** (`ntboot.c`, gated on `wow64_mode`, fires right after `nt_set_main_image_info`, three plain volatile dword reads, commit `d1a3578`): `CHALICE C4-65: preferred_base_dword@0x40205c=0x00000000 call_operand_live@exe_entry+0x17=0x000000004766205C iat_slot_live@exe_base+0x205c=0x0000000000002068`. Three findings in one line: (i) the literal preferred-base address `0x40205c` genuinely reads `0`, live, no fault — traced to `kmain.c`'s own `mmu_on()`: `l2_dev_table` maps `[0x200000,0x40000000)` as valid device-nGnRnE identity territory (2MB blocks) EXCEPT the explicitly null-guarded `l2_dev_table[0]` (`[0x0,0x200000)`, C4-28's own fix) — `0x40205c` falls in `l2_dev_table[2]` (`[0x400000,0x600000)`), a real, valid page-table entry, just not backed by any actual QEMU peripheral in that range, so a genuine ARM64 LDR there succeeds and QEMU's own bus silently returns 0 for the unassigned read — matches the task's own "if zero-filled mapped territory, that alone explains call 0" framing exactly, BUT only proves the address WOULD read 0 if dereferenced, not that the guest's own instruction actually reads THIS address. (ii) the LIVE in-memory operand at `exe_entry+0x17` is `0x4766205c`, NOT `0x40205c` — direct, live proof the HIGHLOW reloc pass genuinely ran and correctly patched this exact byte range, at Phase-0 map time, before any guest code ever executes. (iii) the IAT slot at that correct address reads the file's raw unbound value `0x2068` at THIS point in boot — expected and correct, since this print fires immediately after the EXE itself maps, well before `kernel32.dll`/`kernelbase.dll` (and therefore C4-57's own binder) are even mapped. Finding (ii) alone already REFUTES theory (a) outright; finding (iii) is a snapshot too early to settle theory (b) either way.

**Step 3 — live diagnostic #2, continuous per-syscall watch** (`ntsvc.c`'s `nt_dispatch_log`, extending C4-64's own `nt_wow64_live_state_probe` hook with a fourth read, `c465_iat0 = *(volatile u32b*)(boot_exe_base+0x205c)`; `nt_find_boot_exe` — C4-57's own private helper, `ntfile.c` — exposed non-static for this purpose, `ntfile.h` prototype added; commit `cf53bee`). Reused the established hello9 swap method (`build.sh`/`shell.c` two-line diff, `9c8eb2f`'s own pattern, this atom's own swap commit `05bed7b`, revert `77200f8`, net-zero confirmed via `git diff d1a3578 HEAD -- build.sh shell.c` returning empty) — booted twice (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`), receipts `scratch/c4-65_hello9_boot_receipt{,_full}.log`. `grep -o "c465_iat0=0x[0-9A-F]*" | sort -u` over the full ~11245-line transcript yields exactly TWO distinct values: `0x00000000000002068` (before bind) and `0x000000005D09D7AC` (from bind onward) — no third value, no flicker, no reversion. Located the exact transition: line ~5410-5415, `CHALICE C4-57: both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped -- binding boot exe IAT` / `import DLL=KERNEL32.dll` / `bound GetStdHandle -> 0x000000005D09D7AC` / `bound WriteFile -> 0x000000005D09F9BC` / `IAT bind done bound=2 failed=0` — confirms C4-57's own binder engages correctly for a hello9 swap boot (matches that atom's own citation, re-verified live rather than assumed). From that transcript line through the END of the boot (line ~11244, well past the fatal crash), `c465_iat0` is grepped as `0x000000005D09D7AC` on EVERY SINGLE probe line, with zero exceptions — including transcript line 11153 (`svc id=80 rip=0x0000000047661000 rsp=0x00000000488CC000 [rsp]=0x00000000488D0000 c465_iat0=0x000000005D09D7AC`, the exact `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` call C4-64 pinned as the last syscall before the zeroing) and line 11156 (`svc id=49 rip=0x0000000000000000 ... c465_iat0=0x000000005D09D7AC`, the FIRST zero-`rip` reading) through every subsequent zero-`rip` line to the terminal `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0` / `pe10 ret=3221225477`. Cross-checked against C4-60's own `NtProtectVirtualMemory` raw-arg diagnostic for this same window: no protect call anywhere near this transcript region targets the IAT's own page (`0x47662000`, the next page after the one `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000,0x1000)` call actually protects) — nothing plausibly re-protects, invalidates, or overwrites the slot. This is a stronger result than a snapshot could give: theory (b) is refuted not merely "at bind time" but continuously, bracketing the exact instant of use.

**Step 4 — synthesis**: Step 2(ii) proves the operand location is correct; Step 3 proves the value at that location is correct and stable through the fault. A textbook x86 `CALL dword ptr [mem32]` reading a correctly-relocated pointer to a correctly-bound, durably-stable, valid function VA cannot legitimately compute `0` — yet `State.rip` reads exactly `0` at this precise call, in a fresh boot this atom captured independently (not reusing C4-64's own receipt), landing in the identical `elr=0x26D0C02B0` `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` trampoline (C4-58/59/61/62/63) and terminating identically (`pe10 ret=3221225477`/`0xC0000005`). Since BOTH floor-side data-preparation gaps this task named are now proven, by direct and continuous live measurement (not code-reading), to be absent, the remaining explanation is necessarily internal to FEX's own native call/branch-dispatch machinery (the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob — `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/{Dispatcher,JIT,OpcodeDispatcher,BranchOps}` per C4-63's own site enumeration, or `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`'s `HandleSyscallImpl`, C4-64's candidate #8, independently re-refuted this atom too since its own input `[rsp]` and the IAT slot it would need to be corrupting are BOTH proven valid throughout) — reading or computing this exact guest memory address through some path this floor's own instrumentation, sitting at the syscall-dispatch boundary, cannot observe (the divergence happens strictly BETWEEN two syscalls, inside native/JIT'd guest execution this floor has no hook into, same limitation every C4-58-through-64 atom already worked within). Same verdict CLASS as every predecessor atom in this specific sub-thread (not floor-satisfiable) — but this atom is the first to directly, empirically eliminate BOTH of the two specific mechanisms (reloc application, IAT binding persistence) a floor-side fix could plausibly have targeted, closing the loop C4-56/57 opened.

**C4-66 verdict**: NOT ATTEMPTED. Per the task's own explicit law ("If diagnosis REFUTES the IAT/reloc theory (slot reads nonzero-and-valid live), STOP at C4-65, no speculative fix, document verdict"), and since this atom's own Step 3 shows the slot reading nonzero-and-valid CONTINUOUSLY (not just once), there is no floor-satisfiable relocation or IAT-binding gap to fix — applying base relocations a second time, or re-binding an already-correctly-and-durably-bound IAT, would be a functional no-op against already-correct code, not a fix. hello9's `WriteFile`-milestone (`hello9: the door opens`) remains unreached; the blocker is unchanged in class from C4-58 through C4-64, now with the floor-side possibility set exhausted for this specific mechanism.

## C4-64 — CANDIDATE #8 REFUTED as the corruption SOURCE, zeroing event PINPOINTED to a single syscall boundary: hello8 (canonical) shows `State.rip==0` at ZERO of 2287 live per-syscall probe readings; hello9 shows it at 19 consecutive readings, starting the probe line immediately AFTER the shared `NtProtectVirtualMemory(*BaseAddress=0x47661000)` call (`svc id=80`) and persisting to the terminal fault/crash. At that exact boundary hello8 performs a textbook x86 `ret` (`rsp+=4`, new `rip` == the `[rsp]` value the PRIOR probe line recorded) landing cleanly in the pre-arranged WOW64 exit stub (`0x488D0000`) and reaching `NtTerminateProcess(0x2A)` (`pe10 ret=42`); hello9 instead performs a `call`-shaped push (`rsp-=0x24`, a FRESH return address `0x4766101B` appears on the guest stack) and `State.rip` reads `0` at that same instant, never a popped value — hello9 never issues the second (`0x488D0000`) `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call hello8 relies on, and instead runs into the already-documented `elr=0x26D0C02B0` self-fault trampoline (C4-58/59/61/62/63) six probe-lines later, terminating via unhandled `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (`pe10 ret=3221225477`/`0xC0000005`). `[rsp]` is validly mapped and nonzero at every one of hello9's 19 zero-`rip` readings (constant `0x4766101B`) — ruling out a zeroed-stack-slot restore. Verdict: **branch (c)** — the zeroing is hello9-specific, happens OUTSIDE syscall dispatch (between two adjacent syscalls, `id=80`→`id=49`), and is a genuine native-control-flow/JIT-cache-miss divergence between the two guest images at this one call site, not a corruption of `HandleSyscallImpl`'s restore mechanism. No fix applied.

**Task**: finish C4-64 (a prior lane completed all boots/diagnostics/commits and died at the wall before gates/analysis/docs) — rerun the two incomplete gates, extract and diff the full ~2200-line probe sequence from both the hello8-canonical and hello9 full receipts, answer the hello8-rip==0 discriminator named in the handoff spec, state the timing conclusion on `rip(N+1)==[rsp](N)`, diff the shared tail window, and write this doc. No boot, no diagnostic change, no fix — salvage/analysis atom only.

**Gates, rerun clean** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-64_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` receipt confirmed pre-existing (`pe8 ret=78`, GATE PASS, no rerun needed). `pe9_gate.py` — banked receipt was 0 bytes (interrupted mid-run by a too-short wrapper timeout); rerun with a 240s wrapper (the script's own internal per-command waits sum to ~124s plus a ~20s connect window and a 5s teardown, so the prior lane's shorter timeout killed it before its own final `transcript` write): both backends `pe9 ret=0`/`pe9 fex ret=0`, GATE PASS, clean, no flake. `ramfb_gate.py` — never ran; rerun clean, 7/7 pattern checks OK, GATE PASS, no flake (no `BrokenPipeError`/`no serial socket` transient hit either run).

**Canonical exit, formally confirmed**: `grep -n "ExitStatus" c4-64_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt_full.log` → one hit, `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x000000000000002A`, immediately followed by `pe10 ret=42` in the tail — the banked "ExitStatus=0x2A → ret=42 holds" claim is CONFIRMED, not just asserted.

**Probe design + gregs-offset derivation, read out of the committed diagnostic** (`5b3b9af`, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntfile.c,ntfile.h,ntsvc.c}`, not re-derived this atom, cited from that commit's own header comment in `ntfile.h`): `nt_wow64_live_state_probe()` extends C4-59's `nt_wow64_live_state_rip` TEB→`TlsSlots[16]`→`CurrentFrame` chain to also read `CpuStateFrame::State.gregs[REG_RSP]` and the 4 bytes at that guest-stack address — the two quantities candidate #8 (`WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`, `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:434-464`, confirmed this atom by direct read of the vendored file at that path) reads/writes on every real `Nt*` syscall: `ReturnRIP = *(uint32_t*)(Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP])` (`Module.cpp:434`) then, gated on `Frame->State.rip` already equalling one of two fixed bridge-stub addresses (`BridgeInstrs::Syscall`/`BridgeInstrs::UnixCall`, `Module.cpp:449/456`), `Frame->State.rip = ReturnRIP` (`Module.cpp:464`). Offset derivation, confirmed this atom by direct field-order read of `FEX/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h:91-106` (no compile, same method C4-63's bonus section and this diagnostic's own header comment both used): `InlineJITBlockHeader` u64@0 (`:91`) + `DeferredSignalRefCount` u64@8 (`:94`) + `pf_raw` u32@0x10 + `af_raw` u32@0x14 (`:101-102`) + `rip` u64@0x18 (`:104`, C4-59/63-confirmed, header comment itself: "Current core's RIP. May not be entirely accurate while JIT is active") → `gregs[16]` u64[]@0x20 (`:106`). `REG_RSP=4` confirmed this atom at `FEX/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/X86Enums.h:15`. `gregs[REG_RSP]` absolute offset = `0x20 + 4*8 = 0x40` — matches the diagnostic's own `current_frame + 0x40` read exactly. Timing note, from the diagnostic's own committed comment (`ntsvc.c`, `nt_dispatch_log`): the probe fires nested inside `HandleSyscallImpl`, AFTER the current dispatch's `ReturnRIP` read but BEFORE its `Frame->State.{rip,gregs[RSP]}=...` restore write for THAT dispatch — each printed line therefore shows the PREVIOUS dispatch's already-committed `rip`/`rsp` restore, plus the CURRENT dispatch's not-yet-consumed `[rsp]` (the value its own restore write will use next).

**Step 1 — full probe extraction**: `grep -c "CHALICE C4-64: svc"` → hello8 (`c4-64_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt_full.log`) 2288 lines, hello9 (`c4-64_hello9_boot_receipt_full.log`) 2223 lines. Parsed with a strict `id=/rip=/rsp=/[rsp]=` regex: 2287/2288 hello8 lines and 2222/2223 hello9 lines matched (1 malformed line in EACH log — two `puts()` calls interleaved onto one line, a pre-existing logging-concurrency artifact of this floor's own serial writer, not a probe defect; excluded from all counts/diffs below). hello8's probe window spans log lines 4305-11258 (2287 entries); hello9's spans 4512-11256 (2222 entries).

**Step 2 — the hello8-rip==0 discriminator (decisive)**: `grep -c "rip=0x0000000000000000"` → **hello8: 0 occurrences, across all 2287 parsed entries. hello9: 19 occurrences**, ALL in one unbroken run at the very end of the parsed sequence (indices 2204-2222 of 2222, 0-indexed — i.e. the LAST 19 of 2222 total probe lines), and `rip` never returns nonzero after the first zero reading through the end of this transcript. This directly answers the handoff's own branch test: **branch (b) is REFUTED** (hello8 would also have to show `rip==0` in this window under the "legitimately stale-zero for both boots" theory, and it categorically does not, in a 2287-entry sample) — **branch (c) applies**: the zeroing is hello9-specific.

**Step 3 — locating the transition relative to syscall boundaries**: the entry immediately BEFORE hello9's first zero reading is `svc id=80 rip=0x0000000047661000 rsp=0x00000000488CC000 [rsp]=0x00000000488D0000`, immediately followed on the same log line by `CHALICE C4-60: NtProtectVirtualMemory BaseAddress_ptr=0x4116B558 *BaseAddress=0x47661000 RegionSize_ptr=0x4116B550 *RegionSize=0x1000 NewProtect=0x20` (this floor's own C4-60 unconditional-arg diagnostic, banked, unrelated to this atom) — i.e. the last syscall before the zeroing is the SAME `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call that protects hello9/hello8's own EXE entry page (`0x47661000`, `hello9_x86.exe+0x1000`) executable. The VERY NEXT probe line (the next `svc id=49` dispatch) is hello9's first zero: `rip=0x0 rsp=0x00000000488CBFDC [rsp]=0x000000004766101B`. **`[rsp]` is nonzero and stays the CONSTANT `0x4766101B` across all 19 zero-`rip` entries** — this rules out a "zero `ReturnRIP` restored from a zeroed guest-stack slot" theory (the guest stack at that address is validly mapped and holds a plausible, nonzero, non-degenerate value throughout). Per the handoff's own branch (c) test: **the zeroing happens OUTSIDE syscall dispatch, between two adjacent syscalls — `id=80` (`NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)`) and the immediately following `id=49` dispatch** — time-bracketed to this single syscall-to-syscall window, not resolved to a specific native instruction (that would require disassembling hello9_x86.exe's own guest code around this boundary, out of scope for this atom).

**Step 4 — hello8's OWN behavior at the byte-identical predecessor state, the cleanest data point this probe produced**: hello8's own `id=80`/`NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` probe line reads `rip=0x0000000047661000 rsp=0x00000000488CC000 [rsp]=0x00000000488D0000` — **BYTE-IDENTICAL to hello9's own `id=80` line at the same call**, both logs, confirmed side by side. hello8's VERY NEXT probe line: `svc id=49 rip=0x00000000488D0000 rsp=0x00000000488CC004 [rsp]=0x0000000000000000`. Reading this against the timing model in the probe-design section above: `rsp` INCREASED by exactly 4 (`0x488CC000`→`0x488CC004`, a POP, not a push), and the new `rip` (`0x488D0000`) equals EXACTLY the `[rsp]` value the id=80 line recorded (`0x488D0000`) — a textbook x86 `ret`: pop the return address off the stack into `eip`. hello8 lands cleanly at `0x488D0000` — the WOW64 stub page (the same address the C4-30/C4-58 fault dumps independently name `STUB PAGE addr=0x488D0000`) — walks through several more `svc id=49` (`UNIMPLEMENTED`) probe lines executing inside that stub with stable state, issues a SECOND `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call (`svc id=80`, `*BaseAddress=0x488D0000`, making the stub page itself executable), one `svc id=90` (`UNIMPLEMENTED`, `rsp=0x488CBFF4 [rsp]=0x488D000F` — a further `0x10`-byte push, consistent with the stub's own short prologue), one `svc id=147`, then `svc id=44` with `rip` jumping to `0x0000000058040000` (the same fixed WOW64-context-switch address seen earlier in BOTH transcripts via `NtSetInformationThread ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x58040000`), immediately followed by `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x000000000000002A` — clean exit, `pe10 ret=42`.

**hello9, at the identical fork, diverges completely**: `id=80` line identical to hello8 (above); the NEXT line is hello9's first zero: `rsp` DECREASED by `0x24` (`0x488CC000`→`0x488CBFDC` — 36 bytes of PUSHES, the opposite direction from hello8's pop) and `[rsp]` now holds `0x4766101B` — an address `0x1B` bytes into hello9_x86.exe's OWN entry page (`0x47661000+0x1B`), i.e. a FRESH return address consistent with the guest having just executed a `call` into its own code, not a value carried over from the `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call. `rip` reads `0` instead of any popped target. hello9 NEVER issues the second (`0x488D0000`) `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call — confirmed by a full-transcript `BaseAddress=` diff (241 total `NtProtectVirtualMemory` calls in hello8 vs 252 in hello9 across the WHOLE boot — hello9 makes MORE calls overall, protecting several of its own extra code/import pages, `0x47661000`-`0x47665000` and `0x486B7000`, each protected TWICE, plus `0x4766205C` twice, none present in hello8's sequence, consistent with C4-57's own "image-shape difference" finding — but hello8's OWN final call, `BaseAddress=0x488D0000`, has NO counterpart anywhere in hello9's 252-call sequence). Instead, hello9's `rip` stays `0` for all 19 probe lines through to the already-documented `elr=0x26D0C02B0` self-fault trampoline (C4-58/59/61/62/63's own `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` mechanism — this atom's own capture of that same fault, live, matches those atoms' register/instruction dumps exactly, no new fault site), then `CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, `FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating` — **`pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005`, `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`)**, not a clean exit.

**Step 5 — candidate #8 verdict**: `HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced `ReturnRIP` restore (`Module.cpp:434-464`) is **REFUTED as the corruption SOURCE** — its own input (`[rsp]`) is validly mapped and holds a plausible nonzero value at every single hello9 probe line, including all 19 zero-`rip` ones, so it cannot be reading/propagating a zeroed slot. The zeroing instead correlates exactly with hello9 taking a CALL-shaped native path (pushes, fresh stack value) at the precise fork where hello8 takes a RET-shaped path (pop, stack value carried from the predecessor call) back into the pre-arranged exit stub — a genuine native-control-flow divergence between the two guest images, landing hello9 on code FEX's JIT has evidently not yet compiled a block for. This is directly consistent with C4-63's own Step 2 algebraic finding: a zero `Op->PC`-baked `rip` at the `NoExecOp`/`BreakOp` trampoline (the SAME trampoline this atom's own fault capture confirms hello9 reaches) requires the dispatch-loop's own `ldr RipReg,[STATE,#rip]` read at `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress` (`Dispatcher.cpp:147`) to have ALREADY read `0` from memory before that trampoline runs — this atom's probe shows exactly WHEN that pre-existing zero first appears (immediately after the `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` return, at the call-vs-ret fork above), narrowing C4-63's "one specific load instruction" window from "somewhere in the shared syscall burst" down to this one syscall-to-syscall boundary. **Native dispatch-loop/JIT-cache event, confirmed and now time-bracketed by direct measurement — not a floor-side bug, not patchable from this kernel's own C sources** (same class of verdict as every prior C4-58 through C4-63 atom).

**Step 6 — timing conclusion on `rip(N+1)==[rsp](N)`, per the probe-design's own documented model**: checked programmatically over the FULL parsed sequences (excluding the 1 malformed line each): only **34 of 2286 hello8 pairs** and **35 of 2221 hello9 pairs** (~1.5%) literally satisfy `rip` at entry N+1 equalling `[rsp]` at entry N. This is NOT a refutation of the timing model — the overwhelming majority of consecutive probe lines are EXACT reprints of a stalled/retried `UNIMPLEMENTED` dispatch (identical `id`/`rip`/`rsp`/`[rsp]` repeated many times running — e.g. `svc id=49 rip=0x47661000 rsp=0x488CC000 [rsp]=0x488D0000` appears 14 times consecutively in BOTH logs, immediately before the `id=80` transition above), where the equality test is vacuous (state has not genuinely advanced between prints). At the ONE boundary in this entire window where the underlying dispatch state DOES genuinely advance — the `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` return analyzed in Steps 3-4 above — the rule holds EXACTLY in hello8 (`rip(N+1)=0x488D0000=[rsp](N)`) and is precisely where it visibly BREAKS in hello9 (`rip(N+1)=0`, despite `[rsp](N)` holding the SAME `0x488D0000` hello8's own identical predecessor call recorded). **Timing conclusion the data supports**: the documented restore-timing model (probe shows dispatch N-1's committed `rip` plus dispatch N's not-yet-consumed `[rsp]`) holds at every point in these transcripts where it is actually testable (i.e. where consecutive prints reflect genuinely different dispatch state) — and hello9's own restore, at this one specific dispatch, is the SINGLE point across both ~2200-line transcripts where a value the model predicts (and hello8's own byte-identical precedent confirms) fails to appear. This is the most surgical single data point the probe produced, and it pins the divergence to one syscall-to-syscall transition rather than a diffuse or gradual drift.

**Step 7 — shared tail window diff**: the full-transcript `NtProtectVirtualMemory` `BaseAddress=` sequence is IDENTICAL between hello8 and hello9 for the last 5 calls immediately preceding divergence — `...0x5D0BA000, 0x5D0C0A68, 0x5D0C0A68, 0x47661000` — then hello8 has ONE more (`0x488D0000`) that hello9 never reaches. The C4-59-named "18x id=49/QPC burst + NtClose + NtTestAlert + NtFlushInstructionCache + NtProtectVirtualMemory" shape is confirmed present and byte-identical in TYPE/COUNT/POSITION in both logs' `CHALICE C4-64` probe lines up to and including the `id=80` line itself (both logs show the same repeating `id=49`/`id=147` pattern with stable `rip=0x47661000 rsp=0x488CC000 [rsp]=0x488D0000` immediately before it) — `NtClose` appears twice, identically positioned, in each transcript (both far earlier in the boot, lines 87/159 hello8 vs 108/181 hello9, a constant +21/+22-line offset from hello9's own extra early syscalls, unrelated to this atom's divergence); `NtTestAlert`/`NtFlushInstructionCache` are never named explicitly in either transcript (both are `UNIMPLEMENTED` numeric-`id`-only dispatches in this floor's own logging, not resolved to a name string by the existing code) — the numeric-`id` burst itself (repeating `id=49`) is what C4-59 characterized by that name, and it matches exactly, both logs, through the fork.

**Convention sweep**: build-artifact/PE-timestamp churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before this commit, confirmed via `git status --short` (no tracked-file diffs remain); `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked, same convention every prior hello9 atom used.

**Commits**: banked, verified not redone — `5b3b9af` (diagnostic), `3d341db` (temp hello9 swap), `274e483` (revert to canonical hello8). This documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-64) is the final commit, source-untouched. Receipts: `scratch/c4-64_pe10_fex_canonical_{prefix,final}_receipt{,_full}.log` (canonical baseline + post-revert reconfirm, `ExitStatus=0x2A`/`pe10 ret=42` confirmed), `scratch/c4-64_hello9_boot_receipt{,_full}.log` (the 19-line zeroing window + fault + crash, `pe10 ret=3221225477`), `scratch/c4-64_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log` (all three GATE PASS, `pe9`/`ramfb` freshly rerun clean this atom, no flakes). **Next atom**: disassemble hello9_x86.exe's own guest code at `0x47661000` onward (the entry page, now confirmed executable at this fork) to identify the actual x86 instruction(s) — call target, import thunk, or relocation-fixup branch — hello9 executes instead of hello8's plain `ret`, and determine why FEX's JIT has not compiled a block there (first-touch cache miss vs. a genuinely different guest code path from hello9's own import table, C4-57). Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-63 — SOURCE-READING ONLY: exhaustive vendored-tree grep for every explicit `State.rip`-writing site finds no literal-zero write anywhere; the fatal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` trampoline's own RIP-store (`OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:4978`, `BreakOp`/`NoExecOp`) is proven algebraically to be a pure reporting path, narrowing (not resolving) C4-58/59's "zeroing event" to one specific load instruction. One new, unconfirmed candidate named (`Module.cpp:464`, `HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced RIP restore). No fix applied, no code changed, no build/boot.

**Task**: from FEXCore's own vendored source (`./FEX`, already present in this repo, cross-referenced against this file's own prior citations — C4-33/39 already cite `Dispatcher.cpp` and `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`), find every site that writes a literal/default zero into `CpuStateFrame::State.rip` (or FEX's equivalent guest-PC-tracking field) during normal (non-crash) operation, and determine whether any such site's own triggering condition plausibly correlates with hello8-vs-hello9's image-shape difference (LookupCache/BlockCache-miss path, cache size, IR::Break reason code — C4-59's own already-named leads).

**Step 1 — exhaustive grep, every candidate pattern the task named** (`rip\s*=\s*0`, `rip\s*=\s*{}`, `->rip\s*=`, `.State.rip`/`->State.rip`, `SetRIP`/`InvalidRIP`, plus every `reset`/`invalidate`/`clear`-named function touching `CpuStateFrame`/`InternalThreadState`/`ArchState`): **zero hits for a literal `rip = 0`/`rip = {}` anywhere in the tree** (`./FEX`, both `FEXCore/` and `Source/Windows/`). Every genuine `CPUState::rip` write site, file:line + one-line trigger condition:
1. `FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h:217` — `rip = ~0ULL;` inside `CPUState()`'s constructor, `#ifndef NDEBUG` only — debug-build default-init sentinel (deliberately ALL-ONES, not zero, to catch spurious pre-init reads); fires once per NEW `CPUState` object (`ContextImpl::CreateThread`), never mid-execution.
2. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp:412` — `Thread->CurrentFrame->State.rip = InitialRIP;`, `ContextImpl::CreateThread` — one-time thread-creation seed (this floor's own boot-seeded `exe_entry`, always nonzero).
3. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/JIT.cpp:539` — `Frame->State.rip = GuestRip;`, `Arm64JITCore::ExitFunctionLink`, gated on `TFSet` (trap-flag/single-step mode only) — `GuestRip=Record->GuestRIP`, a compile-time-baked block-link target; TF is never set in this boot (no single-stepping engaged, per C4-59's own transcript reading).
4. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:501` — `str x1,[STATE,#rip]`, inside the `CallbackPtr` reentrant thunk-callback trampoline — writes whatever RIP the C++ thunk-invoking caller passes as its 2nd argument; hello8/hello9's own trivial `WriteFile`/`GetStdHandle`/syscall-only flow never invokes a thunked callback (no `Thunks.cpp` activity in either transcript), path never entered.
5. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:115` — `str EC_CALL_CHECKER_PC_REG,[STATE,#rip]`, `#ifdef ARCHITECTURE_arm64ec` only — this floor targets 32-bit x86 guests under `libwow64fex.dll` (WOW64), not x64-under-ARM64EC; not the active code path for this floor's blob.
6. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:4978` (`OpDispatchBuilder::BreakOp`, called by `NoExecOp`/`InvalidOp`/`PermissionRestrictedOp`/`UnimplementedOp`) — `_StoreContextGPR(GPRSize, GetRelocatedPC(Op, -Op->InstSize), offsetof(CPUState, rip))` — compiles, via `_GetRelocatedPC` (`OpcodeDispatcher.h:1564`: `Offs = Op->PC + Op->InstSize + Offset - Entry`) and `DEF_OP(EntrypointOffset)` (`JIT/ALUOps.cpp:57-60`: `Constant = Entry + Op->Offset`) → `InsertGuestRIPMove` (`JIT/Arm64Relocations.cpp:90`: an inline per-block `LoadConstant` immediate-load, NOT a runtime lookup) — this is exactly, and only, the trampoline C4-61/62 already disassembled at `elr=0x26D0C02B0`. See Step 2.
7. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/BranchOps.cpp:154,190` (guest branch/call/jump `ExitFunction`-class ops) — `str RipReg/TMP1,[STATE,#rip]` — writes State.rip to the GUEST-COMPUTED branch target (compile-time-inline constant for direct branches, or a genuine runtime register value for indirect ones, e.g. `jmp eax`) — the ONE mechanism that can legitimately write a truly computed, runtime zero under normal operation, but strictly requires the guest CPU to have EXECUTED a branch instruction first; C4-58's own live guest-register readout at this exact fault already proved every register (including all GPRs) was still at its pristine boot-seed default — ruled out for this specific fault.
8. `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:464` (`WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`) — `Frame->State.rip = ReturnRIP;`, where `ReturnRIP = *(uint32_t*)(Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP])` — a plain native C++ pointer dereference reading 4 bytes directly off the GUEST'S OWN STACK at whatever address ESP currently holds, executed on EVERY real Nt* syscall FEX dispatches through its own `BridgeInstrs::Syscall`/`UnixCall` bridge (confirmed live-relevant: the function it calls, `Wow64SystemServiceEx`, is an external wine/`ntdll`-donor symbol — declared `Source/Windows/include/winternl.h:562`, not implemented in this vendored tree — that performs the real ARM64 `svc` trap this floor's own `kmain.c`/`ntsvc.c` intercept and log by `svc id=NN`, i.e. every syscall in C4-59's own "18x QPC burst" window passes through this exact function). Not a literal `rip=0` in source, not `grep`-discoverable — the value is a RUNTIME memory read, depends entirely on the guest's own stack contents at the moment of dispatch.

**Step 2 — is site #6 (`NoExecOp`/`BreakOp`, the `elr=0x26D0C02B0` trampoline's own feeder) an independent zeroing site, or a reporting path?** Algebraic trace, not inference: `_GetRelocatedPC`'s `Offs = Op->PC + Op->InstSize + (-Op->InstSize) - Entry(frontend) = Op->PC - Entry(frontend)`; `DEF_OP(EntrypointOffset)`'s `Constant = Entry(JIT backend) + Offs`. Both `Entry`s are set, once per compile, from the SAME `GuestRIP` parameter threaded through `ContextImpl::CompileBlock`→`GenerateIR`→`OpDispatchBuilder::BeginFunction` (`Entry=RIP`, `OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:3979`) on the frontend side, and `ContextImpl::CompileCode`→`CPUBackend::CompileCode`→`this->Entry=Entry` (`JIT.cpp:824`) on the backend side — no code path sets them independently, both trace to the same `GuestRIP` argument of the SAME `CompileBlock` call. Substituting: `Constant = Entry(backend) + Op->PC - Entry(frontend) = Op->PC` (the two `Entry`s cancel exactly, REGARDLESS of what `GuestRIP` actually is). **This proves `BreakOp`'s own RIP-store always bakes in `Op->PC` — the literal guest address of the instruction the frontend decoder determined was invalid/unmapped/no-exec (`GenerateIR`'s own `if (!BlockInstructionsLength) { ... NoExecOp(...); }` gate, `Core.cpp` ~line 685-699, fires only on the BLOCK'S OWN FIRST instruction) — never an independently-computed or defaulted zero.**

For this constant to compile to exactly 0, the very FIRST instruction of the block being compiled (`Op->PC == Entry == GuestRIP`) must itself be at guest address `0x0` — meaning the dispatch loop's OWN block-cache-miss path (`Dispatcher.cpp`'s `NoBlock` label, fed by `ldr RipReg,[STATE,#rip]` at `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress`, `Dispatcher.cpp:147`, immediately followed by `CompileBlock(CTX,Frame,RipReg,0)` on any L1/L2/L3 cache miss — `Dispatcher.cpp` lines ~144-266) must have already read `State.rip==0` from memory BEFORE this NoExecOp/BreakOp/trampoline sequence ever starts. **This narrows the C4-59 "zeroing event" window from "somewhere inside FEXCore's native dispatch-loop code" down to one specific point: the value must already be 0 in memory strictly BEFORE the single `ldr RipReg,[STATE,#rip]` load at `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress` for this one dispatch-loop iteration.** Everything downstream of that load — the L1/L2/L3 cache lookup, the `NoBlock` fallback, `CompileBlock`, the frontend decoder's own `BAD_RELOCATION`/`INVALID_INST` classification at guest address 0 (unmapped, so naturally undecodable), `NoExecOp`, `BreakOp`, `IR::Break`'s own `SynchronousFaultData` write (already fully traced by C4-62), and the fixed `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` trampoline itself (`mov w1,#0;ldr x1,[x1]`, C4-61/62) — is proven, by this atom's own algebraic source trace, to behave correctly and merely relay/report whatever `State.rip` already held. None of it independently invents a zero.

**Step 3 — candidate #8 (`HandleSyscallImpl`), plausibility for hello8-vs-hello9, genuinely unconfirmed by source reading alone**: `HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced `ReturnRIP` write is exercised on every syscall in the exact window C4-59 already measured as byte-identical in TYPE/COUNT/POSITION between hello8 and hello9 (the 18x `NtQueryPerformanceCounter`(id=49) + `NtClose`(id=15) + `NtTestAlert`(id=248) + `NtFlushInstructionCache`(id=147) + `NtProtectVirtualMemory`(id=80) burst immediately before the divergence point, C4-59's own Step 3 table) — but its OUTPUT depends on the guest's OWN ESP register (`Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP]`) and the 4 bytes sitting at that stack address at each call, neither of which this atom's static source read can inspect. No source-level condition gates this differently for hello8 vs hello9 — any difference would have to come from the guest's actual runtime stack layout, which could plausibly differ for a larger/import-bearing image per hello9's own already-documented image-shape differences (C4-57's own "real `.idata`/IAT section, larger image, `-lkernel32` link surface" framing). This is NOT ruled in or out by source reading alone — it is the single most concrete, NEW "next place to instrument" this atom can name: a live probe dumping `Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP]` and the 4 bytes at that address on every `HandleSyscallImpl`/`BridgeInstrs::Syscall`-class dispatch in the shared-tail window, for both hello8 and hello9, diffed — extendable from the SAME `nt_wow64_live_state_rip`-class instrumentation C4-59 already built and left in place.

**Step 4 — verdict, per this atom's own instructions**: no site anywhere in the vendored FEXCore/Windows-WOW64 source explicitly writes a literal-zero `State.rip` under any condition reachable in this boot's own flow. The fault-shape's own mechanism (`NoExecOp`/`BreakOp`/`GuestSignal_SIGSEGV`, already identified by C4-61/62) is now PROVEN (algebraic source trace, not inference) to be a pure downstream reporting path, not the zeroing site itself — a genuine, useful narrowing result, not a repeat of C4-59's own runtime-measured conclusion (it confirms the SAME conclusion from the opposite, source-level angle, and additionally narrows the "where" down to one specific load instruction, `Dispatcher.cpp:147`). One plausible but unconfirmed candidate (`HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced RIP restore, `Module.cpp:464`) is named for a future live-tracing atom, NOT chased further this atom (would require a live boot/instrumentation, out of this atom's own source-reading-only budget). Both remaining candidates live entirely inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob (`FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/{Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp,JIT/*.cpp,OpcodeDispatcher.cpp}`, `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`) — none are reachable or patchable from this floor's own `kernel-arm/zoneA/*.c` sources: this floor's own C code supplies neither the JIT's internal `Entry`/`GuestRIP` bookkeeping (candidate #6) nor the guest's own ESP/stack contents at the exact moment `HandleSyscallImpl` fires natively, before this floor's own `svc` trap handler ever runs (candidate #8). **Not floor-satisfiable — same verdict as C4-58/59, now confirmed via a full source-level trace rather than pure runtime measurement alone. No fix applied, no code changed.**

**Bonus, resolves a residual C4-59 uncertainty**: C4-59's own hand-derived `State.rip` offset (`0x18` within `CpuStateFrame.State`) was flagged as NOT independently compile-checked (blocked by a missing `<malloc.h>`/uncommitted `fmt` submodule on the build host). This atom directly re-read `FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h`'s own field list/`static_assert`s (`InlineJITBlockHeader`@0, `DeferredSignalRefCount`@8 (`NonAtomicRefCounter<uint64_t>`, 8-byte aligned per its own `static_assert`), `pf_raw`@16 (u32), `af_raw`@20 (u32, per `static_assert((offsetof(pf_raw)+4)==offsetof(af_raw))`), `rip`@24=`0x18` after 8-byte re-alignment) and confirms the offset by direct structural reading of the real header (not a compile, but a full field-order read cross-checked against the header's own `static_assert`s, not a guess) — `0x18` is correct. Does not change any prior atom's own verdict, but removes a previously-open residual doubt about the diagnostic's own correctness.

**No fix applied, per this atom's own explicit law**: source-reading only, no `kernel-arm/zoneA` C source touched, no build (`GAIA_NO_RUN=1 ./build.sh` not run), no boot (`pe10_probe.py` not run) — nothing to gate-check, nothing to regress.
