## C4-62 — `elr=0x26D0C02B0` identified as FEXCore's own `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` deliberate self-fault trampoline (real vendored source match, not inference); its consumer is gated on `SynchronousFaultData`, not on this floor's raw ARM64 fault classification, and this floor's existing classification for the fault shape already matches every prior working `NoExecOp` delivery. No fix applied.

**Task**: test whether `elr=0x26D0C02B0` (`mov w1,#0; ldr x1,[x1]`, dfsc=0x06 translation fault, far=0x0, C4-61) is a deliberately-designed FEX-internal trap that a FEX-side handler is supposed to catch, PROVIDED it receives the correct NT status code from this floor — the same bug-class shape as C4-38/39/40's alignment fix (where `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl`'s `HandleUnalignedAccess` gate genuinely required a status code, `STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`, this floor originally never produced).

**Step 1 — wider disassembly, `elr-128..elr-32` (24 more 32-bit words, none previously captured)**: extended the existing `elr==0x26D0C02B0`-gated diagnostic (`kmain.c`, `sync_try_guest_exception`, C4-61's own addition) with a second dump covering the 96 bytes immediately before C4-61's own `elr-32..elr+64` window, +18 lines. Built (`GAIA_NO_RUN=1 ./build.sh`, `timeout 180`), checkpoint-committed (`01a14c1`), canonical `pe10 fex` re-run FIRST confirming the new print stays silent and `ret=42` is unaffected (`scratch/c4-62_pe10_fex_canonical_prefix_receipt.log`). Temp hello9 swap re-applied (exact C4-55..61 method, `shell.c`/`build.sh` two-line diff, `mkpe32.sh` rebuild), checkpoint-committed (`c2a06c3`), booted (`timeout 240 python3 scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, receipt `scratch/c4-62_hello9_boot_receipt.log`).

Live capture at the terminal fault, combined with C4-61's own already-captured `elr-32..elr+64` window and disassembled with `aarch64-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64` (full 192-byte window, `elr-128..elr+64`):
```
   elr-128: stp x5, x6, [x28, #48]
   elr-124: stp x8, x9, [x28, #64]
   elr-120: stp x10, x11, [x28, #80]
   elr-116: stp w26, w27, [x28, #16]
   elr-112: add x0, x28, #0x1c0
   elr-108: st1 {v16.2d-v19.2d}, [x0], #64
   elr-104: st1 {v20.2d-v23.2d}, [x0], #64
   elr-100: hlt #0x0
   elr-96:  mrs x0, nzcv
   elr-92:  str w0, [x28, #1032]
   elr-88:  str x25, [x28, #176]
   elr-84:  stp x4, x7, [x28, #32]
   elr-80:  stp x5, x6, [x28, #48]
   elr-76:  stp x8, x9, [x28, #64]
   elr-72:  stp x10, x11, [x28, #80]
   elr-68:  stp w26, w27, [x28, #16]
   elr-64:  add x0, x28, #0x1c0
   elr-60:  st1 {v16.2d-v19.2d}, [x0], #64
   elr-56:  st1 {v20.2d-v23.2d}, [x0], #64
   elr-52:  brk #0x0
   elr-48:  mrs x0, nzcv
   elr-44:  str w0, [x28, #1032]
   elr-40:  str x25, [x28, #176]
   elr-36:  stp x4, x7, [x28, #32]
   elr-32..elr+28: (C4-61's own already-disassembled window: SpillStaticRegs sequence, `mov w1,#0`, `ldr x1,[x1]` <FAULT>, then another SpillStaticRegs sequence starting)
```
**Conditional-branch finding, explicit**: the full 40-instruction, 192-byte window (`elr-128..elr+64`) contains ZERO `cbz`/`cbnz`/`b.cond`/`tbz`/`tbnz`/`b`/`bl`/`br` instructions of any kind — every word decodes to a register spill (`stp`/`st1`), a fixed terminal instruction (`hlt #0`, `brk #0`, or the `mov w1,#0`+`ldr x1,[x1]` pair), or `mrs`/`str` instructions belonging to the START of the next block in memory. This rules out the "reached via a conditional branch from earlier code" half of the task's own question: there is no runtime skip/condition anywhere in this local window. The three trap instructions (`hlt`, `brk`, the null-deref) are each the FIXED, unconditional TAIL of their own respective code block, and each block is entered from FAR AWAY via a separate indirect branch (see Step 2), not via straight-line fallthrough from guest code or a local conditional check.

**Step 2 — FEX source search, real match found**: searched the vendored tree (`./FEX`, confirmed present at repo root, `FEX/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp`). Lines 394-430 emit, in this exact order (confirmed against the LIVE disassembly above, byte-for-byte, not merely by name):
```cpp
{
  // Guest SIGILL handler
  GuestSignal_SIGILL = GetCursorAddress<uint64_t>();
  SpillStaticRegs(TMP1);
  hlt(0);
}
{
  // Guest SIGTRAP handler
  GuestSignal_SIGTRAP = GetCursorAddress<uint64_t>();
  SpillStaticRegs(TMP1);
  brk(0);
}
{
  // Guest Overflow handler
  GuestSignal_SIGSEGV = GetCursorAddress<uint64_t>();
  SpillStaticRegs(TMP1);
  // hlt/udf = SIGILL ; brk = SIGTRAP ; ??? = SIGSEGV
  // Force a SIGSEGV by loading zero
  if (CTX->ExitOnHLTEnabled()) { /* ret path, not taken on this floor */ }
  else {
    LoadConstant(ARMEmitter::Size::i64Bit, ARMEmitter::Reg::r1, 0);
    ldr(ARMEmitter::XReg::x1, ARMEmitter::Reg::r1);
  }
}
```
`LoadConstant(x1,0)` compiles to `mov w1,#0x0` (32-bit write, zero-extends `x1`); `ldr(x1,x1)` compiles to `ldr x1,[x1]` — an exact, instruction-for-instruction match to the fault Step 1 disassembled, including the identical `SpillStaticRegs`-shaped prologue and the identical adjacency to the `hlt`/`brk` siblings, in the identical order. This IS a deliberate fault-as-signal idiom, confirmed against real source, matching the task's own premise precisely.

`GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` is entered ONLY via `IR::Break(FAULT_SIGSEGV)` (`FEX/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MiscOps.cpp`, `DEF_OP(Break)`, already partially cited in this file's own C4-33 entry): `IR::Break` FIRST writes `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` (`FaultToTopAndGeneratedException=1`, plus `Signal`/`TrapNo`/`si_code`/`err_code` taken from the IR op's own `Reason`, a JIT-compile-time constant chosen by the x86 frontend's own emitters — `NoExecOp`/`PermissionRestrictedOp`/`INT n`/`INTO`/`HLT`, per `OpcodeDispatcher.cpp`, already cited by this file's own C4-33 entry) THEN does `ldr(TMP1, STATE, offsetof(CpuStateFrame, Pointers.GuestSignal_SIGSEGV)); br(TMP1);`. The decision to reach this trampoline is made EARLIER, at JIT-compile time, by which `IR::Break` variant the frontend emitted for the ORIGINAL guest instruction — not by any runtime condition inside the trampoline arena itself (consistent with Step 1's own finding of zero local conditional branches).

**The consumer, found**: `FEX::Windows::HandleGuestException` (`FEX/Source/Windows/Common/Exception.h:12-79`) is the real, present-in-source handler this task's step 2 asked for — it reads `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` and reconstructs the guest-visible `EXCEPTION_RECORD` from `Fault.Signal`/`Fault.TrapNo`/`Fault.err_code`, entirely independent of whatever native `EXCEPTION_RECORD`/status code this floor supplies as its own `Src` parameter beyond having reached this consume path at all. For `FAULT_SIGSEGV`+`TrapNo==X86_TRAPNO_PF` (the exact `NoExecOp` signature this fault carries, confirmed below): `Dst.NumberParameters=2; Dst.ExceptionInformation[0]=EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_FAULT; Dst.ExceptionInformation[1]=Rip;` and, UNCONDITIONALLY before the switch, `Dst.ExceptionAddress=reinterpret_cast<void*>(Rip);` — `Rip` here is FEX's OWN internally-tracked guest x86 program counter (`State.rip`), not a field this floor's classifier computes or supplies. Unlike `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl`'s `EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` gate (C4-39, which DID require a specific incoming status code this floor originally never produced), `HandleGuestException` has NO gate on the incoming status code's specific value — it is unconditionally invoked for this whole class of self-generated faults and derives everything it needs from `SynchronousFaultData` alone.

**Cross-check against this floor's own already-successful deliveries, live re-read, no rebuild needed**: this file's own C4-30/31/33 entries already establish that every PRIOR `NoExecOp` self-fault on this floor (`exe_entry`, `kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, C4-27's stub page) carried the identical `SynchronousFaultData` fingerprint — `Signal=SIGSEGV(0xB) TrapNo=PF(0xE) si_code=2 err_code=0x15(PROT|USER|INSTR)` — and was delivered correctly end-to-end under this floor's SAME, unconditional `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`/`params=2` classification (`kmain.c`'s `sync_try_guest_exception`, the generic `ec==0x24||0x25` Data-Abort branch — no `NoExecOp`-specific branch has ever existed there, and none was ever needed). Re-read the ALREADY-ON-DISK `scratch/c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`'s own `SynchronousFaultData` print at the terminal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` fault (the SAME fault this atom targets, no new boot needed for this specific check): `raw=0x15020E0B01 FaultToTopAndGeneratedException=1 Signal=0xB TrapNo=0xE si_code=2 err_code=0x15` — BYTE-IDENTICAL to the canonical `NoExecOp` fingerprint. This fault is not a new, unhandled signal class requiring a new classification branch; it is the SAME class this floor's classifier already produces the correct status code for.

**Step 3/4 verdict**: the task's own condition 3 ("a handler that expects THIS kind of fault ... PROVIDED it receives the right NT exception status code from us") is satisfied on the source-match half but not actionable on the "provided the right status code" half, because this floor's status code for this exact fault shape is ALREADY correct — proven by identical prior successful deliveries under the same classification, not merely assumed. There is no narrower `dfsc==0x06 AND far==0 AND elr-in-arena` branch to add: the existing unconditional `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` branch for non-alignment Data Aborts is not wrong for this fault, and a narrower branch would produce the identical `status`/`params` value this floor already produces — changing nothing observable, since `HandleGuestException`'s `ExceptionAddress`/`ExceptionInformation[1]` are sourced from `Rip` (FEX's own `State.rip`), never from this floor's `status` value. Per the task's own condition-4 spirit (no actionable gap that would change behavior): **no fix applied**.

**`addr=0x0`, explained without reopening C4-58/59**: `HandleGuestException`'s `Rip`-sourced `ExceptionAddress`/`ExceptionInformation[1]` explain the observed `addr=0x0` completely — `Rip`=`State.rip`, which C4-58/59's own live measurement already proved is EXACTLY `0x0` at this precise fault ("the zeroing event", the first and only zero `State.rip` value either boot ever produces, occurring strictly inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll`'s own native dispatch-loop bookkeeping, between the last successful `NtContinue` resume and this fault, with zero syscall/exception boundary crossing it visible to this floor, per C4-59's own tightest-possible measurement window). No status-code choice in `sync_try_guest_exception` can change `State.rip`'s value — this task's own theory (a misclassification masking a would-be self-heal or a differently-shaped delivery) does not hold for the observed symptom either: a "perfectly" classified record would still report `addr=0x0`, for the same upstream reason C4-58/59 already named and time-boxed as blob-internal.

**hello9 boot outcome, re-tested**: receipt `scratch/c4-62_hello9_boot_receipt.log`. UNCHANGED from every prior atom (C4-56/57/58/59/61): `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, `FirstChance=FALSE`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`; final `CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX` dump shows `Eip=0x47661000 (hello9_x86.exe+0x1000)`, byte-identical to the boot-seeded `exe_entry` value — the guest CPU never advanced. Grepped the full boot transcript for `hello9_x86.c`'s own milestone string (`"hello9: the door opens"`) and for `WriteFile`/`GetStdHandle`: zero occurrences — the milestone is NOT reached, matching C4-58/59's own already-established verdict (guest Eip never leaves `exe_entry`) exactly.

**Revert + canonical reconfirm**: hello9 swap (`shell.c`/`build.sh`) reverted to the exact pre-swap commit (`git checkout 82eec0a -- shell.c build.sh`), confirmed net-zero via `git diff 82eec0a`; `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` removed (untracked, rebuildable via `kernel-arm/chalice/mkpe32.sh`); build-artifact/PE-timestamp churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit this atom. ## C4-61 — NAMED: the null register at the fatal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` fault (hello9's dead-end crash, C4-57/58/59) is `x1`, zeroed by a hardcoded immediate two instructions before the fault, not by any structure-field load this project has documented offsets for.

**Task**: name what pointer is null at the fault, without re-chasing the already-time-boxed "why does hello9 reach this fault at all" question (C4-58/59's own confirmed-blob-internal verdict, unchanged).

**Step 1 — existing diagnostics checked first**: grepped `kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c` for the fault-delivery code path (`CHALICE ARENA`, `SynchronousFaultData`, `esr=`, `elr=`). The function that actually delivers this specific class of fault as a synthesized guest exception is `sync_try_guest_exception` (NOT `sync_handler`, which is a separate, only-used-when-nothing-else-applies raw hardware-fault dump with its own full x0-x30 print — confirmed this function never runs for this fault, since the transcript always ends in a clean `NtRaiseException`/second-chance-terminate sequence, the guest-exception path's own signature, not a bare kernel abort). `sync_try_guest_exception` already snapshots the FULL SAVEALL_SYNC register frame into a C-local array (`regs_snap`/`regs_safe[0..30]`, existing C4-21/22 infrastructure — snapshotted specifically to survive a later zero-fill of the original frame, per that code's own comment) but only ever PRINTS two of those 31 registers at the point this fault is handled: `resolve_line("lr ", regs_safe[30])` and `resolve_line("x0 ", regs_safe[0])`. No full x0-x30 dump existed at this delivery point before this atom.

Grepped every existing scratch log matching `c4-5[6-9]_*.log`/`c4-60_*.log` for the literal string `elr=0x26D0C02B0` (cheaper than a new boot, per the task's own instruction): four already contain it — `c4-56_hello9_swap_reattempt_receipt.log`, `c4-57_hello9_swap_binder_receipt.log`, `c4-59_hello9_swap_receipt_full.log`, `c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`. All four already carry the pre-existing `CHALICE C4-30 ELR WINDOW @elr-32..+32` print — 16 raw ARM64 32-bit words (64 bytes total), elr itself at window index 8 — i.e. the actual faulting instruction's own bytes, and its immediate neighbors, were ALREADY on disk before this atom touched anything. None of the four carry a full x0-x30 register dump at this fault.

**Step 2 — static vs dynamic, answered by an existing print, no new work needed**: the same transcripts already carry, unconditionally, `CHALICE REGION elr=0x26D0C02B0 -> reserve-commit-arena committed base=0x26D0C0000 size=0x4000` (`nt_fault_region`, a pre-existing classifier function used throughout this file, e.g. C4-30/31's own dumps). This says the address is DYNAMIC — inside a bump-allocated arena, size `0x4000` (16KB), NOT the static vendored `libwow64fex.dll` image file (whose ranges this project resolves separately, via `map-fex-offsets.sh` or direct `objdump -d` on the on-disk blob, a technique used for a completely different address family — the fixed native-code addresses named in C4-46/48/50/51/52/53). `kmain.c`'s own existing source comment at this exact print site (predating this atom) already characterizes this specific `0x4000`-byte arena class as "FEX's own dispatcher trampoline class, per C4-17/18/21's dump of the SAME class of ... arena" — i.e. this determination was already made and documented by earlier atoms; this atom did not need to re-derive it, only confirm the SAME classifier fires for THIS exact address (it does, in all four pre-existing logs and this atom's own fresh one). Consequence for HOW to read the instruction bytes: read from the live memory window this floor's own `nt_fault_read32` already captures (dynamic/JIT path), not from the on-disk blob file (static path would be wrong here).

**Step 3 — diagnostic added, narrow, one-address-only, measurement-only** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c`, `sync_try_guest_exception`, +27 lines, right after `regs_safe` is constructed): a full `x0`-`x30` GPR dump (reusing the already-captured `regs_safe[]` array, no new register-capture plumbing needed) plus a 64-byte instruction hexdump starting at `elr` (`elr..elr+64`, 16 words), both gated strictly to `elr == 0x26D0C02B0ULL` — this specific fixed address only, never firing on the ~20 unrelated shared self-heal cycles (20 DIFFERENT `elr` values, `dfsc=0x21` alignment class, C4-59's own table) or on the canonical hello8 boot (which never reaches this address at all, confirmed both by C4-58/59's own prior receipts and this atom's own fresh canonical re-run below).

**Register receipt, live** (`scratch/c4-61_hello9_boot_receipt_full.log`, around the 5th of 5 occurrences of `elr=0x26D0C02B0` in this transcript — the terminal, fatal one, matching the prior atoms' own count):
```
CHALICE C4-61 FULL REGS @elr=0x26D0C02B0:
  x0=0x0000000270001380 x1=0x0000000000000000 x2=0x00000002300009B0 x3=0x0000000000000000
  x4=0x0000000000000000 x5=0x0000000000000000 x6=0x0000000000000000 x7=0x0000000000000000
  x8=0x00000000488CBFDC x9=0x00000000488CBFFC x10=0x0000000000000000 x11=0x0000000000000000
  x12=0x0000000000000100 x13=0x0000000000000000 x14=0x0000000000000000 x15=0x00000002300106E0
  x16=0x0000000047088324 x17=0x000000004116B470 x18=0x000000004116C120 x19=0x000000004116B6E0
  x20=0x0000000000000000 x21=0x000000004766101B x22=0x0000000000000001 x23=0x000000005A0610B8
  x24=0x000000004116BBC0 x25=0x0000000299980FF0 x26=0x00000000488CBFE0 x27=0x00000000B7734007

CHALICE C4-61 ELR BYTES @elr..+64: 0x00000000F9400021 0x00000000D53B4200 0x00000000B9040B80 0x00000000F9005B99 0x00000000A9021F84 0x00000000A9031B85 0x00000000A9042788 0x00000000A9052F8A 0x0000000029026F9A 0x0000000091070380 0x000000004C9F2C10 0x000000004C9F2C14 0x000000005800BF00 0x00000000AA1C03E1 0x000000005800BF02 0x00000000D63F0040
```

`x1=0x0`, live-measured, EXACTLY matching this same fault's own `far=0x0` (the hardware-reported faulting data address) — `x1` is the register holding the address that gets dereferenced. `x28=0x270001140` matches this SAME transcript's own `CurrentFrame=0x270001140` (the pre-existing TLS PROBE print, C4-22/31 infrastructure, same boot) — confirms `x28` is FEXCore's own reserved state-frame-pointer register at this exact fault, consistent with (not merely assumed from) this file's own already-documented `CpuStateFrame`/`InternalThreadState::CurrentFrame` citations.

**Instruction-level disassembly**: the 16-word/64-byte `ELR WINDOW` (elr-32..+32) from the pre-existing `c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log` is byte-identical to this atom's own fresh `ELR BYTES` capture at the overlapping offsets (same fixed host address, same code, confirmed across two independent boots/atoms) — cross-validates that this is a fixed, non-relocating piece of code, not a per-boot JIT artifact that happens to reuse the same value coincidentally. Wrote the 16 words to a raw binary file (`struct.pack('<I', word)` per word, little-endian, matching ARM64 in-memory instruction encoding) and disassembled with `aarch64-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64` (same tool CLASS — a real AArch64 disassembler — prior atoms used for the vendored blob, applied here to a raw memory-window capture instead of the on-disk file, per Step 2's own static/dynamic determination):
```
   0:	a9031b85 	stp	x5, x6, [x28, #48]
   4:	a9042788 	stp	x8, x9, [x28, #64]
   8:	a9052f8a 	stp	x10, x11, [x28, #80]
   c:	29026f9a 	stp	w26, w27, [x28, #16]
  10:	91070380 	add	x0, x28, #0x1c0
  14:	4c9f2c10 	st1	{v16.2d-v19.2d}, [x0], #64
  18:	4c9f2c14 	st1	{v20.2d-v23.2d}, [x0], #64
  1c:	52800001 	mov	w1, #0x0                   // #0
  20:	f9400021 	ldr	x1, [x1]                    <-- elr, the fault
  24:	d53b4200 	mrs	x0, nzcv
  28:	b9040b80 	str	w0, [x28, #1032]
  2c:	f9005b99 	str	x25, [x28, #176]
  30:	a9021f84 	stp	x4, x7, [x28, #32]
  34:	a9031b85 	stp	x5, x6, [x28, #48]
  38:	a9042788 	stp	x8, x9, [x28, #64]
  3c:	a9052f8a 	stp	x10, x11, [x28, #80]
```
Hand bit-field extraction of the fault instruction's own encoding (`0xF9400021`, standard `LDR (immediate, unsigned offset)`, 64-bit variant, base opcode `0xF9400000`): `Rt = value & 0x1F = 1`, `Rn = (value>>5) & 0x1F = 1`, `imm12 = (value>>10) & 0xFFF = 0` — `ldr x1, [x1, #0]`, matching the disassembler's own output exactly, cross-checked, not a single-source read.

**Step 4 — origin of the null value**: the instruction immediately before the fault (offset `0x1c` in the window, `elr-4`) is `mov w1, #0x0` — a `MOVZ` immediate-zero write to `w1`, which per the AArch64 ABI's own zero-extend-on-32-bit-write rule also zeroes the upper 32 bits of `x1`. There is NO intervening memory load and no base-register-plus-offset dereference between this `mov` and the faulting `ldr x1, [x1]` two instructions later — `x1`'s value at the fault is a literal constant compiled directly into this code, not a runtime read of any structure/field. The surrounding code (everything before the `mov`, in the same 64-byte window) is a register-context SAVE sequence: `stp` pairs spilling `x5/x6`, `x8/x9`, `x10/x11`, `w26/w27`, then `st1` vector stores of `v16`-`v23`, ALL addressed relative to `x28` (confirmed, live, `x28==CurrentFrame` at this exact fault, per the register receipt above) — i.e. this is FEXCore's own native state-checkpoint code, not a JIT translation of guest x86 instructions (consistent with Step 2's own "dispatcher trampoline class" classification). `x30` (`lr`, the return address) reads `0x26D0C017C`, inside the SAME `0x4000`-byte arena, about `0x134` bytes before the fault — consistent with this snippet being a called subroutine within the dispatcher/trampoline block (it has its own return address), not code fallen into from straight-line execution.

**Step 5 — cross-reference against documented structures**: every structure offset this project has so far reverse-engineered (FEX-MAP.md's own C4-33, C4-34, C4-49, C4-50, C4-51, C4-52, C4-53 sections) is a MEMORY-LOAD-based field, read via `ldr`/`str` at a fixed `base+offset` — `InternalThreadState::CurrentFrame` (offset `0`, first member), `CpuStateFrame::State.rip` (offset `0x18`), `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` (offset `0x5D8`), `InvalidationTracker`'s fixed global/`this` (`ImageBase+0x34c430`) and its `XIntervals` vector (offset `0` within that object), `Decoder`'s `ExecutableRangeBase`/`ExecutableRangeEnd`/`ExecutableRangeWritable`/`HitNonExecutableRange`/`InstStream`/`InstructionSize` fields (offsets `0x68`/`0x70`/`0x78`/`0x79`/`0x80`/`0x88`). None of these are relevant here: `x1`'s null value in this specific instance does not come from a memory load at all — it is a compiled-in immediate, with no base register and no offset to name. **Verdict: no match against any already-documented structure field.** Stated plainly, per this atom's own explicit instruction, and not chased further into an invented theory.

One factual, already-grounded observation is worth recording alongside that verdict (not offered as a new theory, and it does not change the verdict above): this exact `elr` address sits inside the same class of code this file's own PRE-EXISTING source comment (predating this atom, `kmain.c`, citing `IR::Break`, `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MiscOps.cpp:65-77`) already uses to explain how FEXCore synthesizes a guest exception — that mechanism's own documented design deliberately executes a trapping memory access to generate a hardware fault which the native signal/exception path then intercepts and re-packages as the correct guest-visible exception. A hardcoded null-pointer dereference is consistent with that already-cited design (a guaranteed, deliberate trap, not necessarily an uninitialized-field bug), but this is explicitly NOT offered as a new theory replacing Step 5's own verdict — it does not identify a matching structure field, and is not chased further.

**Step 6 — fix proposal**: none. Step 5 does not land on a concrete, addressable, floor-satisfiable gap — the null value is a constant compiled into FEXCore's own generated dispatcher code, inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob, not a field this kernel's own C sources supply, compute, or could patch. Per this atom's own condition ("if it requires deeper vendored-library changes or isn't clearly safe, name it and STOP without applying anything"): named, not applied.

## C4-60 — NAMED (unconditional `NtProtectVirtualMemory` raw-arg diagnostic, no vendored-blob disasm): hello8's own "extra JIT round" (C4-59's own sharpened clue) targets this floor's OWN C4-27 BOP-stub page, already notify-registered by the existing C4-54 fix — not a new floor-side gap, no fix applied.

**Diagnostic** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, +13 lines, resumed from a prior stalled lane's already-committed work): an unconditional print of `BaseAddress`/`*BaseAddress`/`RegionSize`/`*RegionSize`/`NewProtect`, placed before the existing `if (!BaseAddress || !*BaseAddress || !RegionSize || *RegionSize == 0) return STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER;` early return — every call is now visible, including ones that would otherwise bail out invisibly.

**Build/boot**: `GAIA_NO_RUN=1 ./build.sh` clean compile. `scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, full transcript `scratch/c4-60_pe10_fex_canonical_receipt_full.log` (8976 lines) — boots to `pe10 ret=42` cleanly, no regression from the diagnostic.

**Step 1 — diagnostic receipt / extra-round target**: 241 `CHALICE C4-60: NtProtectVirtualMemory` prints total this boot (`grep -c`). The transcript's tail (line 8943-8969):
```
8943: CHALICE C4-60: NtProtectVirtualMemory BaseAddress_ptr=0x4116B558 *BaseAddress=0x47661000 RegionSize_ptr=0x4116B550 *RegionSize=0x1000 NewProtect=0x20
8964: CHALICE C4-60: NtProtectVirtualMemory BaseAddress_ptr=0x4116B558 *BaseAddress=0x488D0000 RegionSize_ptr=0x4116B550 *RegionSize=0x1000 NewProtect=0x20
8966: CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC: id=90 ...                          (NtQuerySystemTime, unimplemented)
      CHALICE: svc id=90 UNIMPLEMENTED
      CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC: id=147 ...                          (NtFlushInstructionCache)
      CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC: id=44 ...                           (NtTerminateProcess)
8969: CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A
      pe10 ret=42
```
The LAST `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call before the clean exit (line 8964) targets `BaseAddress=0x488D0000 RegionSize=0x1000 (4096) NewProtect=0x20` (`PAGE_EXECUTE_READ`). `NtFlushInstructionCache`(id=147) follows this call but NOT immediately — one `NtQuerySystemTime`(id=90, unimplemented) call intervenes first, same as `NtQuerySystemTime`'s already-documented position in every prior atom's own tail-sequence receipts (C4-54/55/57/59).

**Address cross-reference against every documented base in this file**: `0x488D0000` is outside ALL of: `hello8_x86.exe` (`0x47660000`-`0x47666000`, C4-44), `syswow64/kernel32.dll` (`0x5D090000`+, C4-49/57), `syswow64/kernelbase.dll` (`0x5E1C0000`+, C4-46/52/53), `syswow64/ntdll.dll`, and the FEX JIT-cache fault-address family (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`/`0x2988xxxx`-range addresses, C4-58/59). Grepping the SAME transcript for `0x488D0000` finds it first at line 21 (`CHALICE: C4-27 stub buffer addr=0x488D0000 seeded at esp=0x488CC000`) and again at line 922 (`CHALICE C4-27: guest stub written addr=0x488D0000 bop=0x58040000 bytes=0xF`) — this is the C4-27 syscall-hinge BOP-stub page (`nt_territory_alloc(16)`, `ntboot.c`), the SAME fixed address C4-53 named as a then-new-fault target and C4-54 fixed via retroactive `InvalidationTracker` registration. Two `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification base=0x488D0000 size=0x10 prot=0x40` prints already fire earlier in this SAME transcript (lines 4303 and 5136 — once per subsequent dynamic x86 image map, kernel32.dll then kernelbase.dll, the SAME "harmless re-insert both times" pattern C4-54 documented) — from the RETROACTIVE one-shot call inside `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, fired the moment `nt_wow64_ready` first flips true (line 4302, on kernel32.dll's own dynamic map), NOT from any `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call on this address. A live `XIntervals` dump (lines 4460/4500/6664 etc, this floor's own pre-existing bracket-list print) confirms `[0x488D0000,0x488D1000)` present from line 4460 onward, through to the end of the transcript — the stub page is tracked continuously, well before the final protect-down call at line 8964 ever fires.

**Step 2 — filter-logic comparison verdict**: `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect` (`ntfile.c`) — the function `NtProtectVirtualMemory` calls unconditionally at every success return — is gated ONLY by `nt_wow64_ready` (a static flag, set once true and never reset, the first time a real guest x86 image other than `ntdll.dll` maps) and by `nt_c453_fex_base` (`libwow64fex.dll`'s base, resolved once and cached); once both are set, it fires for ANY nonzero `(base,size)` pair — it carries NO `Machine`/image-type filter of its own (per its own header comment: "Applies unconditionally to every NtProtectVirtualMemory call... donor semantics: BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect fires for ANY VirtualProtect, arena or image"). The SEPARATE `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` (wired to `NtMapViewOfSection`, C4-46/48) DOES filter: `u16b machine = ...; if (machine != 0x014C) return;` plus an explicit `ntdll.dll` skip (already covered by `BTCpuProcessInit`'s own dedicated path, C4-29). The C4-27 BOP-stub page is never mapped via `NtMapViewOfSection` at all — it is a raw `nt_territory_alloc(16)` kernel-side allocation with no PE header, written directly by `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready` (`ntsvc.c`) during `BTCpuProcessInit` — so it cannot structurally satisfy `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`'s own `Machine==0x14C` read (there is no `e_lfanew`/`Machine` field at that address to read). This is exactly the donor-gap class C4-54 already named ("crosses NEITHER of the two syscalls this floor forwards to FEX's notify chain") and already closed — via a THIRD, separate mechanism: a one-shot, unconditional call `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect(c454_stub_base, c454_stub_size, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, teb)` fired from inside `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` itself, at the exact moment `nt_wow64_ready` flips true, independent of which image triggered that flip. **Verdict: the C4-60 target already satisfies the applicable registration path** — it is tracked via the C4-54 retroactive mechanism, confirmed live (lines 4303/5136 above), well before the final protect-down call at line 8964 ever executes. No new gap exists for this target.

**Side curiosity, noted, not chased**: the specific final protect-down call itself (line 8964, `0x488D0000`, `NewProtect=0x20`) produces no MATCHING `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification base=0x488D0000 ... prot=0x20` print of its own (the notify function's own `puts()` line, which per its source runs unconditionally once past its two gates, does not appear to have fired for this specific call, nor for most other single-page protect-down calls observed after the `nt_wow64_ready` flip point — e.g. `0x486DE000`/`0x486BB000`/`0x486BC000`/`0x486F4000`, all `NewProtect=0x20`, all missing a matching notify print; contrast whole-DLL-section save/restore pairs, e.g. kernelbase's own `.text` range, kernel32's own sections, `0x5D0C0A68`, which consistently DO get a matching print). Both `nt_wow64_ready` and `nt_c453_fex_base` are static globals set once and never reset in this codebase (grepped, confirmed no other assignment sites), so this discrepancy is not explained by either gate re-closing. Not instrumented/chased further this atom (would need a probe INSIDE `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect` itself, before/after the `pe_enter6` call, to see whether the native call is even reached for these specific addresses) — immaterial to this atom's own outcome, since `0x488D0000`'s own tracked-range membership was already established via the earlier, independently-confirmed retroactive registration (lines 4303/5136), and the boot completes cleanly (`ret=42`) regardless of whether this LAST call's own notify attempt fires.

**Step 3 — mechanistic conclusion, answers C4-59's own sharpened clue**: C4-59 measured hello9's own boot as short exactly one `NtProtectVirtualMemory`+`NtFlushInstructionCache` pair compared to hello8's, and hypothesized this was "consistent with FEX's own internal JIT compiler needing more work for hello9's larger `exe_entry` block." This atom's own address-identity finding narrows that: hello8's "extra" pair (line 8964's call + its trailing `NtFlushInstructionCache`) is NOT a second compile pass over `exe_entry` or any guest DLL code — it targets the C4-27 BOP-stub page specifically, a floor-internal construct that only gets written/JIT-compiled/protected AT ALL because `hello8_x86.c`'s `PeMain() { return 42; }` executes a bare `ret`, which (per C4-27/55's own design) lands in this floor's own synthetic stub, triggering the stub's own one-time JIT-compile-and-protect-down step immediately before its `NtTerminateProcess(-1,42)` call. hello9 cannot structurally take this same round, independent of any notify/registration gap: per C4-57 (guest Eip frozen at `exe_entry`, every register pristine at the first exception) and C4-58/59 (the zeroing/fatal-fault event happens strictly inside FEXCore's own native dispatch loop, before any marker-reset-adjacent syscall that would correspond to reaching `PeMain`'s own `ret`), hello9's guest CPU never advances far enough to execute `PeMain`'s `ret` at all — so it never lands in the BOP stub, so the stub's own JIT-compile/protect/flush round never has a reason to happen. The "missing pair" C4-59 measured is a direct, structural DOWNSTREAM CONSEQUENCE of the already-diagnosed (C4-58/59) fact that hello9 never reaches that point — not an independent, additional, floor-fixable gap.

**No fix applied, per this atom's own explicit stop condition**: steps 4+5 (address identity + filter-logic comparison) do not name a concrete, small, source-only notify-wiring gap symmetric to the C4-46/53/54 pattern — the target is already correctly registered by the existing C4-54 fix. The reason hello9 lacks the corresponding round is the ALREADY-NAMED, already-time-boxed-as-blob-internal (C4-58/59) fact that hello9's Eip never leaves `exe_entry` — re-diagnosing that same frontier is out of this atom's own scope (it would not be "a small fix" per the task's own condition; C4-59 already spent a full atom measuring it to the tightest observable window and confirmed it is strictly inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob's own native dispatch-loop code). No hello9 swap/re-test attempted this atom.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-60_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, GATE PASS (required-green). `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0` (`wowbox64`) / `pe9 fex ret=0` (`libwow64fex.dll`), GATE PASS, no regression, both CPU backends. `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, GATE PASS (ramfb pattern correct). `pe10 fex` canonical (`scratch/c4-60_pe10_fex_canonical_receipt_full.log`) → `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS**, no regression from the new diagnostic (this same receipt IS this atom's own final, LAST-run canonical confirmation — no source changed after this boot, no separate re-run needed).

## C4-59 — MEASURED (live State.rip instrumentation, no vendored-blob disasm): C4-58's own fault-shape framing CORRECTED; syscall 49 identity nailed + ruled out; the zeroing event narrowed to the tightest measurable window and confirmed genuinely blob-internal, not floor-satisfiable.

Reuses `nt_fault_read32`'s existing arena/territory/vm-region-aware safe-probe for every read past the raw TEB→TlsSlots deref (that one raw deref matches the SAME unchecked-TEB-pointer convention `ntsvc.c`'s own TlsSlots WIRING code already uses, ~line 2375). Wired at three sites: (1) `kmain.c`'s `sync_try_guest_exception`, tagged with a new shared `nt_c459_cycle` counter (extern global, `ntfile.c`), fires on EVERY guest-exception delivery (superset of the `elr=0x26D0C02B0` class); (2) `ntsvc.c`'s `NtSetInformationThread`, x18 captured as the function's OWN absolute first statement (same clobber-safety idiom C4-48/53/54 established) before any `rd32`/`puts` call could scratch it, tags with the SAME `nt_c459_cycle` (read-only, cross-TU correlation); (3) `ntdisp.S`'s `NtContinue`, one new `mov x1,x18` before the existing `bl nt_continue_trace` (the caller's — FEX-internal native code's — OWN live x18, preserved per ARM64 ABI convention across its own calls into this floor, same convention every other syscall-side TEB reader already relies on), `nt_continue_trace`'s own pre-existing 12-call print cap left in place for its OWN old print but a NEW, UNCAPPED read+log added ahead of it — C4-58's own "12x `CHALICE CONTINUE`" count (both transcripts) was an ARTIFACT of that pre-existing cap, not the true call count; the true count, measured this atom, is 20 per boot (one per shared self-heal cycle), matching the corrected cycle count below exactly.

**Step 1 — CORRECTED fault-shape/count, measured live, not re-derived from the C4-57/58 trace-diff receipts**: booted BOTH canonical hello8 (`scratch/c4-59_hello8_receipt_full.log`) and a temp hello9 swap (C4-55/56 method — `mkpe32.sh` rebuild, `shell.c`/`build.sh` re-pointed, checkpoint-committed before boot, reverted after, `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` stay untracked; `scratch/c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log` is the full, final receipt with the uncapped `NtContinue` instrumentation) with the new instrumentation live. **C4-58's own framing ("both boots already hit 21 times harmlessly" at `elr=0x26D0C02B0`) does not survive direct measurement**: `grep`ing every `CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` line out of both fresh transcripts shows the shared prefix is 20 `esr=0x96000061`/`0x96000021` faults (`ec=0x25`, `status=0x80000002`=`STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`, i.e. the C4-40 `dfsc=0x21` alignment-fault branch of `sync_try_guest_exception`), at 20 DIFFERENT `elr` addresses each (`0x29887D634`, `0x298882514`, `0x298882524`, ... `0x29887A498` — JIT-cache addresses, never `0x26D0C02B0`), byte-identical in both boots' own sequence/order/count. `elr=0x26D0C02B0` (`esr=0x96000006`, `status=0xC0000005`=`STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`, the OTHER, general `ec==0x24||0x25` branch — genuinely a DIFFERENT fault class: `dfsc=0x06` translation fault, not alignment) appears EXACTLY ONCE in either transcript, ONLY in hello9's, as the terminal, fatal fault — never once during any of the 20 shared cycles, never at all in hello8. This corrects, not merely extends, C4-58's own count/address claim.

**Step 2 — syscall id 49 identity + verdict**: `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt:42` — `49  0x031  NtQueryPerformanceCounter` (real Wine ARM64 syscall number, decoded from stock `ntdll.dll`'s own `Zw*` stubs, this file's own pre-existing table). This floor's `nt_svc_table0[NT_SVC_...]` registration (`ntsvc.c`, `nt_svc_init`-class setup) never assigns a handler for id 49 — it stays at the array's blanket `NtStub` default (`return STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`), confirmed by grep (zero `NT_SVC_NTQUERYPERFORMANCECOUNTER`-shaped identifier or `case 49` anywhere in this floor's own sources). **Live count, both boots, this atom's own fresh receipts**: hello8 calls it 2429 times over its FULL boot, hello9 ALSO calls it 2429 times over its full (shorter, fatal-terminated) boot — `grep -c "svc id=49"` on both fresh transcripts, IDENTICAL totals. Narrowed to the specific window that matters (the burst right after the shared marker-reset, immediately preceding hello8's clean exit / hello9's fatal fault): EXACTLY 18 occurrences in BOTH boots, same position (right after `NtClose`(id=15)+`NtTestAlert`(id=248, also UNIMPLEMENTED)), same interleaving with `NtFlushInstructionCache`(id=147)/`NtProtectVirtualMemory`(id=80), same STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED tolerance both times (FEX's own internal polling/backoff loop plainly treats an unimplemented QPC identically either way, since hello8 proceeds to a completely normal clean exit despite the identical 18 failures). **Verdict: hello9 does NOT need syscall 49 any more than hello8 does** — implementing it honestly (a real ARM64 `cntvct_el0`-derived counter/frequency pair) would change nothing observable about this specific divergence, since the WORKING boot already tolerates the exact same failure count/pattern at the exact same position. Ruled out as a fix candidate by direct measurement, not by assumption.

**Step 3 — State.rip-per-cycle table (hello9, `scratch/c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`; hello8's own table is byte-identical through cycle 20 + the marker-reset, `scratch/c4-59_hello8_receipt_full.log`, confirmed diffed)**:

```
cycle=0x02 elr=0x298882514 State.rip=0x486C9A46   (NtContinue #2 resume: same value)

cycle=0x05..0x14 (5-20): same pattern, State.rip ALWAYS matches its own cycle's fault + its own NtContinue resume, byte-identical elr/State.rip sequence in BOTH boots through cycle 0x14 (20)
[marker-reset, IDENTICAL both boots] NtSetInformationThread eip=0x58040000 (BOP marker) -- live-State.rip READ AT THIS CALL =0x58040000 (valid, matches cycle 20's own NtContinue resume)
[marker-reset, IDENTICAL both boots] NtSetInformationThread eip=0x47661000 (exe_entry)   -- live-State.rip READ AT THIS CALL =0x58040000 (still the PRE-write value -- FEX's own post-syscall-return State.rip=Context->Eip assignment, donor Module.cpp:202, happens in FEX-internal code AFTER this floor's own C syscall handler returns, never observed directly)
[IDENTICAL 18x NtQueryPerformanceCounter(id=49) burst, both boots, interleaved w/ NtClose/NtTestAlert/NtFlushInstructionCache/NtProtectVirtualMemory -- Step 2 above]
hello8: 2nd JIT round (NtProtectVirtualMemory + NtFlushInstructionCache AGAIN) -> id=90(NtQuerySystemTime,unimpl) -> id=147 -> id=44 NtTerminateProcess(-1,42) -> pe10 ret=42, CLEAN, State.rip never read again (process already exiting)

cycle=0x15 elr=0x26D0C02B0 (NEVER SEEN BEFORE in either transcript) State.rip=0x0000000000000000  <-- FIRST AND ONLY ZERO, ANYWHERE, EITHER BOOT
  (esr=0x96000006 dfsc=0x06 TRANSLATION FAULT, status=0xC0000005 STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION -- A DIFFERENT CLASS from all 20 shared cycles' dfsc=0x21 ALIGNMENT faults)
```

**The zeroing event**: strictly between the marker-reset's own 2nd `NtSetInformationThread` syscall RETURNING (last live read: `State.rip=0x58040000`, i.e. still the PRE-write value, both boots identical) and cycle 0x15's own fault delivery (`State.rip=0x0`) there is NO further syscall or guest-exception-delivery boundary in EITHER boot except the SAME 18x-QPC-burst+NtClose+NtTestAlert+NtFlushInstructionCache+NtProtectVirtualMemory sequence hello8 ALSO takes, byte-identically, in the SAME position, immediately before its own clean exit — meaning FEXCore's OWN internal, native ARM64 dispatch-loop code (between processing the marker-reset SET call's own post-return `State.rip=Context->Eip` assignment and whatever iteration next reads `State.rip` for JIT dispatch) is where the value goes from a valid nonzero seed to 0, entirely unobserved by any syscall/exception hook this floor has. This is the TIGHTEST possible measurement window without either (a) a hardware single-step/watchpoint capability this floor does not have, or (b) disassembling `libwow64fex.dll`'s own compiled machine code at the exact post-marker-reset continuation address -- both out of this atom's cheap-measurement budget, same as C4-58's own conclusion, now corrected/sharpened rather than merely repeated.

**Verdict, all three of this task's own named floor-satisfiable candidates, REFUTED by direct measurement, not assumed away**: (1) implementing syscall 49 -- REFUTED, Step 2 above, byte-identical call count/position/tolerance in the WORKING boot. (2) `NtContinue`'s own context round-trip losing rip -- REFUTED, Step 3's own table: EVERY one of hello9's 20 `NtContinue` resumes (now uncapped, all logged, not just the old print's first 12) reads a fully valid, correctly-tracking, nonzero `State.rip`, the 20th (last) included -- the zero first appears AFTER the last successful resume, with no further `NtContinue` call anywhere in between. (3) "seed State.rip on a missed path" -- REFUTED, the marker-reset's own 2nd `NtSetInformationThread` SET call supplies a valid nonzero `eip=0x47661000` in BOTH boots, byte-identical -- nothing on this floor's own side ever supplies or observes a zero value before the fault itself.

**No fix applied -- not floor-satisfiable, per task's own explicit condition, CONFIRMED not merely repeated**: with all three named candidates directly refuted and the zeroing window narrowed to the tightest measurable point (strictly inside FEXCore's own native dispatch-loop code, zero syscall/exception boundary crossing it), a small kernel-side (zoneA C) patch has nothing left to change -- the divergence genuinely lives inside the vendored, pre-built native `libwow64fex.dll` blob's own JIT-compile/dispatch bookkeeping for this SPECIFIC one-extra-round case, same as C4-58's own verdict, now measured rather than inferred. hello9's `WriteFile` milestone (the done-line's own gate, explicitly conditioned on `ret=42`) is NOT reached. ## C4-58 (superseded framing, kept verbatim for the record — see C4-59 above for the corrected fault-shape/count and syscall-49 verdict) — TRACE DIFF (no code, existing receipts only): hello8/hello9 divergence NAMED, not in the loader/import-attach path at all — FEX's OWN native (vendored `libwow64fex.dll`) WOW64-context reset/exception-packaging machinery, on ONE EXTRA recurring internal JIT self-fault unique to hello9's boot timing, writes a NULL (`Eip=0x0`) synthetic guest context instead of a valid marker address; guest x86 code is PROVEN (fresh, exception-refreshed read, not the C4-45-flagged stale one) to have NEVER EXECUTED A SINGLE INSTRUCTION before this fires. **Step 1 — trace diff, cheap, no code, existing receipts only** (`scratch/c4-57_pe10_fex_final_canonical_receipt.log`=hello8 canonical, `scratch/c4-57_hello9_swap_binder_receipt.log`=hello9 swap, both already fresh/on-disk from the immediately-prior C4-57 atom, same commit state, no rebuild needed): extracted the `^CHALICE [A-Z0-9_ ]+:` marker-type sequence from both (`grep -oE`), diffed. **Aggregate counts are IDENTICAL between the two boots**: 21×`CHALICE EXCEPTION:`, 21×`CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT:`, 12×`CHALICE CONTINUE:`, 12×`CHALICE ERET TRUTH:` in EACH transcript — the two boots take the exact same CLASS and COUNT of internal self-heal fault cycles throughout; the line-level diff noise in the bulk of both transcripts (blocks appearing at shifted offsets) is pure timing/ordering jitter from this floor's own already-documented nondeterministic ARENA/alignment-self-fault class (C4-38/40, harmless, expected), NOT a structural divergence — confirmed by the equal totals. **The two transcripts are BYTE-IDENTICAL in TYPE all the way through the shared JIT steady-state tail**, ending at the same line both share verbatim: `CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC: id=49 flags=0x0000000047085144 pc=0x0000000200000000 sp=0x0000000000000000` / `CHALICE: svc id=49 UNIMPLEMENTED`. **Immediately after that shared line is the FIRST and ONLY point the two transcripts genuinely diverge**:
- hello8 (canonical): proceeds straight to `id=80`→`id=90 (NtQuerySystemTime) UNIMPLEMENTED`→`id=147`→`id=44 (NtTerminateProcess)` → `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → `pe10 ret=42`.
- hello9 (swap): instead takes ONE MORE full internal self-fault cycle at the IDENTICAL recurring JIT-internal site both boots already hit 21 times harmlessly (`CHALICE ARENA: esr=0x96000006 ec=0x25 iss=0x06 elr=0x26D0C02B0 far=0x0`, `dfsc=0x06` translation fault, SAME `elr` value as every prior occurrence in BOTH transcripts, a fixed FEX JIT-translation-cache address, NOT inside `hello9_x86.exe`'s own mapped range `0x47660000-0x47666000`) — but THIS ONE occurrence, unlike the prior 21 in either boot, does not silently self-heal via `CONTINUE`/`ERET TRUTH`; it is delivered onward: `CHALICE EXCEPTION: code=0xC0000005 elr=0x26D0C02B0 dispatch=0x47086C40`, then TWO `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)` calls (id=13) write **`eip=0x0000000000000000`** — NOT a valid marker address like the `0x58040000`(BOP-trampoline)/`0x47661000`(exe_entry) pair the SAME mechanism writes at the tail-end of EVERY OTHER boot (including hello8's own, verbatim, a few lines earlier in both transcripts) — read back as `0x0` by the immediately-following `NtQueryInformationThread`, then `NtRaiseException` (id=207, real syscall id `0xcf` per `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt`) fires `code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, `FirstChance=FALSE` (second-chance, unhandled) → `pe10 ret=3221225477`.

**Phase named**: the divergence is NOT the loader/import-attach path (Road-B's binder already proven engaged, 2/2 bound, C4-57), NOT a TLS callback, NOT any code inside `hello9_x86.exe` itself at all (see Step 2 below — the guest CPU never ran). It is FEX's own native WOW64-context-reset/exception-packaging bookkeeping (the SAME long-flagged, never-fixed "internal dispatcher/signal-return marker" mechanism already named across C4-18/21/24/26/29/30/54 — always previously observed writing VALID marker addresses, never 0) misfiring specifically on the ONE EXTRA recurring internal self-fault that hello9's different boot timing causes to land AFTER the shared steady-state tail, where hello8's own boot ordering never reaches this fault at that position at all (hello8 exits cleanly one fault-cycle "earlier" in the same recurring sequence). The timing shift itself is plausibly attributable to hello9's materially different image shape (real `.idata`/IAT section + the extra `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification`/IAT-bind bookkeeping this floor's own C4-57 Road-B binder performs while mapping it) shifting exactly WHEN in the syscall sequence this internal JIT self-fault class recurs — not proven to the instruction level (that would need vendored `libwow64fex.dll` disasm, out of this atom's no-code/cheap-step budget), but the SHARED-then-diverging-at-one-point trace shape is decisive that this is where the fork happens.

**Step 2 — true guest ctx at the fault, FRESH (exception-refreshed, NOT the C4-45-flagged stale stash) — verified by source-reading, no new instrumentation needed**: the existing `CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX`/`GUEST BYTES` print (`ntsvc.c`, `NtRaiseException`, gated on `nt_raise_trace_count==1` — fires ONCE, at the very FIRST `NtRaiseException` call in the ENTIRE boot, which is exactly this fault) reads through `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg`, a POINTER into the live `WOW64_CPURESERVED` memory block, not a value snapshot — the same mechanism a prior atom (source comment, `ntsvc.c` ~line 290) already proved live/current for a genuine exception delivery specifically ("`BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` reconstructs WowContext from the live FEXCore thread state then calls `BTCpuSetContext`... with NO `NtSetInformationThread` syscall round-trip... confirmed live: no id=13 SET call appears between the last `BTCpuSimulate` entry and this RAISE#1") — this is the OPPOSITE case from the C4-45-flagged stale read (that warning applies only to reading this same pointer during an ORDINARY `NtTerminateProcess` syscall, where FEX never refreshes it; here it is read at the exception itself, RAISE#1, the one case already proven fresh). Content, this hello9 receipt: `Eip=0x47661000 (hello9_x86.exe+0x1000)` = `exe_entry`, byte-identical to the boot-seeded value; `Esp=0x488CC000` = the boot-seeded initial stack; `Ebp=Esp` (empty-frame convention); `Eax=Ecx=Edx=Ebx=Esi=Edi=0x0` — **every single guest x86 register is still at its pristine boot-seed default**, not merely `Eip` — matching the C4-45-documented "boot-seed defaults, no real execution" fingerprint exactly, but now on the FRESH exception-time path, not the stale one C4-45 flagged. `GUEST BYTES @Eip-16..+32`: leading zeros then `55 89 E5 83 EC 18 ...` (`push ebp; mov ebp,esp; sub esp,0x18`) — hello9's own real, correctly-mapped `PeMain` prologue, present in guest memory, byte-for-byte plausible x86 function prologue — the code IS there, correctly relocated/mapped, simply never fetched. `GUEST STACK @Esp-8..+16`: `0x0 0x488CC000 0x488D0000 0x0 0x0 0x0` — no return address, no call-chain, consistent with a frame that was never entered.

**Step 3 — verdict, named jumper-to-0**: NOT guest code (every register pristine at the very first exception the whole boot ever raises — the CPU never executed one real x86 instruction of `hello9_x86.exe`'s own code). NOT this floor's own kernel C (`ntfile.c`/`ntsvc.c` grepped end-to-end: no call site anywhere ever writes a literal 0 into an EIP/CTX32_EIP-shaped field). NOT wine's guest-side `KiUserExceptionDispatcher`/SEH — never reached; every `CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC` entry surrounding this whole sequence shows `pc=0x4DA434E4` or the shared native dispatcher trampoline address, which a prior atom's own source comment (`ntsvc.c` ~line 290) already established is ALWAYS `libwow64fex.dll`'s fixed native re-entry stub (`BTCpuSimulate+0x10`) regardless of the real guest fault site — never a guest address. **Named**: FEX's own native WOW64-context "reset to consistent state"/exception-packaging routine, inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob (`Module.cpp`, the same `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl`-class machinery already cross-referenced in this file's own source comments) — for this one EXTRA recurring internal JIT self-fault instance (unique to hello9's boot-timing shift, named in Step 1), it computes/writes a NULL (`Eip=0`) synthetic guest context via `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)` instead of the valid marker address (`0x58040000`/`0x47661000`) it writes at the equivalent point in every OTHER boot (including hello8's own) — that null value is what ultimately surfaces as `NtRaiseException(..., addr=0x0)`'s fatal, unhandled AV.

## C4-57 — Road-B kernel-side i386 IAT binder built + engages correctly, but does NOT fix the hello9 boot crash — new frontier: guest Eip never leaves `exe_entry`, bound IAT or not.

**Road A (route the main EXE through wine's own guest-loader `fixup_imports`) — evaluated by source-tracing only, not live-tested**: C4-42..45's own live proofs establish wine's real guest-executed loader chain (`ntdll32 LdrInitializeThunk -> loader_init -> build_main_module(LDR_DONT_RESOLVE_REFS) -> fixup_imports(wm, NULL)` on the main module, donor `dlls/ntdll/loader.c:4507-4513`) genuinely runs and correctly binds DLL-to-DLL imports for `kernel32.dll`/`kernelbase.dll` themselves — but its entry point is Hangover/FEX's OWN per-process container bootstrap (`Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`, native ARM64, `BTCpuProcessInit`-driven), which loads SYSTEM DLLs for FEX's own bookkeeping and never reaches wine's PE-side USER EXE loader at all. Why: this floor's Phase-0 (`nt_boot_process`, `ntboot.c`) maps the boot EXE directly and seeds the guest's FIRST Eip straight at the EXE's own entry point — never at any `ntdll32 LdrInitializeThunk` — so `build_main_module`/`fixup_imports` never fire for the boot EXE's OWN Import Directory. Road A's fix would mean re-architecting Phase-0 to inject the main image through `LdrLoadDll`/`build_main_module` instead of direct entry-seeding — a materially larger, riskier surface change. **Rejected in favor of Road B** (smaller, self-contained, no Phase-0 boot-sequence change).

**Road B — kernel-side i386 IAT binder, built + PERMANENT** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, +168 lines, commit `20c104c`): once BOTH `syswow64/kernel32.dll` and `syswow64/kernelbase.dll` are guest-mapped (proven live by C4-42..45), walks the boot EXE's OWN PE32 Import Directory (`IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR` array, 4-byte `IMAGE_THUNK_DATA32`/`IMAGE_IMPORT_BY_NAME` shapes — deliberately NOT `pemod.c`'s existing `do_imports` code, which hardcodes 8-byte `IMAGE_THUNK_DATA64` reads/writes for PE32+/DLL-to-DLL imports and resolves via `zc_resolve()` (the kernel-native-stub table) — the wrong tool for a guest i386 IAT against real `KERNELBASE`/`KERNEL32` exports, per C4-56's own verdict) and resolves each name via the already-`is_pe32`-aware `pe_find_named_export` (C4-36) against those two real, live-mapped images, writing the resolved 32-bit VA directly into the IAT slot. One-shot (`nt_iat_bind_done` latch), fires from `NtMapViewOfSection` the moment the second of the two DLLs registers (same call-site-driven-notify idiom C4-46/48/53/54's own `InvalidationTracker` hooks already use) — always strictly before the guest's own seeded Eip executes (both DLL loads are driven by FEX's own container bootstrap, which completes long before `BTCpuSimulate` ever reads the synthetic Eip/Esp seed).

**hello9 swap-boot receipt, binder engaged** (temporary re-swap, `mkpe32.sh` rebuild + `shell.c`/`build.sh` re-pointed at `hello9_x86.exe`, for documentation/measurement only — reverted immediately after, net-zero diff restored, confirmed via `git diff 2eecfa8`; transcript saved `scratch/c4-57_hello9_swap_binder_receipt.log`):
```
CHALICE C4-57: both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped -- binding boot exe IAT
CHALICE C4-57: import DLL=KERNEL32.dll
CHALICE C4-57: bound GetStdHandle -> 0x000000005D09D7AC
CHALICE C4-57: bound WriteFile -> 0x000000005D09F9BC
CHALICE C4-57: IAT bind done bound=0x0000000000000002 failed=0x0000000000000000
```
Both imports resolved successfully (`2/2 bound, 0 failed`) against the real, live-mapped `syswow64/kernel32.dll`/`syswow64/kernelbase.dll` export tables — the binder's own logic works exactly as designed.

**But the fault that follows is BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-56's own pre-binder receipt** — same signature, same address, same everything:
```
CHALICE ARENA: esr=0x0000000096000006 ec=0x0000000000000025 iss=0x0000000000000006 elr=0x000000026D0C02B0 far=0x0000000000000000
CHALICE C4-38 ESR: dfsc=0x0000000000000006 wnr=0x0000000000000000 (dfsc 0x21==ALIGNMENT FAULT per ARM ARM, not translation)
CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT: esr=0x0000000096000006 ec=0x0000000000000025 status=0x00000000C0000005 elr=0x000000026D0C02B0 far=0x0000000000000000 wnr=0x0000000000000000
CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0x00000000C0000005 addr=0x0000000000000000
CHALICE: NtRaiseException FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating
pe10 ret=3221225477
```
`elr=0x26D0C02B0` — a JIT translation-cache address, NOT inside `hello9_x86.exe`'s own mapped range (`base=0x47660000 size=0x6000` per the boot's own `CHALICE IMAGE`/`CHALICE PROBE` lines) — and critically, the final `CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX` dump at termination reads:
```
CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX: Eip=0x0000000047661000 (hello9_x86.exe+0x0000000000001000) Esp=0x00000000488CC000 ...
```
**`Eip=0x47661000` is the exe's OWN entry point, byte-identical to the value seeded at boot start (`CHALICE PROBE: exe_entry=0x0000000047661000`) — the guest program counter NEVER ADVANCED from `exe_entry` at all**, with a correctly-bound IAT or not. No `hello9: the door opens` string ever printed, no write-syscall ever appears in the transcript — hello9's own `PeMain` code was never reached/simulated in the first place, so the (now correctly bound) `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile` IAT slots were never even read by guest code.

**Verdict**: the C4-56 "unbound IAT" theory named a REAL gap (now genuinely fixed, permanently, by this Road-B binder — 2/2 resolved, correct addresses written, before guest execution per the design above) — but it is **NOT the (or not the sole) cause of the hello9 crash**. With the IAT correctly bound, the identical fault fires at the identical address with the guest Eip still frozen at `exe_entry` — proving whatever faults here happens UPSTREAM of ever simulating hello9's own entry-point bytes, independent of import-table state. Most likely candidate (not yet isolated): FEX's own per-image JIT-compilation/init path triggered specifically by mapping `hello9_x86.exe` (which differs from `hello8_x86.exe` in more than its import table — it carries a real `.idata`/IAT section, is a larger image, and was linked against `-lkernel32`, a materially different section/directory layout than hello8's zero-import minimal PE) faults during ITS OWN bootstrap of this image, before guest-code simulation ever begins — NOT a guest-level indirect-call-through-IAT fault at all (that theory would predict the SAME crash disappearing once the IAT is bound, which it did not).

**Next-atom pointer, named explicitly**: instrument whether FEX's `BTCpuSimulate`/JIT-translate path is EVER entered for hello9's `exe_entry` block at all — a guest-instruction-fetch trace at `0x47661000` (same idiom as `scratch/pe10_exec_trace.py`'s QEMU `-d exec` fallback, or a live kernel-side receipt at whatever call reads/dispatches the guest Eip before `BTCpuSimulate`) — BEFORE assuming any further import-table work is the blocker. If the JIT never even attempts to translate `exe_entry`'s bytes, the fault is in FEX's own per-image init/mapping path for THIS specific image shape (larger, with a real Import Directory), not in guest-code execution.

**Canonical-state restore, this atom's own close-out**: prior lane's revert commit (`59985fc`) was already byte-shape-identical to canonical `2eecfa8` (`git diff 2eecfa8 59985fc` = empty, confirmed) but left uncommitted rebuild-timestamp churn on disk (`kernel-arm/chalice/*.exe` + `kernel-arm/zoneA/pehello_bin.c` — every `build.sh`/`mkpe32.sh` run embeds a fresh PE-header build-timestamp field, pure noise, not a real diff) plus untracked `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb`; both cleaned this atom (`git checkout --`, `rm`) and RE-cleaned after every subsequent rebuild (the documentation re-swap above, and the final gate-ladder run below) — confirmed net-zero vs `2eecfa8` as the final state.

**Gates, no regression** (canonical hello8 wiring, full ladder this atom): `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, `GATE PASS (required-green)`. `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0` (`wowbox64`) and `pe9 fex ret=0` (`libwow64fex.dll`), `GATE PASS (required-green, no regression, both CPU backends)` (`scratch/c4-57_pe9_gate.log`). `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, `GATE PASS (ramfb pattern correct)` (`scratch/c4-57_ramfb_gate.log`). `pe10 fex` final canonical receipt (`scratch/c4-57_pe10_fex_final_canonical_receipt.log`): `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS**.

**Commits**: source-only. `20c104c` (Road-B binder, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, +168 lines, KEPT — real, permanent, working fix for the import-binding gap, independent of the still-open hello9 frontier above) + the prior lane's already-existing `d4e49a8` (temp hello9 swap, pre-boot checkpoint) / `59985fc` (revert to canonical, post-receipt) pair, both net-zero `shell.c`/`build.sh` diffs vs pre-atom canonical. This atom's own documentation re-swap (binder-engaged hello9 boot) was applied and reverted in-tree without a standalone commit (temporary, fully undone, receipt preserved in `scratch/`).
