## C4-56 — hello9 WriteFile-milestone leg: **NOT the C4-40 alignment gap** (task's own premise DISPROVEN by live ESR) — a NEW, deeper, un-trivial import-binding gap named, not fixed.

**Re-measured, fresh boot, byte-identical to C4-55's own swap-attempt receipt** (`scratch/c4-56_hello9_swap_reattempt_receipt.log`, checkpoint `e5f3d38` before boot): `CHALICE ARENA: esr=0x96000006 ec=0x25 iss=0x06 elr=0x26D0C02B0 far=0x0` → `CHALICE C4-38 ESR: dfsc=0x06`. **`dfsc=0x06` is ARM ARM Translation-fault-level-2 — NOT `0x21` (alignment)** — the task's own premise ("if DFSC=0x21 alignment") does not hold; this is a completely different fault CLASS from the six C4-40 alignment sites, so the C4-40 `STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`→FEX-backpatch path was never a candidate here — there is nothing for it to "engage" on. Confirmed via the SAME `sync_try_guest_exception` branch (`kmain.c`, C4-40's own fix): `ec==0x25 && dfsc!=0x21` correctly falls through to the pre-existing `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` branch (`params=2`) — this delivery mechanism worked EXACTLY as designed: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, `FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`. **The routing is not the bug.**

**Identity check against the six C4-40 alignment sites and against C4-30/31's own `NoExecOp` identity** — read live from the SAME TLS `SynchronousFaultData` probe C4-30/31 built (`kmain.c`): `raw=0x00000015020E0B01 Signal=0xB(SIGSEGV) TrapNo=0xE(X86_TRAPNO_PF) si_code=2 err_code=0x15(PROT|USER|INSTR)` — **byte-for-byte identical to C4-30/31's own `NoExecOp` ("instruction fetch from non-executable/absent guest memory") signature**, `err_code`'s `INSTR` bit (`0x10`) proving this is an EXECUTE-class fault (an attempted instruction FETCH), not a data read — even though the ARM64 hardware Data-Abort (`ec=0x25`) fired on a data LOAD instruction at `elr` (disasm below): the ARM64 hardware fault is FEX's OWN JIT-internal mechanism reading a (still-zero) function pointer and then branching through it; the GUEST-VISIBLE fault FEX synthesizes from that is correctly an instruction-fetch AV at guest address `0x0`, matching `far=0x0`/`addr=0x0` exactly.

**Disasm of the fault site** (`elr-32..+32`, `llvm-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64` on the receipt's own 16-word hexdump — a JIT code-cache address, NOT resolvable via `map-fex-offsets.sh`, which the C4-40 receipt already established only resolves STATIC DLL offsets, never JIT'd guest code; attempted per this atom's own task text, structurally inapplicable, not run):
```
mov   w1, #0x0        // <- materializes guest address 0 into a register
ldr   x1, [x1]        // <- ELR: reads the (zero) contents at address 0
mrs   x0, nzcv         // JIT context-save boilerplate around it (x28-relative SIMD/flag spills)
...
```
`mov w1,#0` then `ldr x1,[x1]` immediately upstream of the fault is consistent with a call THROUGH an unbound Import Address Table slot whose content is still the on-disk, never-patched value (i.e., an indirect call/jump ending up at guest address `0x0`), not with a data structure genuinely computed to be null elsewhere.

**Root cause, source-traced (not guessed)**: `nt_boot_process` (`ntboot.c:327`) maps the boot EXE via `PeMapImage(exe_bytes, exe_len, "pe5-exe", nt_territory_alloc, 0, 1, ...)` — **`do_imports=0`** (5th arg), by original SLICE3-SPEC verdict-4 design ("DLL imports are the PE-side loader's own job, never kernel-side" — `pemod.c`'s own comment, correct for `hello8_x86.exe`, which has ZERO imports, `Import Directory (0,0)`, per C4-44's own receipt). `hello9_x86.exe` is the FIRST boot EXE with a REAL Import Directory (`KERNEL32.dll`: `GetStdHandle`, `WriteFile`, confirmed this atom via `llvm-objdump -p hello9_x86.exe`, `IAT Directory` at RVA `0x205c` size `0xc`). Base relocations for `hello9_x86.exe` (`ImageBase=0x00400000`, mapped this floor at `0x47660000`, `.reloc` present, size `0x14`) ARE correctly applied (`apply_relocs=1`, same call) — but relocation only fixes up absolute-address OPERANDS (e.g. a call site's embedded IAT-slot address), never the IAT slot's own CONTENT, which is exactly what real import BINDING (writing the resolved `KERNEL32.dll` function pointers into the IAT) is for. That binding step is supposed to happen via the guest's OWN real Wine/`ntdll32` loader (`process_init`/`build_module`/`import_dll`, the same machinery C4-45 already proved wires `kernel32.dll`+`kernelbase.dll` correctly for DLL-to-DLL imports) — but this floor's Phase-0 boot injects the MAIN image directly (`PeMapImage`+`nt_image_register`, bypassing `LdrLoadDll` entirely, `ntboot.c`'s own "own bootstrap" comment) and NEVER routes the main EXE through that same guest-loader import-fixup pass. Net effect: `hello9_x86.exe`'s `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile` IAT slots stay at their unbound, on-disk (zero) values; the very first indirect call through either slot jumps to guest address `0x0`, faulting exactly as measured. **NOT trivial** — the correct fix requires either (a) teaching the Phase-0 injection path to invoke the SAME real guest-loader import-fixup the DLL-to-DLL path already uses (architecturally correct, but means routing the main image through more of `LdrLoadDll`/`process_init` than Phase-0 currently does), or (b) a from-scratch kernel-side i386 import binder — `pemod.c`'s EXISTING `do_imports` code is the WRONG tool for this (it uses 64-bit `IMAGE_THUNK_DATA64` reads/writes, correct only for PE32+/ARM64 DLL-to-DLL imports, not PE32/i386's 32-bit thunks; and it resolves names via `zc_resolve()`, the Atom-3A kernel-side native-stub resolver, not real `KERNELBASE.dll` export addresses) — genuinely next-atom work, out of this atom's time-box.

**Verdict vs this atom's own task premise**: NOT the C4-40 alignment/backpatch gap (disproven by live `dfsc`), NOT a routing/delivery bug (the AV IS correctly synthesized and delivered), NOT the same C4-30/31 `exe_entry`-not-executable gap either (that was about the MAIN image's own code page; this is about an UNBOUND IMPORT slot) — a fourth, distinct member of the broad "NoExecOp"-signature family, this one specifically an import-binding gap unique to `hello9_x86.exe`'s import table (the first boot EXE ever to have one). **Named, not chased — no fix attempted, per this atom's own time-box.**

**hello9 WriteFile marker: NOT reached, NOT the MILESTONE this atom.** Swap fully reverted (checkpoint `b26f7b2`, byte-identical `shell.c`/`build.sh` diff vs pre-C4-55/C4-56 canonical state, confirmed via `git diff`); `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` untracked, removed from tree. **Confirming `pe10 fex` re-run post-revert** (`scratch/c4-56_pe10_fex_restored_receipt.log`): `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → `pe10 ret=42` — **STILL HOLDS**, no regression. **Gates** (`scratch/c4-56_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` PASS (`ret=78`; one transient drain-timing flake on the first run, reproduced clean on immediate rerun, not a regression), `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`), `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7. **Commits**: source-only checkpoints, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{shell.c,build.sh}` — `e5f3d38` (re-apply hello9 swap, pre-boot), `b26f7b2` (revert to canonical hello8 wiring, post-receipt); `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` stay untracked (rebuildable via `chalice/mkpe32.sh`). Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-55 — **`ret=42` ACHIEVED**: named + fixed the C4-27 stub's one-stack-slot arg shift (FEX's `args_ptr=poppedReturnRSP+4` convention assumes a real ntdll `Nt*` stub's extra external-caller-retaddr slot, which a `ret`-reached synthetic stub never had); one dummy `push 0` restores it. `pe10 fex`: `PeMain ret` → C4-27 BOP stub → real `NtTerminateProcess(-1,42)` → `pe10 ret=42`, crash-free end-to-end, first time ever.

**Measurement, decisive, no guessing**: instrumented `NtTerminateProcess` (`ntsvc.c`) with an unconditional receipt: raw `ProcessHandle`/`ExitStatus` (the two marshaled syscall args as our C function actually received them) plus, from the live `WOW64_CPURESERVED` memory block (`nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg`, the same struct C4-25/45 already read), guest `Eax`/`Eip`/`Esp` and a 16-byte guest-stack-window dump at that live `Esp`. Pre-fix `pe10 fex` boot (`scratch/c4-55_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, line 8682-84):
```
CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0x000000000000002A ExitStatus=0x0000000000000000
CHALICE C4-55: live guest Eax=0x0 Eip=0x47661000 Esp=0x488CC000
CHALICE C4-55 GUEST STACK @Esp..+16: 0x2A 0x0 0x0 0x0
```
`0x2A` = 42 decimal. The stub's OWN two real pushed values (`-1`=ProcessHandle, `42`(from PeMain's `ret`)=ExitStatus) arrived SWAPPED-AND-SHIFTED: `ProcessHandle` read out as `42` (the value the stub intended for `ExitStatus`), and `ExitStatus` read out as `0` (stale/never-written memory one slot further than either real push landed). Not corruption, not a missed call, not FEX-internal noise — a clean, fully deterministic ONE 4-BYTE STACK SLOT arg-marshal shift, and the `live guest Eip=0x47661000/Esp=0x488CC000` (matching `exe_entry`'s own original boot-seeded values byte-for-byte) independently confirms this floor's `WOW64_CPURESERVED` context-stash gets overwritten back to a bookkeeping default by an UNRELATED FEX-internal `ThreadWow64Context` reset shortly before this call — a red herring C4-54 had flagged as its leading (and, per this atom, WRONG) theory for the exit-code mismatch; the live GUEST STACK dump at that (stale) `Esp` coincidentally also shows `0x2A` in its first word, an artifact of that same stale-context read, NOT the true call-time stack (the true call-time stack, only reachable via the marshaled register args themselves since FEX overwrites the `CPURESERVED` save area before our C function ever runs, is exactly what `ProcessHandle`/`ExitStatus` already report).

**Root-cause arithmetic (the stub's own donor comment, `ntsvc.c` C4-27 header block, quoted verbatim: "FEX derives its `args` pointer as (poppedReturnRSP)+4, exactly the address a genuine `mov eax,id; lea edx,[esp+4]; call [Wow64Transition]` ntdll stub would compute BEFORE its own internal `call` pushes a return address")**: a REAL ntdll `Nt*` stub is always entered via a normal `call` FROM SOME EXTERNAL CALLER — so at its own entry, `[esp+0]` already holds a return address (to that external caller), `[esp+4]`=arg0, `[esp+8]`=arg1, ALL THREE placed there before the stub's own code runs one instruction. The stub's `lea edx,[esp+4]` (computed at entry, before its OWN `call [Wow64Transition]` pushes a 4th slot) points at arg0's true location, and FEX's `poppedReturnRSP+4` formula (recomputing the SAME address purely off `ESP` at the BOP trap, without trusting EDX) needs, and gets, exactly 2 return-address-shaped slots above the args at trap time: (1) the stub's own `call [Wow64Transition]` retaddr, (2) the EXTERNAL caller's retaddr into the stub. This floor's C4-27 stub has NO external caller — it's the literal, synthetic address PeMain's own `ret` pops, reached via `ret`, not `call` — so slot (2) never existed; the stub only ever supplied its 2 pushed args + slot (1) (its own `call BOP`'s retaddr) = 3 slots total where FEX's formula needs 4, silently reading `args[0]` from where the stub's OWN `ExitStatus` push actually landed (one slot too shallow) and `args[1]` from one slot further out (whatever stale memory happened to sit there — measured as `0`).

**Fix, one line, source-only** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c`, `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready`): inserted a single dummy `push 0` (bytes `6A 00`, 2 bytes) between the stub's existing `push -1` (ProcessHandle) and `mov eax,0x2C; call BOP`, giving the stub the exact 4-slot shape FEX's formula expects — the pushed value itself is a pure placeholder (analog of "the external caller's return address", never read by anything downstream since this synthetic call chain never returns). New stub layout or bytes: `50 6A FF 6A 00 B8 2C 00 00 00 E8 <rel32>` = 15 bytes total (was 13), unchanged 16-byte `nt_territory_alloc(16)` buffer still fits it with 1 byte to spare.

**Confirming `pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-55_pe10_fex_fixed_receipt_full.log`, line 8684/8688):
```
CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0x00000000FFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x000000000000002A
...
pe10 ret=42
```
Both args now exactly correct: `ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF`(=-1, the stub's own pushed pseudo-handle) and `ExitStatus=0x2A`(=42, `hello8_x86.c`'s `PeMain() { return 42; }`, genuinely carried through the entire `PeMain ret → stub → BOP → real syscall` chain for the first time). **`pe10 ret=42`** — the done-line chain this whole C4-1..55 thread has been building toward is closed, crash-free, end-to-end.

**hello9_x86 swap attempted, reverted (new, unrelated, deeper frontier — not chased, new-fault law)**: built `hello9_x86.exe` (`mkpe32.sh` already supported it; binary just hadn't been built/wired yet) and temporarily repointed `pe10`'s `nt_boot_process` call (`shell.c`) + disk-image mapping (`build.sh`) at it instead of `hello8_x86.exe`. Result (`scratch/c4-55_hello9_swap_attempt_receipt.log`): a NEW, DIFFERENT crash — `CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` (alignment fault, `esr=0x96000006`) at `elr=0x26D0C02B0` inside a `reserve-commit-arena` region (a DIFFERENT address from any prior-atom fault), `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`. hello9's `PeMain` calls REAL `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile` through the genuine kernel32/kernelbase import-thunk machinery (a materially larger, separate surface than the C4-27 stub this atom fixed) — unrelated to this atom's own fix, not chased (task's own "swap if trivial" condition not met). Fully reverted: `shell.c`'s `pe10` verb boots `hello8_x86.exe` again (byte-identical to pre-atom), the temporary disk-mapping addition and built `hello9_x86.exe` binary removed — zero net diff against C4-54 other than the intended one-line `ntsvc.c` stub fix.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-55_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` — (1) the C4-55 diagnostic receipt (checkpoint before first boot), (2) the one-line stub arg-shift fix (checkpoint before the confirming boot). The hello9 swap detour (`shell.c`/`build.sh`) was applied then fully reverted in-tree (temporary, never left as a surviving diff). Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-54 — the C4-27 syscall-hinge stub page registered with `InvalidationTracker` (retroactive `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` call, same function C4-53 wired) + `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` wired to notify on every real guest EXEC-protect allocation; the C4-53-named `+0x488D0000` NoExecOp fault is CONFIRMED GONE (byte-identical transcript diff up to the exact point it used to fire); process now exits cleanly but `ExitStatus=0` not `42` — new, unpinned frontier, not `ret=42`, not chased.

**Recap of the fault this atom targeted** (named by C4-53, not fixed there): `hello8_x86.exe`'s `PeMain` fully executes (`return 42;`) and its own `ret` lands, byte-exact, in this floor's own `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready` (`ntsvc.c`) buffer — a `push eax; push -1; mov eax,0x2C; call rel32->BOP` stub written into a page allocated by `nt_territory_alloc(16)` (`ntboot.c`), NOT via any guest-visible `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` call. That page crossed neither of the two syscalls C4-46 (`NtMapViewOfSection`) and C4-53 (`NtProtectVirtualMemory`) had already wired into FEX's notify chain, so `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` never learned the range was executable — FEX's frontend decoder refused to fetch from it (`NoExecOp` self-fault), delivered as a genuine guest `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` at `addr=0x488D0000`, second-chance unhandled, `pe10 ret=3221225477`.

**Donor-gap classification (this atom's own task framing, taken at face value)**: treated as the THIRD instance of the C4-46/53 donor-notify gap, symmetric to "a real `VirtualAlloc(...,PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE)` never reaching `BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect`" — even though, precisely, this specific page is not a guest syscall allocation at all (see below); the fix still closes the identical STRUCTURAL gap (a page carrying an EXEC bit that never reaches `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification`/`XIntervals`).

**Fix, two parts, both source-only**:

1. **Retroactive one-shot stub-page registration** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, right at the `nt_wow64_ready = 1` set-site — the FIRST moment a REAL dynamically-mapped guest x86 image other than ntdll.dll is seen, which can only happen after `BTCpuProcessInit` has already run to completion, per C4-53's own already-proven ordering argument): fetches the stub's `(addr, 16)` range via a new getter, `nt_wow64_stub_range(unsigned long *addr_out, unsigned long *size_out)` (`ntsvc.c`/`ntsvc.h`), which returns valid (1) only once `nt_wow64_stub_written` is true — guaranteed true by this call site, since the stub is always written during `BTCpuProcessInit` itself (well before any subsequent dynamic map can run) — then calls the SAME `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect` function C4-53 already wired from `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, now promoted from `static` to external linkage (declared in `ntfile.h`) so both `ntfile.c`'s own retroactive call and `ntsvc.c`'s new general-purpose call (below) share the one implementation — `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification(this=fex_base+0x34c430, Address=stub_addr, Size=16, Prot=PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE=0x40)`, via the same `pe_enter6` bracket/`nt_wow64_ready` guard C4-53 established. No new guest-visible syscall exists to hook for this floor-internal buffer (it was never allocated via one) — a direct, one-shot registration at the earliest safe moment is the correct minimal analog.

2. **General `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` EXEC-protect notify** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c`), per this atom's own literal task text ("wire the executable-allocation notify ... from NtAllocateVirtualMemory ... when Protect has EXEC bits and wow64 guest"): `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` now captures the guest TEB as its own absolute first statement (`mov %0, x18`, same x18-clobber-safety idiom C4-48/53 established for `NtMapViewOfSection`/`NtProtectVirtualMemory` — every callee below, `nt_arena_*`/`nt_vm_alloc_pool`/etc, is plain `aarch64-elf` C, free to clobber x18 as scratch), then calls `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect((u64b)actual_base, actual_pages, Protect, teb)` at EVERY success return whose `Protect` carries an EXEC bit (`Protect & 0xF0` — the exact mask `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification`'s own disasm, C4-53, tests: bits 4-7, `PAGE_EXECUTE|_READ|_READWRITE|_WRITECOPY`) — covering, by actual code path: (a) commit-at-existing-arena-base, (b) Atom-3A tracked-region bookkeeping-only success, (c) unconstrained (`ZeroBits==0`) arena reserve+commit, (d) the `ZeroBits!=0` LOW-pool path (the SAME branch the BOP trampoline allocation itself takes — harmlessly no-ops there via the shared `nt_wow64_ready` gate, since that allocation happens during `BTCpuProcessInit`, before `nt_wow64_ready` can be true). `base`/`size` in every call site are the ACTUAL post-allocation values (IRON — never the caller's `*BaseAddress` hint). `NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx` needed no separate wiring: confirmed (grep + read, not assumed) it unconditionally delegates to `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` (`ntsvc.c:~1127`, `long status = NtAllocateVirtualMemory(ProcessHandle, BaseAddress, effective_zero_bits, RegionSize, AllocationType, Protect);`).

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-54_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 8684 lines; excerpt `c4-54_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): the retroactive registration fires exactly as designed — `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification base=0x488D0000 size=0x10 prot=0x40` appears at line 4144 (right after `CHALICE C4-46: BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection name=syswow64/kernel32.dll ... rc=0x0`, i.e. exactly at the `nt_wow64_ready=1` set-site) and again at line 4948 (kernelbase.dll's own map — harmless re-insert, `nt_wow64_ready` already true). This boot's own pre-existing `XIntervals` bracket-list diagnostic print (unrelated instrumentation, already in the tree) confirms the range is live: `[0x488D0000,0x488D1000)` appears in that dump from line 4289 onward, present in every subsequent dump through the end of the transcript.

**Decisive comparison, byte-for-byte, against the immediately-prior C4-53 atom's own `scratch/c4-53_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`** (`diff` with all hex literals elided): the two transcripts are IDENTICAL, line for line, from the very start through line ~8676 — same syscall sequence, same 42 `CHALICE ARENA`/`CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` alignment-fault diagnostics (a separate, pre-existing, harmless class, unchanged count both runs), same `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` calls for kernelbase.dll's own loader save/restore idiom (C4-53's own finding, unaffected). The OLD transcript's line 8677 begins a NEW fault sequence (`CHALICE ARENA: esr=0x96000006 ... elr=0x26D0C02B0` → `CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` → `CHALICE EXCEPTION` → guest `NtSetInformationThread ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x488D0000` → `CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x488D0000` → `FirstChance=FALSE` → `pe10 ret=3221225477`) that the NEW transcript does NOT have at all — it runs straight through the equivalent point to a clean `CHALICE C4-54: NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=0x0` / `pe10 ret=0`, with ZERO additional fault of any kind. **This is the single point of divergence between the two runs, and it is exactly where the targeted fault used to fire** — decisive, not inferred, proof the `+0x488D0000` NoExecOp AV is gone.

**New frontier, genuinely distinct, NOT `ret=42`, NOT chased further this atom (time-box, new-fault law)**: the guest's `ThreadWow64Context` is reset multiple times across the whole run — early on, `<- eip=0x47661000` (`exe_entry`, hello8_x86.exe's own entry) is set, then almost immediately `<- eip=0x486DE0A0` (an address inside `libwow64fex.dll`, never disasm'd this atom — a FEX-internal marker, by pattern-match to every prior C4-18/21/24/26/29/30 leg); much later, near the very end, `<- eip=0x58040000` (the BOP trampoline address) fires immediately followed by ANOTHER `<- eip=0x47661000` (`exe_entry`, again) — i.e. the guest process appears to be re-entered/restarted from its own entry point more than once within a single `pe10` boot. This is the SAME pre-existing, already-repeatedly-named architectural gap (C4-18/21/24/26/29/30: FEX's own internal dispatcher/signal-return marker, meant to be absorbed by an FEX-internal short-circuit, instead routed through this floor's full synthetic NT SEH cycle) — never fixed, always deferred to Pascal — simply surviving further now that the notify-registration gap this atom targeted is closed, rather than crashing outright. A plausible, UNVERIFIED-this-atom explanation for `ExitStatus=0` instead of `42`: one of these mid-flight resets re-enters PeMain (or re-dispatches straight into the stub) with a register file that never re-establishes `Eax=42` before the stub's own `push eax` executes; equally plausible and equally unverified: the final `svc id=44` (`NtTerminateProcess`) call is not our stub's own explicit call at all, but a distinct internal FEX/wow64 teardown path (a native process-exit default, `ExitStatus=0`) firing once `BTCpuSimulate` returns for some unrelated reason. Distinguishing the two needs a NEW, targeted probe (e.g. a guest-`Eax`-at-`push`-time hexdump immediately before the final `id=44` dispatch, or a disasm of `libwow64fex.dll+0x...` at the `0x486DE0A0`-class reset-marker addresses) — genuinely next-atom work; this atom's own mandate (closing the C4-27 stub-page notify gap) is done and gate-proven. Per task's own instruction ("if ret=42 ... else if new fault ... document, STOP"), and since neither branch matches exactly (no crash, but not `ret=42` either): documented here as the new frontier, STOPPED. `hello9_x86` swap (the done-line's `WriteFile` milestone) explicitly gated on `ret=42` by this atom's own task text — NOT attempted, since that condition was not met.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-54_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntfile.c,ntfile.h,ntsvc.c,ntsvc.h}` — (1) the notify-wiring itself (stub-page retroactive registration + `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` EXEC-protect notify), checkpoint prior to first boot; (2) an added unconditional `CHALICE C4-54: NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=` diagnostic print, checkpoint prior to the confirming second boot (used to produce the `ExitStatus=0x0` receipt above). Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-53 — `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` disasm-named (the REAL protect-side remover/inserter for `XIntervals`) and wired; `+0x68BD0` fault GONE, `kernelbase_entry` proven ALIVE at fault time; C4-52's own remover identified; C4-52 experimental crutch removed; new, later, different fault named (C4-27 stub page never notify-registered).

**Disasm (real blob, `objdump -d` on `kernel-arm/chalice/stock/wow64-chain/libwow64fex.dll`, NOT the vendored source — vendored tree has no `BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect` at all, C4-50's own finding)**: export `BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect` at VMA `0x1800043e8`; real body `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` at VMA `0x1801696d4`. Traced end-to-end this atom:
```
HandleMemoryProtectionNotification(this, Address, Size, Prot):
  AlignedBase = Address & ~0xFFF
  AlignedEnd  = ((Address&0xFFF)+Size+0xFFF) & ~0xFFF + AlignedBase
  lock(this->SharedMutex @ this+0x30)
  if (Prot & 0xF0)                 // PAGE_EXECUTE|_READ|_READWRITE|_WRITECOPY (bits 4-7)
      XIntervals.Insert({AlignedBase, AlignedEnd})        // executable -> INSERT
  else
      // binary-search this->XIntervals (this+0x0); if an overlapping
      // interval is found: XIntervals.Remove(...) AND a second Remove()
      // on the this+0x18 SMC-disabled list too
  if (Prot & 0xC0) { LogMan::Msg(Warn, "...RWX..."); XIntervals.Insert(...) again (this+0x18 list) }
  unlock(SharedMutex)
  if (did-work) InvalidationTracker::InvalidateIntervalInternal(this, AlignedBase, AlignedSize)
```
`this` = `fex_base+0x34c430`, the SAME fixed `InvalidationTracker` global every prior atom (C4-33/49/50/51/52) has read/written — no new object, no "wrong tank" possibility (same proof class as C4-51's own verdict for the map-side query).

**Donor gap named, symmetric to C4-46**: this floor's own `NtProtectVirtualMemory` had ZERO call sites into ANY FEX notify function before this atom (grepped the whole tree pre-atom) — every guest `VirtualProtect` (including the loader's own reloc-patch reprotects) never reached `InvalidationTracker` at all. Fixed: wired a direct call to `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` (its own internal `this+0x30` lock/unlock pair is self-contained, safe to call directly — same precedent C4-52 established for `HandleImageMap`'s own `this+0x30` mutex; the OUTER wrapper's own mutex at `0x18034c398` is a SEPARATE lock we do not need and deliberately do not touch, avoiding the wrapper's own unresolved two-phase lock-branch protocol that could otherwise deadlock `libwow64fex.dll`'s global if called with a guessed flag combo).

**Regression + fix (`nt_wow64_ready` guard)**: a prior lane's first wiring attempt called `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` unconditionally from every `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, including calls that happen VERY early in boot — native ARM64 module loads (`wow64.dll`/`libwow64fex.dll`/`wow64win.dll`/`win32u.dll`) each trigger several `NtProtectVirtualMemory` calls (relocation/import-table reprotects) BEFORE `BTCpuProcessInit` has run, i.e. before `InvalidationTracker`'s C++ constructor (`FEX-MAP.md` C4-32) has executed — calling into its body that early dereferences/locks an uninitialized `shared_mutex`, faulting (regression, confirmed live). Fix: `nt_wow64_ready`, set the first time `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` fires for a REAL guest x86 (Machine `0x14C`) image other than `ntdll.dll` — provably only reachable after `BTCpuProcessInit` (the guest must already be running under FEX via `BTCpuSimulate` to have issued the `LdrLoadDll` that leads there), so `InvalidationTracker` is guaranteed constructed by then. `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect` returns immediately if `!nt_wow64_ready`.

**`OldProtect` correctness fix**: `NtProtectVirtualMemory`'s `*OldProtect` was hardcoded `0x04` (`PAGE_READWRITE`) regardless of the region's true protection — a real, floor-caused bug (not just a missing-notify gap). Guest PE loaders universally use `VirtualProtect(RW)` (save) → patch relocations → `VirtualProtect(OldProtect)` (restore). Live-confirmed this atom (receipt below) that kernelbase.dll's loader does exactly this for its own `.text` section (`base=0x5E1C1000 size=0x69000`): the save call (`prot=0x04`) correctly REMOVES the exec range via the new notify wiring, but because the OLD hardcode always answered `OldProtect=0x04`, the restore call ALSO requested `PAGE_READWRITE` — never re-inserting the range. Fixed: `OldProtect` now derives from the image registry (same membership check `NtQueryVirtualMemory`'s `AllocProtect` answer already uses, FEX-MAP.md C4-32 Measure 2) — inside a registered image: `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE` (`0x40`); outside (plain arena/heap): unchanged `PAGE_READWRITE` (`0x04`). Live-confirmed: the restore call for the SAME kernelbase `.text` range now fires with `prot=0x40`, correctly re-inserting.

**C4-52's remover, named**: C4-52 observed its own single experimental `HandleImageMap` insert vanish again before the guest ever executed there, and left the mechanism unnamed (leading theory: an internal SMC/write-detection invalidation on the ensuing reloc-patch WRITE). This atom's live receipt shows the EXACT SAME kernelbase `.text` range (`base=0x5E1C1000 size=0x69000`) going through this identical save(`prot=0x04`, REMOVE)/restore(`prot=0x40`, INSERT) cycle via the guest loader's own idiom — the SAME mechanism, now donor-correctly modeled end-to-end. (C4-52's own world had zero protect-notify call sites at all, so its specific vanishing-insert was a coincidental adjacent effect of the same loader activity, most likely the SMC-write path it already suspected — moot now: the donor-correct contract removes any need to chase that further, since kernelbase's range now survives correctly through the WHOLE sequence, proven alive at fault time below.)

**Live receipt** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-53_pe10_fex_receipt{,_full}.log`, post-crutch-removal reboot): zero occurrences of `68BD0` in the whole transcript (old fault GONE); repeated `CHALICE C4-49 exe_entry(0x47661000) enclosed=1 kernelbase_entry(0x5E228BD0) enclosed=1` through to the new fault (kernelbase range ALIVE at fault time) — both goal conditions proven simultaneously. `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` fires ~13 times on native ARM64 modules pre-`nt_wow64_ready` (correctly skipped, no crash) then correctly on kernelbase's `.text` save/restore pair as described above.

**New fault, named not chased (new-fault law)**: `hello8_x86.exe`'s `PeMain` runs to completion for the first time ever and its own `ret` correctly lands in this floor's C4-27 syscall-hinge stub (`NtSetInformationThread ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x488D0000`, exact match to C4-27's own design). The stub's first instruction then faults: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x488D0000`, second-chance unhandled, `pe10 ret=3221225477`. Root cause (stub bytes decoded from the receipt's own hexdump — `push eax; push -1; mov eax,0x2C; call rel32→BOP`, byte-exact match to `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready`, `ntsvc.c`): this stub's code page is allocated via `NtAllocateVirtualMemory(...,PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE)` (`ntboot.c`) and never subsequently `VirtualProtect`'d — it crosses NEITHER of the two syscalls now wired to FEX's notify chain (`NtMapViewOfSection`/C4-46, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`/C4-53), so `XIntervals` never gains it. A THIRD instance of the identical donor-notify-gap class — `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` itself has no notify call site either. Out of scope this atom (time-box, new-fault law), not fixed.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-53_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`, same post-crutch-removal build): `pe8_gate.py` PASS (`pe8 ret=78`) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7.

**Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` — protect-notify wiring + `nt_wow64_ready` guard + `OldProtect` fix, then removal of the C4-52 experimental direct-call crutch, commit `0f6a7cf`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-52 — Decisive experiment: called `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` DIRECTLY (bypassing `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s own wrapper), disasm-verified signature/mutex against the REAL blob, via `pe_enter6`, for kernelbase.dll only, immediately after the real notify. **Result: no fault, clean return, `XIntervals` count 8→9 immediately after — the WRAPPER is the swallower, not `Insert`/`HandleImageMap` (C4-51's "silently-caught exception" theory REFUTED). New finding: the inserted range is REMOVED again before the guest ever executes there (count back to 8 at the later self-fault dump, same buffer, `end` shrunk by exactly one `Interval`) — a second, later, unnamed invalidation is the real remaining blocker.**

**Signature/ABI, verified against the REAL blob this atom** (`objdump -d kernel-arm/chalice/stock/wow64-chain/libwow64fex.dll`): `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` VMA `0x180169b10` (C4-50's quoted COFF-symtab RVA `0x168b10` is the raw symtab value; `VMA=ImageBase+0x1000+symtab=0x180001000+0x168b10=0x180169b10`, same address, section-relative RVA `0x169b10`). Prologue: `mov x20,x0`(`this`); `mov x0,x3; mov x19,x3`(`Address`, 4th arg); `stp x1,x2,[sp]`(name `{ptr,len}`) → confirms `HandleImageMap(this=x0,name_ptr=x1,name_len=x2,Address=x3)`, matching the vendored source's own 4-arg signature exactly. **Mutex** (task's own pre-flight requirement): the ONLY lock `HandleImageMap` itself takes is a `shared_mutex` at `this+0x30` (loop's lock/unlock call sites both compute `x23=x20(this)+0x30`, pass to `__shared_mutex_base::lock/unlock`), acquired/released INTERNALLY per qualifying section — no external pre-lock needed. Distinct from `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s own OUTER mutex at `0x18034c398` (unrelated, guards the whole notify call).

**Experiment** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, fires once, kernelbase.dll only, right after the real notify's own call completes): `this=fex_base+0x34c430` (the SAME fixed global C4-49/50/51's dumper reads, C4-51-proven the ONLY container the exec-check consults), `fn=fex_base+0x169b10`, `Address=base` (`0x5E1C0000`), throwaway `"kernelbase.dll"` name (per C4-49's own source read, `Name` only used for an irrelevant `MonoHacks` compare in this function — safe). Called via `pe_enter6(fn,teb,this,name_ptr,name_len,Address,0,0)` — same shim (C4-48) the real notify path already uses, 4 real args in `a0..a3`.

**Live receipt** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-52_pe10_fex_receipt{,_full}.log`): baseline `count=8` unchanged through kernel32.dll's own wrapper-routed notify (lines 4299/4337, AFTER kernel32's notify — confirms wrapper-driven insert never persisted there either). kernelbase.dll's wrapper-routed notify runs (line 4946, `rc=0x0`); our OWN direct call runs immediately next (line 4947), its post-call dump (line 4949) shows `count=9` — exactly ONE net insert since baseline-8, meaning **the wrapper's own two calls contributed ZERO inserts, our bypass call contributed exactly ONE** — decisive proof the wrapper swallows the insert, not `HandleImageMap`/`Insert` itself. Later, the self-fault dump (line 6179, ~1230 lines afterward) shows `count=8` again, SAME buffer address (`begin` unchanged, no reallocation), `end` shrunk back by exactly one `Interval` (16B) — a genuine single-element removal, not a full reset (all 8 baseline ranges unchanged, same order). Fault unchanged: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x5E228BD0 (kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0)`, `pe10 ret=3221225477` — NOT `ret=42`, no regression.

**Sharpened next leg (not chased, time-box)**: something between line 4949 and line 6179 explicitly removes the ONE newly-inserted kernelbase interval — most likely `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::InvalidateContainingSection(Address,bool)` (sibling function immediately following `HandleImageMap` in the disasm, VMA `0x180169d74`, NOT disassembled this atom), plausibly a genuine SMC (self-modifying-code) write-detection hook firing on an ordinary write inside kernelbase.dll's own image (TLS/reloc/IAT patching during its own init) between our notify-time insert and the guest's jump into `DllMainCRTStartup` — this floor has no page-level write-protection trap, so if true, FEX's own frontend/JIT must call this directly, not via a hardware fault. Needs: disasm of `InvalidateContainingSection` for its full body/callers, plus either an intervening diagnostic dump (to narrow the exact removal instant) or a breakpoint-style hook on its entry.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-52_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` PASS (`pe8 ret=78`) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7.

**Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` (`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`: C4-52 direct `HandleImageMap` experimental call + immediate post-call `XIntervals` dump, gated once, kernelbase.dll only, kept as diagnostic — no persisting fix reached, not removed), commit `abe9bcb`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-51 — MONAD REFRAME REFUTED by full blob disasm: the decode-time exec-check queries ONLY `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` (our dumper's own target, proven correct-and-only structure) -- `ImageTracker` is NEVER consulted by the runtime check at all, its container is a wholly separate AOT-code-cache subsystem. Additionally, disasm+live-read REFUTED C4-50's remaining candidates (ii) EXECUTE-filter and (iii) object-identity mismatch -- kernelbase.dll's real `.text` section IS executable+nonempty and DOES enclose the fault address, and the SAME global `InvalidationTracker` instance is used by both the insert call site and the query call site. No fix (nothing floor-fixable found); the true stopping mechanism is now narrowed to ONE candidate: a silently-caught C++ exception inside the prebuilt blob's own notify wrapper, swallowed before `IntervalList::Insert` ever runs, with the wrapper's hardcoded `rc=0` masking the failure.

**Q1 answer -- `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap` (RVA 0x165710, VMA 0x180166710), full body disassembled (`objdump -d`, ~280 real instructions read end-to-end, not sampled)**: inserts into its OWN per-instance data (a `shared_mutex`-guarded map at `this+0x70`, lock at `this+0x50` -- CONFIRMED via `add x24,x20,#0x50` / `bl __shared_mutex_base::lock` / `add x0,x20,#0x70` immediately preceding the map-populate call at the function's tail, `this` = the SEPARATE global `ImageBase+0x34c4b0` object, 0x80 bytes after `InvalidationTracker`'s own `+0x430` -- two genuinely distinct global singletons, byte-verified via each one's own `adrp 0x18034c000; add x0,x0,#0x430` (InvalidationTracker) vs `add x0,x0,#0x4b0` (ImageTracker) call-site immediates in `BTCpuProcessInit`'s internal wrapper, RVA `0x28b8`, lines `0x1800029c8`/`0x1800029f4`). The function's ONLY real conditional GATE is `this+0xb8==1` (a per-instance bool, almost certainly the `EnableAOTIR`/code-cache-enabled feature flag -- gates only whether `.reloc` (`IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_BASERELOC`, index 5, confirmed via `mov w2,#0x5; bl RtlImageDirectoryEntryToData`) is parsed into a `GuestRelocationType` hashmap (`ankerl::unordered_dense`, confirmed via the exact mangled callee name) for later AOT-cache relocation validation) -- BOTH branches (gate taken or not) converge on the SAME map-populate call (`add x24,x20,#0x50` at `0x180166c0c`, reached from EITHER path), meaning `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap` DOES run to completion and DOES insert a `MappedImageInfo`-shaped entry for kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll regardless of the AOT-feature flag -- no early exit specific to these two DLLs was found. This container/insert is entirely orthogonal to the executable-range gate (Q2) -- it exists purely to let `ImageTracker::LookupExecutableFileSection`/`OpenCodeMapFile` (AOT code-cache readback, unrelated subsystem) find a previously-mapped image's on-disk code-cache file later, by name.

**Q2 answer -- the REAL decode-time query chain, disassembled instruction-by-instruction, not inferred**: `FEXCore::Frontend::Decoder::CheckRangeExecutable(Address,Size)` (RVA `0x1be90`, VMA `0x18001ce90`) FIRST checks a per-Decoder CACHE (`Decoder+0x68`=`ExecutableRangeBase`, `Decoder+0x70`=`ExecutableRangeEnd` -- the SAME fields `kmain.c`'s own C4-34 diagnostic already reads) for a fast-path hit; on a cache MISS, it makes exactly ONE virtual call: `obj = *(*(Decoder+0x18)+0x108); vtable=*obj; fn=vtable[0x38/8=7]; fn(obj, Decoder+0x10-field, Address, &sret_pair)`. Traced the callee: this vtable slot resolves to `WowSyscallHandler::QueryGuestExecutableRange(InternalThreadState*, Address)` (RVA `0x43f8`), whose ENTIRE body (5 real instructions, `objdump -d` confirms, no other code) is `mov x1,x2 /*discard ThreadState arg, x1<-Address*/; adrp x0,0x18034c000; add x0,x0,#0x430 /*x0 = FIXED GLOBAL InvalidationTracker instance*/; b InvalidationTracker::QueryExecutableRange` -- a literal, unconditional TAIL-CALL trampoline, byte-verified, into `InvalidationTracker::QueryExecutableRange(Address)` (RVA `0x169910`), which does a binary search FIRST in `this+0x0` (the main `XIntervals` vector -- confirmed via `ldp x9,x8,[x19]` at function entry, the SAME `begin/end` pair layout `kmain.c`'s dumper already reads) and, on a miss, a SECOND binary search in `this+0x18` (a separate `DisableSMCDetection`-tagged interval list, confirmed via the SIBLING function `InvalidationTracker::DisableSMCDetectionEv`'s own `[this+0x18]` access, immediately following in the disasm) -- returning a flag byte distinguishing which list satisfied the query. **`ImageTracker` is referenced NOWHERE in this entire call chain** (`WowSyscallHandler::QueryGuestExecutableRange`'s own disasm is 5 instructions total, no `ImageTracker`-class vtable/symbol anywhere near it) -- the MONAD REFRAME's fear ("we may be gauging the wrong tank") is definitively, disasm-provenly WRONG: the executable-range gate consults `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` ONLY, the EXACT global object (`ImageBase+0x34c430`) our own C4-33/49/50 dumper has been reading all along. `ImageTracker`'s container (Q1, above) plays NO role whatsoever in this decision.

**Q3 answer -- live dump of the ACTUALLY-queried container, this atom's own `pe10 fex` re-run (`scratch/c4-51_pe10_fex_receipt.log`)**: `CHALICE C4-49 XIntervals ivt=0x4DD8C430 ... count=8`, the SAME 8 ranges as every prior atom, byte-identical; `kernelbase_entry(0x5E228BD0) enclosed=0x0` -- **CONFIRMED, again, on the exact right structure**: kernelbase.dll's range is genuinely absent from the ONE AND ONLY container the exec-check reads (no "wrong tank" possibility remains -- Q2 already proved there is only one tank, and Q3 confirms it's empty of this range at fault time).

**New this atom -- candidates (ii) and (iii) from C4-50, BOTH refuted with hard evidence, sharpening the open gap to one remaining candidate**: (iii) object-identity mismatch -- REFUTED: `BTCpuProcessInit`'s wrapper (RVA `0x28b8`) calls `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` with `this=ImageBase+0x34c430` (`adrp 0x18034c000; add x0,x0,#0x430`, line `0x1800029c8-cc`), the IDENTICAL immediate `QueryExecutableRange`'s own tail-call trampoline uses -- same global object, byte-for-byte, no aliasing possible. (ii) EXECUTE-filter exclusion -- REFUTED: extended `kmain.c`'s C4-50 diagnostic (this atom's only code change, still diagnostic-only) to walk kernelbase.dll's REAL, currently-mapped section table (`SizeOfOptionalHeader`@`nth+0x14`, first section = `nth+0x18+SizeOfOptionalHeader`, each `IMAGE_SECTION_HEADER` read at `+0x8`(VirtualSize)/`+0xC`(VirtualAddress)/`+0x24`(Characteristics), same raw-deref idiom as the existing `NumberOfSections` check) -- live result (`c4-51_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): section[0] (`.text`) has `VA=0x1000 VSize=0x68F15 Characteristics=0x60000020` -- bit `0x20000000`(`IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE`) IS set, `VSize` is nonzero (`any_exec_nonempty_section=1`) -- and `SectionBase=Address+VA=0x5E1C1000`, `SectionEnd=SectionBase+VSize=0x5E229F15` PROVABLY ENCLOSES the actual fault address `0x5E228BD0` (falls inside `[0x5E1C1000,0x5E229F15)`). By `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap`'s own disassembled section-walk loop (traced instruction-by-instruction this atom, RVA `0x169b10`-`0xc10`: `NumberOfSections!=0` gate not taken -> loop walks each `IMAGE_SECTION_HEADER`, tests `Characteristics` byte-3 bit-5 (`=bit29=0x20000000=EXECUTE`) via `tbz`, and for the FIRST qualifying section unconditionally calls `IntervalList::Insert(this=x20, SectionBase=x25, SectionEnd=x26)` at RVA `0x169bc0`) -- this section, with these real live values, SHOULD trigger exactly that `Insert` call. `IntervalList::Insert` itself (RVA `0x5668c`, disassembled fully) only skips on `Base==End` (empty interval, not the case here: `0x5E1C1000 != 0x5E229F15`) and otherwise writes directly into `this+0x0` (`begin/end/cap`, standard libc++ vector layout, CONFIRMED as the exact fields `QueryExecutableRange` reads via `ldp x9,x8,[x19]` at its own entry) -- the SAME memory our dumper watches. **All three preconditions this atom checked (header gate, object identity, EXECUTE-filter) are satisfied for a real, live-observed, in-range section -- yet the insert STILL empirically never lands (this atom's own re-run: `count=8` unchanged, `enclosed=0`).**

**Sole remaining candidate for next atom (sharpened, not chased -- would require either instrumenting the blob's OWN unwind path, which this floor cannot recompile, or a creative host-side trap on the specific `bl` instruction)**: `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s own wrapper (RVA `0x3474`, C4-50's own disasm) hardcodes `rc=0` unconditionally regardless of what its two inner calls (`HandleImageMap` x2) actually do, AND both `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` and its own callees (`GetSectionFilePath`, `LogMan::Msg` on the RWX-warning branch, `FEXCore::Allocator::aligned_alloc`, `memmove`, the `unordered_dense` hashmap insert inside `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap`) are all real C++ code with their own exception-unwind landing pads (CONFIRMED present in disasm: `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap`'s own tail has TWO catch blocks, RVA `0x169d3c`/`0x169d5c`, each doing `unique_lock::~unique_lock()` then `_Unwind_Resume` -- i.e., a lock-guard RAII cleanup path that exists BECAUSE the compiler proved an exception CAN propagate through the locked region, e.g. from `IntervalList::Insert`'s own `std::vector::insert`/reallocation path, `FEXCore::Allocator::aligned_alloc` failing, or `LogMan::Msg`'s `fmt`-library formatting call throwing). If ANY exception fires partway through the section-walk loop (plausibly on an early section, before reaching whichever section would have inserted kernelbase's range) it unwinds straight out through `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s OWN catch-and-swallow (C4-50's own noted `__Unwind_Resume` at `0x1800044b4`), which still returns its hardcoded `rc=0` -- perfectly explaining a "successful", non-crashing notify call that nonetheless leaves `XIntervals` unchanged. NOT verifiable further without either (a) a live host-side single-step/watchpoint on the blob's own exception-throw/landing-pad instructions (needs new floor plumbing this atom does not have time to build), or (b) accepting this as the terminal, disasm-complete verdict for this call chain and moving to a workaround (e.g., have this floor's OWN `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` hook directly populate `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` itself via a raw memory write to the now-fully-understood vector layout at `ImageBase+0x34c430+0x0`, bypassing the blob's own insert path entirely -- floor-satisfiable, NOT attempted this atom per the task's own scope, a real candidate fix for next atom).

**No new fault, no regression, no fix applied this atom**: `pe10 fex` re-run (`scratch/c4-51_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, `c4-51_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 6196 lines) is BYTE-IDENTICAL to every prior atom's own fault (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, `ExceptionInformation[0]=0x8`(execute), `pe10 ret=3221225477`) -- this atom's `kmain.c` change is diagnostic-only (per-section live memory read, no control-flow/behavioral code touched).

## C4-50 — disassembled the REAL prebuilt `libwow64fex.dll` blob (objdump -t/-d, NOT vendored source) for the notify path; found a whole second, non-vendored `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap` handler proving the blob is a different/newer FEX revision than what's checked out; named the exact gating branch in `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` (`NumberOfSections==0` early-exit) and then, via a live in-memory read of both DLLs' real PE headers, EMPIRICALLY REFUTED that branch as the actual cause of the stall (both DLLs show nonzero `NumberOfSections=12`, gate provably not taken, yet `XIntervals` still never gains either range per C4-49). No fix applied — the true stopping mechanism remains open, now narrowly scoped to three candidates for the next atom.

**Toolchain note**: the Xcode-bundled `/usr/bin/objdump` (really the LLVM/Xcode toolchain's objdump) reads this PE32+/COFF-arm64 DLL directly (`file(1)`: "PE32+ executable (DLL) (GUI) Aarch64, for MS Windows") and dumps the FULL, non-stripped COFF symbol table (`objdump -t`, 7262 lines/symbols) plus disassembly (`objdump -d`) with demangled-looking (still-mangled, C++ Itanium ABI) symbol names as branch targets — no cross-toolchain (`aarch64-linux-gnu-objdump`/`llvm-objdump`) needed, this was already on `$PATH`. Symbol-table address column is a **section-relative RVA from image base** (`.text` VMA per `objdump -h` = `0x180001000`, itself already `ImageBase(0x180000000)+SectionRVA(0x1000)`) — i.e. `VMA = 0x180001000 + symtab_address` directly (NOT `-0x1000` first; verified by landing exactly on a function boundary, `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` symtab addr `0x3474` → VMA `0x180004474`, confirmed against the disassembly listing's own `<label>:` boundaries).

**(1) What `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` (VMA `0x180004474`) actually does, full disasm read**: 11 real instructions — `str x19,[sp,#-0x10]!` / `str x30,[sp,#8]` (prologue) → `adrp/add x0,#0x18034c398` (a global `std::mutex`) → `mov x19,x1` (`x19` = the fn's own `x1` arg = `Address`) → `bl mutex::lock` → `mov x0,x19; mov w1,wzr` → `bl 0x1800028b8` (internal helper, tail-embedded inside the SAME code chunk `BTCpuProcessInit`'s own body occupies, no separate export — this floor's `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` resolves the export `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` itself fine, this inner helper is simply never separately exported) → `bl mutex::unlock` → `mov w0,wzr; ret` (**rc=0 is a hardcoded hardcoded hardcoded constant, NOT a status derived from either handler's outcome** — the 9 live `rc=0x0` receipts across C4-46 through C4-49 prove nothing whatsoever about whether the handlers succeeded internally, only that they didn't throw past this wrapper's own catch/unwind cleanup path, also present in the disasm at `0x1800044b4` (`__Unwind_Resume` on exception)).

**(2) The internal helper (VMA `0x1800028b8`, args: `x0=Address`, `w1=0`/unused `MainImage` flag) — full disasm read**: calls `FEX::Windows::GetSectionFilePath(Address)` (RVA `0x5124`) first, gets back a `fextl::string` (ptr/len pair, SSO-aware — the `tst w8,#1`/`csinc`/`csel` sequences at `0x1800028e4`-`f8` are libc++'s short-string-optimization discriminant, not a gate); computes the basename via a backslash-rscan loop (`0x180002900`-`34`, matches `BaseName`'s donor semantics — scans backward for the LAST `\`, C4-49-consistent); then calls, IN THIS EXACT ORDER, on the SAME `(name, len, Address=x19)` triple: **first** `_ZN3FEX7Windows19InvalidationTracker14HandleImageMapENSt3__117basic_string_viewIcNS2_11char_traitsIcEEEEy` (RVA `0x168b10`, VMA `0x180169b10`) at call site `0x1800029d4` — this IS the vendored-source-matching one (class `InvalidationTracker`, 4-arg shape `(this,name_ptr,name_len,Address)` matching `Common/InvalidationTracker.cpp`'s signature exactly); **second**, immediately after, `_ZN3FEX7Windows12ImageTracker14HandleImageMapENSt3__117basic_string_viewIcNS2_11char_traitsIcEEEEyb` (RVA `0x165710`, VMA `0x180166710`) at call site `0x180002a08` — **5-arg shape, `(this,name_ptr,name_len,Address,MainImage:bool)`, belonging to class `FEX::Windows::ImageTracker`** (own ctor `_ZN3FEX7Windows12ImageTrackerC1ERN7FEXCore7Context7ContextEb` RVA `0x165490`, own vtable/RTTI `_ZTVN3FEX7Windows12ImageTrackerE`/`_ZTIN3FEX7Windows12ImageTrackerE`, own members `HandleImageUnmap`/`LookupExecutableFileSection`/`LoadAOTImages`/`OpenCodeMapFile`/`MappedImageInfo`/`AOTImageInfo` — an AOT-code-cache image-tracking subsystem). **`grep -rn "class ImageTracker\|ImageTracker::" FEX/Source` (vendored tree) returns ZERO hits** — this class, and this entire second handler call, DOES NOT EXIST in the vendored source this whole 49-atom thread has been citing. This is hard, symbol-table-level (not inferential) proof that `kernel-arm/chalice/stock/wow64-chain/libwow64fex.dll` was built from a materially different/newer FEX tree than the vendored `FEX/Source` checkout (commit `86ff33bbe`, near tag `FEX-2605`) — C4-49's own suspicion, now confirmed by direct symbol evidence rather than a single mispredicted branch.

**(3) The gate, read directly off `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap`'s OWN disasm (VMA `0x180169b10`)**: prologue saves `x19-x29`; `mov x20,x0` (`this`), `mov x0,x3; mov x19,x3` (`x0`/`x19` = `Address`, the fn's 4th/`y`-typed arg), `stp x1,x2,[sp]` (save name ptr/len) → `bl RtlImageNtHeader` (VMA `0x1801b8a80` — itself a bare `adrp x16,...; ldr x16,[x16,#0xad0]; br x16` IAT-thunk, i.e. `RtlImageNtHeader` is an IMPORT from the native ARM64 `ntdll.dll`, NOT implemented inside `libwow64fex.dll` at all — its own internal logic is UNEXAMINED this atom) → `ldrh w8,[x0,#0x6]` (`IMAGE_NT_HEADERS.FileHeader.NumberOfSections`, standard PE offset: `Signature`(4)@0 + `Machine`(2)@4 + `NumberOfSections`(2)@6 — confirmed, not assumed) → `cbz x8,0x180169c14` (**if `NumberOfSections==0`, skip straight to the function's tail** — `mov x27,xzr` then falls into the SAME epilogue path the main loop's own exit uses, i.e. returns having never called `IntervalList::Insert`). Traced the ENTIRE function body instruction-by-instruction this atom (not sampled): the section-walk loop (`0x180169b7c`-`c10`) that follows contains the ONLY two `IntervalList::Insert` call sites in this function (`0x180169bc0` unconditional-per-executable-section, `0x180169c0c` a second conditional insert inside an `if` — both gated on reaching the loop body at all, which requires `NumberOfSections!=0` from the `cbz` above); each per-section iteration ALSO separately tests `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE` (`ldrb w8,[x22,#3]; tbz w8,#5,<next-section>` — byte 3 of the 4-byte `Characteristics` DWORD is bits 24-31, bit 5 of that byte = bit 29 = `0x20000000` = `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE`, donor-correct) before inserting — a second, per-section filter, confirmed but NOT yet live-checked against kernel32/kernelbase's actual section Characteristics this atom. **This `NumberOfSections==0` check is THE, and the ONLY, function-level early-exit gate in this entire function** — no other skip condition of any kind exists in its ~130-instruction body.

**Live gate-value check** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c`, new diagnostic block, piggybacked on the same self-fault handler C4-49's `XIntervals` dumper already uses — fires once per DLL, gated on `nt_image_base_by_name` first resolving each name): reads `NumberOfSections` directly out of THIS BOOT'S actual, already-mapped `syswow64/kernel32.dll`/`syswow64/kernelbase.dll` PE headers, at `base+e_lfanew+6` (RAM is identity-mapped, matches the existing `root_key_names[]` spot-check idiom's own bounds-checked-raw-C-deref pattern — no new mechanism). **Result** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-50_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, transcript lines 4279/6140): `syswow64/kernel32.dll base=0x5D090000 ... pesig=0x00004550 machine=0x14C NumberOfSections=0xC -> GATE NOT TAKEN`; `syswow64/kernelbase.dll base=0x5E1C0000 ... pesig=0x00004550 machine=0x14C NumberOfSections=0xC -> GATE NOT TAKEN`. Both real, valid PE headers (`'PE\0\0'` signature intact, `machine=0x14C`=i386 matching `file(1)`'s own report on the on-disk `i386-windows/kernel32.dll`/`kernelbase.dll`), both with 12 sections — **unambiguously nonzero**, meaning `PeMapImage` (`pemod.c`) correctly copied the real header bytes to each DLL's mapped `base` (no header-corruption bug on this floor's OWN mapping side).

**Verdict: the `NumberOfSections==0` gate is REFUTED as the cause of the observed `XIntervals` stall.** By this branch's own logic (traced above), with `NumberOfSections=12` for both DLLs, `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` should fall straight into the section-walk/insert loop for both — yet C4-49's 9 live `XIntervals` dumps across this SAME run (re-confirmed, unchanged, this atom's own `pe10 fex` re-run: `count=8`, same 8 pre-existing ranges, neither `0x5D090000` nor `0x5E1C0000` present at any point) prove the insert never actually happens. Two facts, both now live-proven true simultaneously with no contradiction resolved: the one visible gate isn't taken, AND the insert still doesn't happen. **Three genuinely narrowed, un-eliminated candidates for the next atom** (none chased further this atom — time-box, each needs its own disasm/live-check leg): (i) `RtlImageNtHeader` (native ARM64 `ntdll.dll` import, NOT implemented in `libwow64fex.dll`, own logic UNEXAMINED) may not literally return a pointer AT `Address` the way this atom assumed — needs `objdump -t/-d` on `kernel-arm/chalice/stock/ntdll.dll` (the ARM64-native one, NOT the i386 guest copy) to find and disassemble `RtlImageNtHeader`'s real body; (ii) the per-section `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE` filter (confirmed to exist, not yet live-checked) may filter out every section of these two SPECIFIC DLLs for a reason not yet inspected — needs a live dump of each DLL's actual `IMAGE_SECTION_HEADER.Characteristics` array (both mapped images are already sitting in identity-mapped RAM, same read technique as the `NumberOfSections` check above, section table located via `nth+opthdr_size` per `pemod.c`'s own parse); (iii) the `shared_mutex`-guarded `IntervalList::Insert` calls (`this+0x30` for the main tracker, `this+0x18` for the second conditional insert) may write into an interval-list object that ISN'T the SAME global `InvalidationTracker.XIntervals` instance C4-49's dumper reads (object-identity/aliasing not yet checked — `this` here is `x20`, the fn's own first arg, itself passed down from `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s internal helper as a FIXED/derived pointer not yet traced back to its own origin this atom).

**No new fault, no regression, no fix applied**: `pe10 fex` re-run this atom is BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-49's own (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`) — same fault, sharper disasm-scoped understanding only. This atom's kmain.c change is diagnostic-only (a live memory read alongside the existing self-fault handler, no control-flow/behavioral code touched) — no floor-satisfiable fix was identified this atom (the actual stopping mechanism, per the verdict above, remains genuinely open pending legs (i)-(iii)).

## C4-49 — traced HandleImageMap end-to-end (vendored source: NO skip condition exists); fixed a real, necessary gap (`NtQueryVirtualMemory` MemorySectionName, class 2, was `STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS` unconditionally); empirically PROVED via a re-armed live `XIntervals` dumper that the fix, though it makes `GetSectionFilePath` succeed with the byte-correct real path for BOTH DLLs, does NOT make `XIntervals` gain either range — REFUTING the task's own leading hypotheses (both (i) empty-Name and (ii) MEM_PRIVATE-vs-MEM_IMAGE). `HandleImageMap` does not "decline" by any condition visible in source; the actual insert mechanism (`RtlImageNtHeader(Address)` + section walk, native ARM64 host code inside the prebuilt `libwow64fex.dll` blob) silently fails to take effect for real, and this is now the FIRST atom in this thread to catch the vendored-source-citation methodology itself producing a wrong prediction — needs blob disasm next, not more source reading.

**Measure 1 (FEX source, `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`), every early-return/skip condition enumerated**: `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` (`:1009-1013`) — `std::scoped_lock Lock(ThreadCreationMutex); HandleImageMap(reinterpret_cast<uint64_t>(Address)); return STATUS_SUCCESS;` — ZERO conditions, confirms C4-48's own citation exactly (only `Address` is read, no `AllocType`/`Prot`/`Unk1`/`Unk2` check of any kind). `HandleImageMap` itself (`:175-179`): `fextl::string ModulePath = FEX::Windows::GetSectionFilePath(Address); fextl::string ModuleName = BaseName(ModulePath); InvalidationTracker->HandleImageMap(ModuleName, Address); ImageTracker->HandleImageMap(ModulePath, Address, MainImage);` — no early return here either, `GetSectionFilePath` failing (returns `{}`) just yields empty `ModulePath`/`ModuleName`, passed onward unconditionally. `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` (`Common/InvalidationTracker.cpp:63-95`): `auto* Nt = RtlImageNtHeader(reinterpret_cast<HMODULE>(Address)); auto* SectionsBegin = IMAGE_FIRST_SECTION(Nt); ...for (Section...) if (Section->Characteristics & IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE) { ...XIntervals.Insert({SectionBase,SectionEnd}); ... }` — the ONLY use of `Name` anywhere in this function is a `MonoHacks`-config-gated string-equality special case (`Name=="mono-2.0-bdwgc.dll"`), entirely irrelevant to kernel32/kernelbase; the `XIntervals.Insert` itself depends SOLELY on `RtlImageNtHeader(Address)` parsing the real PE header bytes at `Address` plus each section's own `Characteristics`/`VirtualAddress`/`Misc.VirtualSize` fields — nothing here reads `Name`/`ModulePath`, nothing here calls `NtQueryVirtualMemory(MemoryBasicInformation)` (the class-0/Type-field theory) at all. `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap` (`Common/ImageTracker.cpp:127-179`) similarly re-derives `Nt` straight from `Address` (not `Path`) and only uses `Path`/`ModuleName` for its own AOT-code-cache bookkeeping (`FEXCore::Config::Get_ENABLECODECACHINGWIP()`, off by default) — not gating. **Conclusion, source-level**: no skip condition of any kind exists in this call chain, for either of the task's two named suspects — donor quote in C4-46/48 ("only Address is read") stands, sharpened: Address is *sufficient* per this source, GetSectionFilePath's outcome is cosmetic-only.

**Measure 2 (boot log, both this atom's runs) — the nested `NtQueryVirtualMemory` call, its class, and our floor's answer**: `grep`'d `scratch/c4-48_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log` for the notify call sites (`CHALICE C4-46: BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection ... rc=0x0`, lines 4103/4841) — immediately preceded (lines 4102/4840, `4106`/`4844` too) by `CHALICE: NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY base=<same addr> class=0x2 buf=... len=0x218`. `class=2` == `MemorySectionName`/`MemoryMappedFilenameInformation` (`wine/include/winternl.h:2423`, `winternl.h` `MEMORY_INFORMATION_CLASS` enum) — this IS `GetSectionFilePath`'s own call (`Common/Module.h:25-37`, `NtQueryVirtualMemory(NtCurrentProcess(), Address, MemoryMappedFilenameInformation, &Buffer, sizeof(Buffer), NULL)`, `sizeof(Buffer)=sizeof(MEMORY_SECTION_NAME)(16B)+array<WCHAR,PATH_MAX>(260*2=520B)=0x218` — byte-exact length match, not a coincidence). This floor's own `NtQueryVirtualMemory` (`ntfile.c`, pre-this-atom): `if (MemoryInformationClass != 0 /* MemoryBasicInformation */) return STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS;` — an UNCONDITIONAL reject for every class except 0, confirmed by direct source read (not by absence of a print — the function's own `nt_dispatch_log_enabled` ENTRY log fires for every call INCLUDING this one, but there is no further diagnostic line after it, unlike class-0 calls which always get a second `CHALICE: NtQueryVirtualMemory addr=... branch=...` line — that line's absence for every one of the four `class=0x2` ENTRY lines in the C4-48 receipt is the log-level confirmation of the early return). **This is the ONE real, concrete gap this floor has**: `GetSectionFilePath` ALWAYS got `STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS` (non-zero) back, ALWAYS returned `{}` (empty path), for every module ever mapped, this entire 48-atom thread. The task's suspected class-0/`MemoryBasicInformation` Type-field theory ("our NtQueryVirtualMemory likely reports Type=MEM_PRIVATE for blob-backed images") does **not** apply to this exact call chain (`HandleImageMap`/`GetSectionFilePath` never call class 0 at all) — and is moot regardless, since this floor's own class-0 handler already correctly answers `MEM_IMAGE` for any address inside a registered image (`ntfile.c`: `if (img) { ...wr32(out+MBI_TYPE_OFF, MEM_IMAGE); }`), confirmed by direct code read, not a live gap.

**Fix (`ntfile.c`, `NtQueryVirtualMemory`)**: implemented class 2 (`MemorySectionName`) donor-exact (`wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:6079-6104 get_memory_section_name`): looks up `nt_image_find_containing(addr)` (this floor's own image registry, already the source of truth for class-0's `MEM_IMAGE` answer), synthesizes a real NT-style path (`"C:\windows\"` + the registry's own registered name with `/`→`\`, e.g. `"C:\windows\syswow64\kernelbase.dll"`) so `FEX::Windows::BaseName` (`Common/Module.h:39-41`, splits on the LAST `\`) still extracts the correct real basename, writes the native (64-bit host, `libwow64fex.dll` is itself a native ARM64 PE) `UNICODE_STRING` shape (`Length`@0/`MaximumLength`@2/`Buffer`@8, 16 bytes total, `wine/include/winternl.h:65-69`) with `Buffer` pointing at the caller's OWN buffer tail (`MemoryInformation+16`, donor-exact: real Wine writes `info->SectionFileName.Buffer = (WCHAR*)(info+1)`, i.e. the string data lives immediately after the 16-byte header in the SAME output buffer — matches FEX's own `Buffer{ MEMORY_SECTION_NAME Info; array<WCHAR,PATH_MAX> PathW; }` wrapper exactly, `PathW` sitting right after `Info`), NUL-terminated, truncates safely (falls back to `STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH`) if the caller's buffer is too small (never hit in practice — FEX's own `0x218`-byte buffer comfortably fits any realistic path).

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-49_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 6164 lines): the fix WORKS exactly as intended — `grep`'d for the four class-2 calls: `NtQueryVirtualMemory MemorySectionName addr=0x47660000 name=C:\windows\hello8_x86.exe`, `addr=0x48690000 name=C:\windows\syswow64\ntdll.dll`, `addr=0x5D090000 name=C:\windows\syswow64\kernel32.dll` (both occurrences), `addr=0x5E1C0000 name=C:\windows\syswow64\kernelbase.dll` (both occurrences) — ALL FOUR resolve to the CORRECT real basename this time (previously always `{}`/empty). Both `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` calls still complete `rc=0x0` (unchanged, still no crash) — confirming the fix is additive, not regressive.

**Re-armed `XIntervals` dumper (`kmain.c`, was one-shot `static int ivt_dumped`, consumed by an EARLIER, unrelated self-fault at transcript line ~1362 in EVERY prior atom's receipt — meaning `XIntervals` had literally never been inspected at or after either DLL's own notify call before this atom)**: changed to a capped counter (`ivt_dump_count`, cap 16) so it fires at every self-fault occurrence, plus added an explicit `kernelbase_entry(0x5E228BD0)` enclosure check alongside the original `exe_entry` one. **RESULT, definitive, not inferred**: all 9 fires across this run's full transcript (`grep -c "^CHALICE C4-49 XIntervals"` = 9, spanning transcript lines 1377 through 6136 — i.e. BEFORE, BETWEEN, and AFTER both `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` calls at lines 4143/4907) report the **IDENTICAL** `count=8` interval list, byte-for-byte the same 8 `[Base,End)` ranges every single time (`[0x47020000,0x47660000) [0x47660000,0x47665000) [0x48690000,0x488BD000) [0x4C910000,0x4CA30000) [0x4DA40000,0x4DDA2000) [0x4EDC0000,0x4EEF0000) [0x4FF00000,0x50030000) [0x58040000,0x58041000)`) — kernel32.dll (`0x5D090000`) and kernelbase.dll (`0x5E1C0000`) NEVER appear, at ANY point in the run, including the LAST dump (line 6136), which fires at the EXACT SAME self-fault event as the final `NtRaiseException ... kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0` termination. `kernelbase_entry(0x5E228BD0) enclosed=0x0` every single time. **This empirically REFUTES both of the task's own leading hypotheses, on real evidence, not source-inference**: (i) the empty-`GetSectionFilePath`/empty-Name theory — DISPROVEN: `GetSectionFilePath` now succeeds with the byte-correct real path for both DLLs (confirmed above) and `XIntervals` STILL never gains their ranges, so a resolvable path was never the blocker; (ii) the `MemoryBasicInformation`/`Type=MEM_PRIVATE-vs-MEM_IMAGE` theory — DISPROVEN at the source level (Measure 2 above: this call chain never queries class 0 at all) and moot regardless (this floor's own class-0 answer is already correct).

**What this actually proves, precisely, and the sharpened next leg**: `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap`'s vendored-source body (`RtlImageNtHeader(Address)` + section walk + unconditional `XIntervals.Insert`) does NOT, per this atom's own direct empirical test, actually take effect for kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll's dynamic runtime maps — despite `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` completing `rc=0x0` (no crash, no early return visible in the vendored source, and now also a CORRECT resolved path). Two possibilities, genuinely undistinguished this atom (time-box; both need ARM64 disasm of the actual compiled `libwow64fex.dll` blob, not more source reading — the vendored `FEX/Source` tree, `git log` shows commit `86ff33bbe` (2026-05-10, near tag `FEX-2605`), is NOT proven to be the exact source for the Hangover-11.9-tagged prebuilt blob `kernel-arm/chalice/fetch-stock-dlls.sh` actually downloads — this is the FIRST atom in the whole 49-atom thread where a vendored-source citation has been directly, empirically shown to mispredict the real blob's behavior on its own headline claim, "Insert always happens, Name irrelevant"): (i) `RtlImageNtHeader(Address)` itself may be silently failing (returning NULL, so the section loop never executes, so `XIntervals.Insert` never runs) specifically for DYNAMICALLY-mapped runtime addresses, even though the SAME function-name's logic clearly succeeds for the Phase-0/boot-time images (`exe_entry`/`ntdll32`, both correctly present in every one of the 9 dumps) — plausible if the ACTUAL compiled `RtlImageNtHeader` (donor `wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c:4167-4188`, wrapped in `__TRY/__EXCEPT_PAGE_FAULT`) relies on a host OS signal-handling mechanism (`sigsetjmp`/`SIGSEGV`) this bare-metal floor never provides for NATIVE (non-guest) FEX code, so a genuine host-side page-fault-on-read inside this guarded region might behave differently than either "crash visibly" or "succeed" — needs a live disasm-driven trace of the ACTUAL blob's `RtlImageNtHeader`/`HandleImageMap` machine code (not the vendored source) at exactly this call, the honest next step; (ii) the compiled blob's real `HandleImageMap`/`BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` genuinely differs from the vendored source (an actual, real early-return this atom's source-reading cannot see) — indistinguishable from (i) without disassembling the blob directly. **NOT** `ret=42`; the fault is BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-48's own (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0, `pe10 ret=3221225477`) — no new fault, no regression, a sharper and now disasm-scoped understanding of the SAME frontier.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-49b_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 — none of these exercise `MemorySectionName` (no dynamic DLL load), so the new class-2 handler is untested by them but also cannot regress them.
