## C4-48 — BOTH C4-47-pinned bugs FIXED (nested-syscall dispatch now works); frontier lands back on the C4-45 fault, a genuinely NEW/distinct finding (not `ret=42`, not the C4-47 bug recurring).

**Design chosen**: **sp-carve** (donor-exact, `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/signal_arm64.c`'s `syscall_frame` allocation policy) over an explicit depth-4 frame-stack array. Why: donor's OWN mechanism already carves `frame = sp - frame_size` fresh on EVERY dispatcher entry, no fixed TEB-slot reuse at all — nesting is then automatic and unbounded (depth == C call-stack depth, no arbitrary cap), since a nested entry's own `sp` is provably lower than its enclosing call's `sp` (the nested call is reached via several more stack frames of native C in between), so a fresh carve below it can never collide with the still-live outer frame's memory. A hand-rolled depth-4 array would (a) impose an arbitrary, unmotivated depth cap where the donor's own design has none, (b) require an explicit depth counter/index (more state, more failure modes) where the donor's approach needs none (the chain is entirely self-describing via each frame's own `prev` link), (c) not actually match `sizeof(struct syscall_frame)`'s own 0x330-byte carve, which is already computed relative to `sp` at entry — sp-carve is the smaller, donor-truer diff.

**Bug1 fix (x18 bracket)**: `ntfile.c`'s `NtMapViewOfSection` now reads the guest TEB (`u64b nt_c4_48_teb; __asm__ volatile("mov %0, x18" : "=r"(nt_c4_48_teb));`) as its OWN absolute first statement, before `(void)ProcessHandle;`/`if (!BaseAddress...)`/`nt_handle_get`/`PeMapImage`/etc — all plain `aarch64-elf` C, free to clobber x18 as ordinary scratch (confirmed since C4-47: no `-ffixed-x18` anywhere in `build.sh`) — can touch the register. That captured value is threaded down as an explicit new 4th parameter to `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view(name, base, size, teb)`, which now calls `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` through a NEW asm shim, `pe_enter6` (`ntdisp.S`), instead of a raw, unbracketed C `fn(...)` call. `pe_enter6` is a mechanical 6-argument extension of the EXISTING, already-proven `pe_enter`/`pe_enter1` idiom (same file, same shape: `stp x29,x30,[sp,#-16]!; mov x29,sp; mov x9,x18 (save kernel x18); mov x10,x0 (stash entry ptr); mov x18,x1 (install caller-captured TEB); mov x0,x2..x5,x7 (shift the six real args down); blr x10; mov x18,x9 (restore kernel x18); ldp x29,x30,[sp],#16; ret`) — `long pe_enter6(long(*entry)(void*,void*,void*,unsigned long,unsigned long,unsigned long), void *teb, void *a0, void *a1, void *a2, unsigned long a3, unsigned long a4, unsigned long a5)`, matching `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSectionFn`'s exact 6-param shape (`Unk1,Address,Unk2,Size,AllocType,Prot`), all 8 total args (entry+teb+6) fit in `x0-x7`, no stack-arg marshal needed. `ntsvc.h` declares it; new declared/defined symbols only, no existing function signature changed.

**Bug2 fix (nested-safe frame allocation)**: `ntdisp.S`'s `nt_dispatch` entry preamble:
```
// OLD (Atom A, one-shot, unsafe for nesting):
ldr  x10, [x18, #0x378]         // NT_TEB_SYSCALL_FRAME_OFF: reuse if set
cbnz x10, 1f
sub  x10, sp, #0x330            // carve sizeof(struct syscall_frame)
str  x10, [x18, #0x378]
1:
// NEW (C4-48, sp-carve, always-fresh):
ldr  x12, [x18, #0x378]          // x12 = currently-live frame ptr (0 at top
                                  // level; the OUTER frame's ptr if nested)
sub  x10, sp, #0x330             // ALWAYS carve fresh, below THIS call's
                                  // own (lower, if nested) sp
str  x12, [x10, #0x118]          // frame->prev (NT_FRAME_PREV_OFF) = the
                                  // captured previous value -- LIFO link
str  x10, [x18, #0x378]          // publish new frame as current
```
(`x12` chosen deliberately — `x11` at this point already holds the stashed real caller LR, needed intact until it's stored to `frame->stub_lr@0x110` further down; `x9`/`x10` are the caller-sp/frame-ptr already in play; `x12` is genuinely free here, not touched again until `copy_args`, well after.) `dispatch_return`'s epilogue: the old unconditional `str xzr, [x18, #0x378]` ("Atom A: one-shot frame, no re-entrancy") replaced with `ldr x11, [x10, #0x118]` (load `frame->prev`) followed by `str x11, [x18, #0x378]` — a genuine LIFO pop instead of an unconditional clear. At top level `frame->prev` is always 0 (nothing was live at TEB+0x378 before a top-level syscall entry), so this restores 0 — BIT-FOR-BIT identical outcome to the old code for every existing non-nested call (the thing the four gates below exercise heavily). If nested, `frame->prev` holds the enclosing OUTER frame's still-valid ptr, correctly re-published so that call's OWN eventual `dispatch_return` finds ITS frame intact instead of finding it zeroed early (the exact C4-47-pinned corruption path). New offset `NT_FRAME_PREV_OFF=0x118` (`ntids.h`) — the last free 8 bytes of the gap `NT_FRAME_STUB_LR_OFF`'s own comment already flagged ("between cpsr(0x108) and id(0x120), 24 bytes free": `stub_lr`@0x110 claims the first 8, leaving exactly 0x118-0x120 unused before this atom). 0x330B total frame size UNCHANGED; only the allocation POLICY (always-carve vs carve-once-then-reuse) and the epilogue's restore-vs-zero behavior changed — no other `nt_dispatch`/`dispatch_return` logic touched.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-48_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 — all four exercise single-depth (non-nested) syscall dispatch heavily; the bit-for-bit-identical-at-depth-0 design is what makes this pass unsurprising, not a coincidence.

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-48_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 6084 lines; excerpt `c4-48_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): CONFIRMS both bugs genuinely fixed, not just gate-invisible — `CHALICE C4-46: BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection name=syswow64/kernel32.dll ... rc=0x0` (line 4103) and `... name=syswow64/kernelbase.dll ... rc=0x0` (line 4841) BOTH complete with ZERO intervening fault of any kind — the exact call chain (`NtMapViewOfSection`→`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`→`pe_enter6`→native `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`→`HandleImageMap`→`GetSectionFilePath`→nested `NtQueryVirtualMemory`→`nt_dispatch`, re-entering `nt_dispatch` from INSIDE the still-in-flight outer `NtMapViewOfSection` syscall) that previously crashed at `elr=0x40209E0C`/`far=0x118D9B` (C4-47) now runs clean, for BOTH DLLs, not just one — direct, not inferred, proof both fixes land correctly. No `elr=0x40209E0C` anywhere in the 6084-line transcript (`grep` confirmed); no unrecoverable "no known image/arena/vm range" kernel fatal-dump (the C4-46 atom's own new-frontier signature) either — THAT specific fault class is provably gone. The run also does NOT time out (C4-46's own run hit a 45s drain timeout with no `pe10 ret=` line at all) — this run prints a clean `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005` `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`), proving the guest is making forward progress past the point that previously hung.

**New frontier, genuinely distinct, NOT `ret=42`, NOT a C4-47 regression**: the guest proceeds through `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)` (both already-working mechanisms, C4-25) into `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0` (`RAISE#1`, `info[0]=0x8` — EXECUTE-type access violation, the SAME `ExceptionInformation[0]` semantics `NoExecOp` self-faults carry throughout this whole chain, C4-31/32/45), `FirstChance=FALSE`, clean second-chance terminate — `pe10 ret=3221225477`. This is BYTE-IDENTICAL in address, status code, and violation-type to C4-45's ORIGINAL fault (the one C4-46 was expressly built to eliminate, and which C4-46's OWN receipt confirmed "is GONE" the one time it got far enough to observe kernelbase.dll's map before hitting the C4-47 crash first). Ruled out, not guessed, as a repeat of the C4-47 class: no raw ARM64-level fault, no kernel-internal corruption signature, no `elr` inside `nt_dispatch` or any kernel `.text` address anywhere in this run — this is a clean, guest-SEH-mediated exception delivery/termination, structurally identical to how C4-45's original fault always presented. **What this proves, precisely**: `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`/`HandleImageMap` completing without crashing (this atom's entire mandate, now proven done) is NOT sufficient, by itself, to make FEX's `InvalidationTracker`/`XIntervals` actually treat `kernelbase.dll`'s mapped range as executable at the moment the guest jumps into its entry point. Two honest, undistinguished-this-atom candidates for the next leg: (i) `HandleImageMap`'s real body needs more than the bare `Address` argument this floor passes — C4-32's own quoted decompile said only `Address` is read, but that quote may be a partial/simplified view, not the FULL body (worth a deeper Hangover/FEX source read, if available, beyond what's already quoted here); (ii) real Wine's equivalent path backs its interval-tree insert via `GetSectionFilePath`, which (per its own name) resolves a HOST FILESYSTEM path for the mapped section — this floor's images are ALL blob-backed (embedded in the disk image via `mkdisk.sh`, `nt_image_register`'s entries carry no real host path), so `GetSectionFilePath` may silently fail/no-op for every image on this floor, meaning the `XIntervals` insert never actually happens even though `HandleImageMap`'s own call chain runs to completion without crashing (a fast, harmless early-return, not caught by watching for a fault). Neither pinned this atom (time-box; this atom's own mandate — the nested-dispatch pair — is done and gate-proven); next atom's smallest honest path is probably (ii) first (cheaper to test: instrument `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`'s own `rc` more precisely, or trace whatever `GetSectionFilePath`-equivalent call the notify chain makes, if any is visible in the transcript between the notify call and its `rc=0x0` print).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntdisp.S,ntfile.c,ntsvc.h}` + `kernel-arm/chalice/ntids.h` (`pe_enter6` asm shim, `NT_FRAME_PREV_OFF` define, TEB-capture-and-thread in `NtMapViewOfSection`/`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, sp-carve LIFO frame-chain rewrite of `nt_dispatch`/`dispatch_return`'s allocation policy), commit `85afa91`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-47 — NAMED, NOT FIXED (verdict: dual interacting bug, no small honest fix this atom): the C4-46-named raw ARM64 fault `elr=0x40209E0C` is inside OUR OWN `nt_dispatch` (`ntdisp.S`), NOT an unknown/wild region — kernel Image links at `0x40200000` (`linker.ld`), so `elr = kernel .text + 0x9E0C`. `aarch64-elf-nm -n gaia-os.elf` brackets it exactly: `nt_dispatch` @ `0x40209e04`, next symbol `copy_args` @ `0x40209ea4` — `0x40209E0C` = `nt_dispatch+0x8`. `aarch64-elf-objdump -d --start-address=0x40209e00 --stop-address=0x40209ea4` confirms the exact instruction: `ldr x10, [x18, #888]` (`#888 = 0x378 = NT_TEB_SYSCALL_FRAME_OFF`) — `nt_dispatch`'s VERY FIRST TEB-relative access, the syscall-frame reuse-check (`cbnz x10,1f; sub x10,sp,#0x330; str x10,[x18,#0x378]` follows immediately).

**Register dump pins the mechanism precisely, not by inference** (`scratch/c4-46_pe10_fex_notify_fault.log:144-170`): `x8=0x23`(35 decimal) `x9=0x4116ACA0`(=sp at call) `x16=0x40209E04`(=`nt_dispatch`'s own entry address) `x18=0x118A23` (misaligned — `&7==3`, unlike every genuine heap/TEB/image pointer elsewhere in this system, so provably NOT a real pointer, just leftover scratch) `lr=ntdll.dll+0x64F84` (return address INSIDE a native ntdll.dll function, i.e. the call that would have returned here IS native ntdll code, not a wild jump). This is BYTE-FOR-BYTE the real stock-Wine ARM64 syscall-stub convention this floor's own header comment already documents (`mov x8,#num; mov x9,x30; ldr x16,[disp]; blr x16`): `x16` == dispatch entry (the `blr` target register, AAPCS64 IP0), `x9` == caller's real LR, `lr` == a genuine native-ntdll return address. `x8=0x23`(35), cross-checked against `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt` (`35  0x023  NtQueryVirtualMemory`), CONFIRMS — not guesses — C4-46's own "likely candidate (i)": `GetSectionFilePath`'s internal `NtQueryVirtualMemory` call.

**Root cause chain, pinned end to end**: `NtMapViewOfSection` (`ntfile.c`)'s new C4-46 hook calls `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` → a raw C function-pointer call `fn(...)` into `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` (native FEX/ARM64 code) → `HandleImageMap` → (per C4-46's own "likely candidate" list, now confirmed) `GetSectionFilePath`, which internally issues a REAL nested syscall (`NtQueryVirtualMemory`, id 35, confirmed above) via a native ntdll.dll-compiled stub — the SAME `blr`-into-`nt_dispatch` convention every guest PE-side stub uses (`ntstub.S`/donor). This ENTIRE new C4-46 call chain — from `NtMapViewOfSection`'s C body through the raw `fn()` call — is genuine `aarch64-elf`-compiled kernel C, which per THIS FLOOR'S OWN documented design (`ntdisp.S` header: "x18 = TEB on the PE (Windows ABI) side, but our own kernel C (aarch64-none-elf) may treat x18 as ordinary scratch — stash/restore around the C handler call") is free to clobber x18 as an ordinary temp register (confirmed: grepped `build.sh` for `-ffixed-x18` — zero hits, x18 is never reserved for this floor's own kernel C build) — and this brand-new C4-46 call site has NO stash/restore bracket at all, unlike EVERY other native/PE-crossing boundary already in this tree (`nt_dispatch`'s own `x22`/`x24` stash-restore around its two internal `blr`s; `pe_enter`/`pe_enter1`'s `x9` stash-restore around their own native `blr`). By the time `HandleImageMap`'s native code reaches its nested `NtQueryVirtualMemory` stub call, x18 (still expected == guest TEB by that native, Windows-ARM64-ABI-respecting code) instead holds whatever unrelated scratch value our own kernel C compiler last left there (`0x118A23`) — so `nt_dispatch`'s FIRST TEB-relative load faults on `far = x18+0x378 = 0x118D9B` (byte-exact match to the receipt's own `far=0x118D9B`), an address with no backing mapping.

**Second, INTERACTING bug, surfaced by reasoning through the naive fix BEFORE attempting it (per task's own monad-check discipline)**: even if x18 were correctly re-installed at the `fn()` call boundary (e.g. via a `pe_enter`-style TEB-install wrapper — mechanically extending the EXISTING, already-proven `pe_enter1` idiom to more args, a genuinely small, donor-consistent change ON ITS OWN), this would NOT fix the fault — it would only MOVE it. `nt_dispatch`'s syscall-frame allocation (`ldr x10,[x18,#0x378]; cbnz x10,1f; sub x10,sp,#0x330; str x10,[x18,#0x378]`) is an explicitly documented **"one-shot frame, no re-entrancy"** design (`dispatch_return`'s own comment: `str xzr, [x18, #0x378] // Atom A: one-shot frame, no re-entrancy`) — a SINGLE fixed slot per TEB, not a stack. The OUTER `NtMapViewOfSection` syscall is STILL mid-flight (its own frame pointer already live at `[x18+0x378]`) when the nested `NtQueryVirtualMemory` call would reach `nt_dispatch` — `cbnz x10,1f` takes the REUSE branch, writing the NESTED call's own `x0-x7`/`x19-x28`/`fp`/`sp`/`pc`/`stub_lr` fields directly OVER the OUTER frame's still-live data (same fixed memory address, not a fresh allocation), and the nested call's OWN `dispatch_return` runs `str xzr,[x18,#0x378]` on its way out — so when the OUTER `NtMapViewOfSection` call chain eventually unwinds back to the OUTER `nt_dispatch`'s own `dispatch_return`, `[x18+0x378]` reads back **0**, and `ldr x16,[x10,#0x100]` with `x10=0` dereferences address `0x100` — an immediate, DIFFERENT, still-unrecoverable crash in the OUTER syscall's own return path. Confirmed by direct reading of the asm (`ntdisp.S`), not speculation — this is exactly the hazard the task's own MONAD correction predicted ("stack/frame/TEB state clobber... nested dispatch our floor may not support").

**Verdict**: BOTH bugs are load-bearing for a genuine fix — the x18 hand-off (small, isolated, safe: extend `pe_enter1` to a `pe_enter6`, reinstall the captured entry-time TEB right at the `fn()` call boundary) and the single-slot re-entrancy (NOT small: requires changing `nt_dispatch`/`dispatch_return`'s core frame-allocation/teardown logic — the HOTTEST path on this floor, exercised by literally every syscall ever made, zero margin for asm error, and no existing gate (`pe8`/`pe9`/`ramfb`) exercises nested-syscall correctness to catch a regression here). Per task's own ranked-option law ("if neither [route] is small → document verdict, STOP"): NOT attempting a partial x18-only fix (it would trade this named crash for a different, unrecovered, harder-to-diagnose one at the OUTER dispatch's own return path — net negative progress, not forward motion) and NOT attempting the frame-reentrancy redesign this atom (large, high blast-radius on the hottest path in the kernel, unverifiable with confidence within this atom's time-box). Documented, STOPPED per instruction. No code changed this atom — gates unexercised (nothing to regress), tree unchanged from commit `8310bcd`.

**Next atom's smallest honest path (both legs, in order)**: (1) the isolated, safe x18 hand-off fix (`pe_enter6`, mechanical extension of the proven `pe_enter`/`pe_enter1` pattern: `stp x29,x30,[sp,#-16]!; mov x29,sp; mov x9,x18 /*save kernel x18*/; mov x10,x0 /*stash entry ptr*/; mov x18,x1 /*install TEB*/; mov x0,x2; mov x1,x3; mov x2,x4; mov x3,x5; mov x4,x6; mov x5,x7; blr x10; mov x18,x9 /*restore*/; ldp x29,x30,[sp],#16; ret` — x0=entry,x1=teb,x2..x7=six args, matches `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSectionFn`'s six-param shape exactly) so x18 is PROVABLY correct at the `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` call boundary, captured via the SAME `mov %0,x18` idiom already used elsewhere on this floor (`ntsvc.c:589/1221/1331`), read as the FIRST statement of `NtMapViewOfSection` (before any intervening C code can touch it) and threaded down as an explicit parameter; (2) SEPARATELY, make `nt_dispatch`'s frame slot genuinely nest-safe — smallest correct shape: on entry, capture whatever `[x18+0x378]` ALREADY holds into the new frame itself (one more 8-byte slot, e.g. offset `0x120` — currently unused per the struct layout this atom audited: `0x00-0x38`=x0-x7, `0xe8`=fp, `0xf0`=sp, `0x100`=pc, `0x110`=stub_lr, `0x130-0x178`=x19-x28) instead of the bare `cbnz`-reuse-or-carve-once check; on `dispatch_return`, RESTORE that captured previous value into `[x18+0x378]` instead of unconditionally zeroing — turns the single slot into a minimal LIFO chain, safe for the one nesting depth this atom observed, and IDENTICAL to today's behavior for the existing non-nested (top-level) case (previous value is always 0 there, restoring 0 == today's `str xzr`). Needs its OWN careful build+boot verification cycle (own atom, own time-box) before trusting it on the hottest path in the kernel.

**Gates**: unexercised this atom (no code change, nothing to regress). **Commit**: none (documentation only, HANDOFF.md + FEX-MAP.md). Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-46 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED: pinned + fixed the C4-45-named `kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0` NoExecOp AV; a new, deeper, unrecovered fault found and named, not chased.

**Measure 1 (per task instruction) — exception detail + last syscalls before the AV, `scratch/c4-45_pe10_fex_receipt_postfix.log`**: the `NtRaiseException` immediately preceding termination reports `code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, `ExceptionInformation[0]=0x8` (EXECUTE-type access violation per Windows' AV-info-0 convention: `0`=read `1`=write `8`=execute/DEP), `ExceptionInformation[1]=0x5E228BD0` (the same faulting address). Two `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context` round-trips immediately prior both report the SAME live `eip=0x5E228BD0` — LIVE, not the stale `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg` artifact C4-45 itself flagged (that mechanism's own print, `CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX: Eip=0x47661000 (hello8_x86.exe+0x1000)`, IS present in this same receipt and IS the known-stale boot-seed value — correctly NOT what this atom trusts). The underlying ARM64-side self-fault (`kmain.c`'s existing C4-31 `SynchronousFaultData` dump) reports `Signal=SIGSEGV(11) TrapNo=PF(14) si_code=2(SEGV_ACCERR) err_code=0x15` — decoding the x86 page-fault error-code bits: `Present=1 Write=0 User=1 InstructionFetch=1` — a genuine x86-semantic NX/DEP violation (present page, execute denied), byte-matching FEX's own documented `NoExecOp` self-fault signature (C4-31/32, below).

**Measure 2 (donor disasm) — RVA `0x68BD0` pinned exactly**: `objdump -d stock/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll` (ImageBase `0x7b000000`, confirmed via its own PE optional header: `ImageBase=0x7b000000 AddressOfEntryPoint=0x68bd0`) shows RVA `0x68BD0` is `_DllMainCRTStartup@12`'s own first byte (`66 90 nop; 55 push ebp; 89 e5 mov ebp,esp; ...`) — the module's real, PE-header-declared entry point, NOT a section-boundary/truncation artifact: `.text` section header reads `VA=0x1000 VSize=0x68f15 RawPtr=0x1000 RawSize=0x69000`, spanning RVA `[0x1000,0x69F15)` — entry point sits `0x1345` bytes before the section's own end, comfortably inside, with normal code (`_DllMainCRTStartup`/`_wine_spec_unimplemented_stub`/`_wine_longjmp`) on both sides. Confirms: the loader is calling kernelbase.dll's OWN, correctly-resolved entry point (dependency-load order: `kernel32.dll`'s `load_dll` call loads its `kernelbase.dll` dependency FIRST and calls ITS entry before kernel32's own) — this is the guest's very FIRST instruction fetch into either DLL (neither kernel32 nor kernelbase have executed any code yet at this point), matching `KERNEL32.DLL`/`KERNELBASE.DLL` both just having become loadable for real via C4-45's `DllPath` fix.

**Root cause, donor-traced (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`)**: `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` (the executable-range interval tree `FEXCore::Frontend::Decoder::CheckRangeExecutable` consults, C4-31/32/33/34's own object) gets populated exactly THREE ways: (1) the tracker's own ctor `VirtualQuery` sweep of whatever is ALREADY mapped/committed at `BTCpuProcessInit` construction time (`Module.cpp:520`ish, one-shot, early in boot); (2) `BTCpuProcessInit`'s own two explicit `HandleImageMap` calls (`Module.cpp:548/551`) — `HandleImageMap(PEB.ImageBaseAddress, true)` for the main EXE, `HandleImageMap(LdrSystemDllInitBlock->ntdll_handle)` for the 32-bit ntdll — BOTH already correctly wired on this floor (C4-29's own fix, confirmed live every boot since); (3) the `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` export (`Module.cpp:1009-1013`, quoted verbatim in C4-32 below), which on REAL Wine fires from `dlls/wow64/syscall.c`'s own `NtMapViewOfSection` THUNK immediately after the real 32-on-64 syscall returns, for any image mapped AFTER process-start via the guest's own `LdrLoadDll` (dependency-chain DLLs — kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll, here, and any future one).

This floor's `NtMapViewOfSection` (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`) is a DIRECT kernel-mode syscall implementation — no wow64.dll thunk layer sits between a guest syscall and this floor's own `Nt*` table (confirmed by this floor's OWN architecture doctrine throughout: every `Nt*` function lives in `ntsvc.c`/`ntfile.c`, dispatched straight off `ntdisp.S`'s `svc` trap). Grepped this entire tree for any `BTCpuNotify*`/`HandleImageMap` call site outside `.md` doc files: **zero hits** before this fix — confirming, not assuming, that path (3) never fires here. Path (1)/(2) only cover whatever is mapped strictly BEFORE `BTCpuProcessInit` runs (the exe + 32-bit ntdll, both this floor's own Phase-0 C-direct maps) — kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll map LATER, via a genuine runtime `NtMapViewOfSection` call (confirmed: `NtOpenFile kernel32.dll`/`kernelbase.dll` both occur hundreds of transcript lines AFTER the `starting FEX (Hangover 11.9)` banner, i.e. well after `BTCpuProcessInit`/its `InvalidationTracker` ctor have already run and finished) — so NONE of the three registration paths ever cover them. First instruction fetch into either -> `NoExecOp` self-fault, unconditionally, every time, until fixed.

**Why this is NOT a repeat of C4-31/32/33/34's exe_entry mirage**: those atoms chased an apparently-identical `NoExecOp` signature for `exe_entry` (`hello8_x86.exe`'s own entry) and eventually (C4-35) found the REAL producer was unrelated (`pe_find_named_export`'s missing PE32/PE32+ dispatch feeding `LdrInitializeThunk=0`, making the "live" `Eip=exe_entry` reading a stale-memory-read artifact, not the genuine faulting address). This atom's fault does NOT share that flaw: the faulting address is confirmed LIVE via two independent, fresh mechanisms (the `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread` round-trip AND the `ExceptionRecord`'s own `ExceptionAddress` field, both agreeing), and the target (`kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`) is a genuinely mapped, correctly-relocated, byte-verified real code address (disasm-matched against the donor file) — not a NULL/garbage jump. The underlying `InvalidationTracker` gap is real and distinct: exe_entry's registration path (2a, `PEB.ImageBaseAddress`) was ALWAYS fine (C4-32's own refutation stands); kernelbase.dll/kernel32.dll's gap is path (3), never wired at all, a DIFFERENT, newly-actionable leg.

**Fix (`ntfile.c`, `NtMapViewOfSection`)**: added `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view(name, base, size)`, called right after `nt_image_register`/`nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32` for every mapped SEC_IMAGE. It (a) lazily resolves `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s address via `pe_find_named_export` against WHICHEVER image is actually the CPU backend (a harmless, fast-failing no-op probe against every other mapped image — same resolve-by-export-name idiom this floor already uses everywhere, C4-4/26/27/29); (b) reads the just-mapped image's own PE `Machine` field (same `e_lfanew`+4 read `nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32` already uses) and only proceeds for `Machine==0x14C` (guest x86 — skips native ARM64 images like wow64.dll/the backend/win32u.dll/wow64win.dll, which are never decoded by FEX's x86 frontend and need no `XIntervals` membership); (c) skips `ntdll.dll` by name (already covered by path (2)'s dedicated, working registration — avoids a redundant/overlapping `XIntervals` insert, a concern C4-31 itself flagged-but-never-chased for duplicate inserts); (d) calls `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection(0, (void*)base, 0, size, 0, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE)` as a raw native ARM64 function pointer call — donor-exact signature quoted below (C4-32), which per its own quoted decompile only locks a mutex then calls `HandleImageMap(Address)` (`MainImage` defaults false) — a pure `RtlImageNtHeader(Address)`-driven section walk needing no other argument, the other params (`Unk1`/`Unk2`/`AllocType`/`Prot`) never read by the quoted body.

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `/tmp/gaia-pe10-probe.log`, 4151 lines, saved excerpt `zoneA/scratch/c4-46_pe10_fex_notify_fault.log`): the C4-45 `kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0` `DllMainCRTStartup` NoExecOp fault is **GONE** — kernel32.dll maps (`CHALICE IMAGE: name=syswow64/kernel32.dll base=0x5D090000 size=0x119000`) and the new notify hook fires (the SAME call site) without reproducing that fault; `kernelbase.dll` never even reaches its own map this run (confirmed: the ONLY "kernelbase" mention in the whole transcript is the pre-existing fatal-dump's own "root_key_names[] relocation spot-check" footer reporting "kernelbase.dll not found in image registry" — i.e. this run's NEW fault happens EARLIER, on kernel32.dll's own self-map, before kernelbase.dll ever loads). A NEW, DEEPER, qualitatively WORSE-CHARACTER fault immediately follows: a raw ARM64 Data Abort (`esr=0x96000006 ec=0x25 far=0x118D9B`) whose `elr=0x40209E0C` resolves to **no known image, arena, or VM range at all** (`kmain.c`'s `sync_try_guest_exception`'s own `known_guest` gate fails for it, correctly routing to the KERNEL's unrecoverable fatal-dump path, not guest-exception delivery) — `lr=ntdll.dll+0x64F84` (a return address INSIDE native ntdll.dll), consistent with `HandleImageMap`'s own `RtlImageNtHeader`/`GetSectionFilePath` call chain reaching one level deeper into native-code territory than anything previously exercised on this floor (this is the FIRST time `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` has ever been invoked here). **No `pe10 ret=` line is ever printed** — the probe's own 45s drain times out and SIGTERMs qemu, unlike C4-45's clean `pe10 ret=3221225477`. Named, NOT chased further this atom (out of scope/time-box): likely candidates for the next atom — (i) `GetSectionFilePath`'s own `NtQueryVirtualMemory(MemoryMappedFilenameInformation)` call (a class this floor doesn't implement, should just error cleanly, low suspicion); (ii) `ThreadCreationMutex`'s own lock implementation touching a native threading primitive (pthread/futex-style) never exercised before on this floor (higher suspicion, first-ever call); (iii) a genuinely wild/uninitialized function pointer inside `HandleImageMap`'s own fextl/libc++ string handling. Needs either a guarded/`nt_setjmp`-wrapped native call (so a fault here degrades to a diagnosis instead of a hang, mirroring `NtTerminateProcess`'s own unwind channel) or a direct disasm of `0x40209E0C`'s containing function.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS (`zoneA/scratch/c4-46...` -- reused pe8/pe9/ramfb gate scripts, no dedicated per-atom log this pass) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 — none of these synthetic gates exercise a dynamically-loaded guest x86 DLL the way `pe10` does, so none regress from this change.

**Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` (`NtMapViewOfSection`'s new `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` hook + `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSectionFn` typedef), commit `8310bcd`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-45 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED: pinned the SECOND `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT` (0xC000007B) C4-44 left unpinned, end to end via donor source, and fixed it.

**PRIME RIVAL check (per task instruction, done FIRST)**: task suspected `NtQuerySystemInformationEx(SystemSupportedProcessorArchitectures)` might be stubbed, forcing a bogus i386-only "unsupported" gate on native ARM64 ntdll (donor `build_main_module`'s supported-machine check, obtained via that exact query). Grepped this floor's OWN source (`ntsvc.c`): implemented honestly since C4-6 (`entry0=native ARM64 0xAA64`, `entry1=guest i386 0x14C`, real bitfield shape). Grepped the C4-44 receipt log for the call: answered TWICE, both success (`native=0xAA64 current=0x14C`, lines 221 and 2641). **Ruled out by direct evidence, not assumption** — not the producer.

**Measure step 1 (per task instruction)**: added a one-shot diagnostic to `NtTerminateProcess` (`ntsvc.c`) — on `ExitStatus==STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`, reads `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg` (the WOW64_CPURESERVED block's embedded I386_CONTEXT, the SAME mechanism C4-25's RAISE#1 receipt proved live for reading the guest's REAL Eip/Esp with no syscall round-trip) and dumps Eip/Esp/Ebp + a `[Eip-16,Eip+32)` guest-byte hexdump + one `[ebp+4]` stack-frame walk, resolved against the image registry. Built clean, gated, checkpoint-committed (`fc97a2a`), booted `pe10 fex` (`scratch/c4-45_pe10_fex_receipt_prefix_diagnostic.log`): the diagnostic FIRED (`CHALICE C4-45: NtTerminateProcess(STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT) live guest Eip=0x47661000 (hello8_x86.exe+0x1000) ... Esp=0x488CC000 Ebp=0x488CC000`) — but this is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the boot-time-SEEDED initial context (`nt_wow64_set_initial_context`'s own seed value, `Ebp==Esp` empty-frame convention), not fresh live data. Cross-checked: only 3 `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)` SET calls occur in the ENTIRE C4-44 receipt (all near boot, lines 888-891), none near the actual termination — **honest negative**: FEX only refreshes `WOW64_CPURESERVED` via `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` on a genuine EXCEPTION (C4-25's own context), never on an ordinary syscall dispatch like a plain `NtTerminateProcess` call — this specific eip-trap mechanism cannot pin an ordinary-syscall call site, only an exception one. Kept as a harmless, gated (one-shot, cheap) diagnostic — not reverted, may still fire correctly for a future FAULT-driven termination.

**Measure step 2 (per task's own fallback instruction — "scan the receipt log for the LAST syscalls before NtTerminateProcess")**: `awk`'d the C4-44 receipt from the `NtQueryInformationProcess ProcessImageInformation` answer (line 3114) to EOF, filtered the routine JIT-loop noise (`CONTEXT AT SVC`/`svc id=49 UNIMPLEMENTED`/AVM/FVM lines, already-established steady-state per C4-44). The distinctive tail: several ARM64 alignment-fault self-heals (harmless, same C4-38/40 JIT-memcpy pattern), then `NtQueryVirtualMemory`+`NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx`, then **`CHALICE: NtOpenFile kernel32.dll dir=C:`**, a `__wine_dbg_header` re-verify print, then `svc id=57 (NtFsControlFile) UNIMPLEMENTED`, `svc id=90 (NtQuerySystemTime) UNIMPLEMENTED`, then straight to `NtTerminateProcess` (id=44, confirmed `0x2C`=44 via `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt`).

**Donor trace, `wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c`** (vendored copy, this repo's `wine/` tree):
- `open_dll_file` (`loader.c:2690-2749`) calls `NtFsControlFile(handle,...,FSCTL_GET_OBJECT_ID,...)` right after `NtOpenFile` succeeds — ONLY branches on SUCCESS (`if (!NtFsControlFile(...)) { ... }`), silently tolerates any failure and falls through to `NtCreateSection`/`NtQuerySection`/`is_valid_binary` — **this floor's `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` answer for it is a proven RED HERRING**, not the bug.
- `is_valid_binary` (`loader.c:2515-2529`) checks `image_info->Machine` (from `NtQuerySection(SectionImageInformation)`, already correctly answered by this floor since C3c-2i) against what's valid for the current WOW64/native mode — fails (returns FALSE) for a wrong-architecture image; `open_dll_file` then sets `status = STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED`.
- `search_dll_file` (`loader.c:3219-3260`) splits its `paths` argument on `;`, tries `open_dll_file` on each `paths-segment + "\\" + search` candidate; `if (status == STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED) found_image = TRUE;` then **CONTINUES the loop** (tries the next path segment) — only a genuine success or `STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND`-vs-other distinction short-circuits early; after exhausting all segments, `if (found_image) status = STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED;` (`loader.c:3257`).
- `find_dll_file` (`loader.c:3270-3323`): `if (status == STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED) status = STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT;` (`loader.c:3323`) — the EXACT conversion producing `0xC000007B`.
- `process_init` (`loader.c:4480-4499`): `default_load_path = peb->ProcessParameters->DllPath.Buffer;` (`loader.c:4482`, used verbatim since it's non-NULL/non-empty on this floor) — then `if ((status = load_dll(NULL, L"kernel32.dll", 0, &kernel32, FALSE)) != STATUS_SUCCESS) { MESSAGE(...); NtTerminateProcess(GetCurrentProcess(), status); }` (`loader.c:4493-4499`) — `load_path=NULL` here makes `search_dll_file` fall back to that SAME `default_load_path` (`search_dll_file`'s own `if (!paths) paths = default_load_path;`, `loader.c:3228`).

**Root cause, pinned**: this floor's `nt_wow64_build_params32` (`ntsvc.c`, C4-37) wrote `PARAMS32_DLLPATH_OFF`'s buffer pointer as an ALIAS of `PARAMS32_CURRENTDIRECTORY_OFF`'s own buffer — both `"C:\\"`, a 3-char bare drive root with ZERO `;`-separated segments (the C4-37 atom's own brief was only "a real, non-NULL value", not a correctly-shaped search path). `search_dll_file`'s loop over `paths="C:\\"` therefore tries EXACTLY ONE candidate: `"C:\\" + "kernel32.dll"` = `"C:\\kernel32.dll"` — byte-for-byte matching the observed `NtOpenFile kernel32.dll dir=C:` receipt line (dirname = the path segment before the basename's last backslash, here literally `"C:"`). This floor's own `NtOpenFile` (`ntfile.c`, C4-4) only redirects `dirname=="syswow64"` or (`"system32"` AND `nt_wow64_process_active`) to the `syswow64/` blob root; `"C:"` matches neither, so the lookup resolves the BARE name `"kernel32.dll"` against the embedded-blob registry, which `mkdisk.sh` registers under that exact bare name for the NATIVE (AMD64) `kernel32.dll` (the 32-bit one is registered as `"syswow64/kernel32.dll"`, a DIFFERENT manifest name) — the WRONG architecture is opened, `is_valid_binary` correctly rejects it, and the STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED→STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT chain above fires, landing on `process_init`'s own `NtTerminateProcess` call.

**Fix** (`ntsvc.c`, `nt_wow64_build_params32`): `DllPath` now gets its OWN heap buffer, ASCII-widened `"C:\\windows\\system32"` (19 chars) — real Windows/Wine's own conventional DllPath default (`%SystemRoot%\\System32`, matching `unix/loader.c`'s `get_dll_load_path` intent, no local clone of that specific function needed since this floor derives the SAME effective value directly). `CurrentDirectory` is UNCHANGED (still `"C:\\"`, `curdir_w`, C4-37's own choice — not implicated, real Windows CurrentDirectory legitimately can be a bare drive root). With this value, `search_dll_file`'s single-segment loop now tries `"C:\\windows\\system32\\kernel32.dll"` — dirname parses as `"system32"`, and this floor's EXISTING `system32`+`nt_wow64_process_active` redirect (already true for every WOW64 boot, `ntboot.c`'s `nt_wow64_set_active`) now correctly serves `syswow64/kernel32.dll` — zero new redirect logic needed, only the DllPath value itself.

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-45_pe10_fex_receipt_postfix.log`, 62 lines total vs the pre-fix 4337): the `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT` termination is GONE. Guest now loads kernel32.dll AND its own kernelbase.dll dependency for real and runs deep into `syswow64/kernelbase.dll`'s own code (resolved by this floor's image registry, proving the module is genuinely mapped this time) before hitting a NEW, LATER, qualitatively DIFFERENT fault: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, `FirstChance=FALSE` (second-chance/unhandled), clean terminate via the existing `nt_setjmp`/`nt_longjmp` channel — `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005` `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`), a materially further-forward, genuinely different bug (a real fault deep inside KERNELBASE's own code, not a loader dependency-resolution/architecture-mismatch gap) — named, not chased (out of this atom's scope/time-box), NOT `ret=42`.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS (`zoneA/scratch/c4-45_pe8_gate.log`) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`, `zoneA/scratch/c4-45_pe9_gate.log`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`zoneA/scratch/c4-45_ramfb_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` — `fc97a2a` (C4-45 `NtTerminateProcess` live-Eip diagnostic, checkpoint before boot) + `acd2fa8` (`nt_wow64_build_params32`'s `DllPath` fix). Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-44 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED (the C4-43-named silent `NtQueryInformationProcess(ProcessImageInformation=37)` gap, wired): donor `build_main_module` (`wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c:2895-2906`) queries `ProcessImageInformation` (class 37) into a local `SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION info;` declared WITHOUT `={0}`, then tests `info.ImageCharacteristics & IMAGE_FILE_DLL` unconditionally — this floor's `NtQueryInformationProcess` only implemented classes `0`/`26`, so class 37 fell through to a silent `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (buffer untouched), leaving `info.ImageCharacteristics` as guest-stack garbage; when that garbage happened to have the DLL bit set, `hello8_x86.exe` (a genuine EXE) self-terminated (`STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`).

**Fix**: fills the SAME `SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION` shape `NtQuerySection(SectionImageInformation)` already answers (`ntfile.c`, C3c-2i) — the `SII_*` offset `#define`s moved from `ntfile.c`-local to `ntfile.h` (shared, one definition, two producers). Source: this floor's own tracked main-image `PeImageInfo` (`pemod.h`), stashed via a new `nt_set_main_image_info(&exe_info, exe_entry)` call in `ntboot.c`'s `nt_boot_process` right after `PeMapImage` maps the boot exe (`exe_info` itself was already computed earlier in the SAME function for the `wow64_mode` gate, C4-6 — no new PE parse, just a stash of already-known data; `exe_entry`, the REAL post-map/relocated address, only exists once `PeMapImage` returns — matches donor `TransferAddress` semantics for a QUERY ON THE RUNNING PROCESS, as opposed to `NtQuerySection`'s pre-map preferred-`ImageBase`-relative answer). `nt_current_main_image_valid`/`nt_current_main_image_info`/`nt_current_main_image_transfer` (`ntsvc.c`): single-process-kernel per-boot stash, same pattern as `nt_current_wow64_peb32`/C4-6.

**Caller-side shape determination** (per task instruction, BEFORE writing the fix): traced this floor's OWN boot log for the real syscall id/class/buffer-length triple. `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt` confirms real syscall id `25` == `NtQueryInformationProcess` for BOTH `ProcessWow64Information`(26) and `ProcessImageInformation`(37) — one real syscall number, differentiated by the `ProcessInformationClass` argument, not by id. Donor `wine/dlls/wow64/syscall.c` has **no per-class WOW64 thunk-table entry for class 37** (unlike e.g. `ProcessBasicInformation`, which that file DOES shrink to a 32-bit shape) — `ProcessImageInformation` is queried by `build_main_module` directly against the NATIVE (64-bit/ARM64) ntdll during process init, called BEFORE the wow64 CPU-simulation rig even starts (donor `loader.c:4489`, `loader_init`, runs long before `init_wow64`). So the buffer this floor must fill is the NATIVE 64-bit `SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION` shape (`0x40` bytes) — confirmed live: this floor's own boot log shows the call at real syscall id `25` with `ProcessInformationLength` implicitly `sizeof(SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION)`=`0x40` (same length `NtQuerySection` already validates against, `SII_SIZE`), no WOW64-shrunk/thunked shape observed anywhere in this trace — NO wow64 thunking seam for this specific class, unlike most other WOW64-guest-driven syscalls this floor otherwise wires.

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, full transcript `zoneA/scratch/c4-44_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 4337 lines; tail-only quick receipt `zoneA/scratch/c4-44_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): the class-37 query IS answered this time (line 3114):
```
CHALICE: NtQueryInformationProcess ProcessImageInformation -> ImageCharacteristics=0x0000000000000102 TransferAddress=0x0000000047661000
```
`ImageCharacteristics=0x102` matches `hello8_x86.exe`'s OWN real COFF `Characteristics` field byte-for-byte (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -p hello8_x86.exe`: `Characteristics 0x102` = `IMAGE_FILE_EXECUTABLE_IMAGE(0x0002)|IMAGE_FILE_32BIT_MACHINE(0x0100)`, **NOT** `IMAGE_FILE_DLL(0x2000)`) — the false-positive garbage bit C4-43 named is CONFIRMED gone (verified against ground truth, not inferred from behavior alone). Behavioral proof the DLL-check branch does NOT fire this time: C4-43's own receipt says the prior run's second `NtQueryInformationProcess` call was "immediately followed by `NtTerminateProcess` ... with no intervening fault/exception trace of any kind" (i.e., zero guest execution between query and terminate). THIS run instead shows **~1223 further transcript lines** of real activity after the answered query — `NtQueryPerformanceCounter`(id 49)/`NtFlushInstructionCache`(id 147)/`NtAllocateVirtualMemory`(id 24)/`NtFreeVirtualMemory`(id 30) cycles, FEX's own JIT/CPU-sim steady-state pattern (same shape recurs both before AND after the class-37 hit, i.e. genuine ongoing execution, not a one-off artifact) — a qualitative (not just line-count, since several of these are wall-clock-timing-dependent spin loops and thus noisy quantitatively) proof of forward progress.

**New frontier, NOT YET disasm-pinned (honest boundary, not a guess)**: `pe10 ret=3221225595` (`0xC000007B` `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`) — the SAME numeric status as C4-43's, but proven to be a DIFFERENT donor call site, since the specific branch (`build_main_module`'s DLL check) that produced C4-43's status is proven NOT to have fired this time (see above). Ruled OUT by direct evidence: `hello8_x86.exe` has an EMPTY relocation directory (`objdump -x`: `Base Relocation Directory [.reloc]` entry = `VA=0 Size=0`) so donor `perform_relocations`'s own `if (!relocs->Size) return STATUS_SUCCESS;` fast-path applies — no double-relocation-delta bug (a real donor-documented risk this floor's own `NtMapViewOfSection` comment already flags for OTHER modules) is possible here; `hello8_x86.exe` also has EMPTY Import/Bound-Import/IAT/Delay-Import directories (`objdump -x`, all `(0,0)`) — ruling out an import-resolution failure inside `build_module`'s "fixup imports" step too. What's left unconfirmed: `perform_relocations`/`build_module`/the `RtlUserThreadStart` continuation all remain plausible NEXT-atom targets, but this floor's `CHALICE CONTEXT AT SVC` trace only logs the shared `__wine_syscall_dispatcher` trampoline `pc` (identical across every syscall id, confirmed: `id=49`/`24`/`30`/`90`/`147`/`44` all show `pc=0x4DA539BC` or a couple of sibling dispatcher addresses) — NOT a caller return address — so the exact guest instruction issuing the SECOND `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`-causing `NtTerminateProcess` genuinely needs a caller-LR-capturing probe or full ARM64 JIT disasm to pin down, out of THIS atom's 30-minute budget. Per task law ("name via donor source/disasm, document, STOP" — not "chase further"), this is the honest stopping point: real, verified forward progress; new status site named as "provably not the C4-43 branch, not-yet-pinned exact call site" rather than a false-confidence guess.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7. **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntsvc.c,ntfile.c,ntfile.h,ntboot.c}` (`NtQueryInformationProcess` class-37 handler, `SII_*` offsets moved to shared `ntfile.h`, `nt_set_main_image_info` stash + `ntboot.c` wiring), commit `963be9c`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-43 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED: wired the C4-42-named `TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer` NULL deref. Disasm-confirmed byte-exact against `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll` (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -d`, ImageBase `0x7bc00000`):

```
7bc0fef9: 64 a1 18 00 00 00    mov    %fs:0x18,%eax
7bc0feff: 8b 80 a8 01 00 00    mov    0x1a8(%eax),%eax   ; TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer
7bc0ff05: 8b 00                mov    (%eax),%eax        ; <- ELR (this is the exact +0xFF05 fault site)
7bc0ff07: 89 ce                mov    %ecx,%esi
7bc0ff09: 85 c0                test   %eax,%eax          ; null-check on the RESULT, not the pointer
7bc0ff0b: 74 3e                je     7bc0ff4b            ; fast empty-activation-stack branch
```

Byte-for-byte match to the C4-42 frontier note and to `wine/include/winternl.h:1087` (`_TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer`, offset `0x1a8`), inside `RtlFindActivationContextSectionString`. This floor's TEB32 builder (`nt_build_wow64_teb32`, `ntsvc.c`) never wired it — only `ImageBaseAddress`/`ProcessParameters` wired so far (C4-29/C4-37).

**Fix (donor-exact, `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:4010-4013`)**:
```c
teb32->ActivationContextStackPointer = PtrToUlong( &teb32->ActivationContextStack );
teb32->ActivationContextStack.FrameListCache.Flink =
    teb32->ActivationContextStack.FrameListCache.Blink =
        PtrToUlong( &teb32->ActivationContextStack.FrameListCache );
```
The SAME real Wine function also wires the 64-bit TEB's `ActivationContextStackPointer` this way (`virtual.c:4027-4030`), the donor-exact precedent the earlier, independently-arrived-at C4-28 64-bit fix (separate heap allocation, same zeroed-`ActiveFrame` semantics) already established for THIS floor. `ACTIVATION_CONTEXT_STACK32` layout (`winternl.h:687-694`): `ActiveFrame`(ULONG)@0x00, `FrameListCache`(`LIST_ENTRY32`, `winternl.h:661-665`, Flink/Blink 4B each)@0x04, `Flags`(ULONG)@0x0C, `NextCookieSequenceNumber`(ULONG)@0x10, `StackId`(ULONG32)@0x14. `_TEB32` (`winternl.h:1084/1087`) embeds this struct in-place at offset `0x184`, with `ActivationContextStackPointer` at `0x1a8` pointing AT it (not a separate allocation) — this floor mirrors that exactly: `TEB32_ACTIVATIONCONTEXTSTACK_OFF=0x184`, wired to point at itself; `FrameListCache` self-linked (Flink==Blink==its own address); `ActiveFrame`/`Flags`/`NextCookieSequenceNumber`/`StackId` left `0` (teb32 is `nt_zalloc`'d, and the fault site's own disasm above only ever reads `ActiveFrame`, null-checking the RESULT — 0 satisfies the fast-path branch, same load-bearing-field-only precedent as C4-28).

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, `zoneA/scratch/c4-43_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, 4313 lines): the C4-42 fault (`NtRaiseException addr=...+0xFF05`) is **GONE** (0 occurrences). Guest runs measurably deeper — syscall trace shows a SECOND `NtQueryInformationProcess` call (real syscall id `25`, confirmed via `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt`; the FIRST call, `ProcessWow64Information` class 26, is already-handled and logged early at transcript line 201 — this second call is silent, i.e. NOT class 0/26) immediately followed by `NtTerminateProcess` (id `44`) with **no intervening fault/exception trace of any kind** — a clean, deliberate guest-driven exit:
```
pe10 ret=3221225595   ; 0xC000007B STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT, NOT ret=42 (no crash — a controlled guest exit)
```
**NEW, DIFFERENT bug class, named via donor source (no ARM64 disasm needed — this is a clean exit, not a fault)**: the second, silent `NtQueryInformationProcess` call matches donor `build_main_module` (`wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c` ~2895-2906) exactly:
```c
SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION info;   // NOTE: no `= {0}` -- unlike the sibling wow64/syscall.c copy
NtQueryInformationProcess( GetCurrentProcess(), ProcessImageInformation /* class 37 */, &info, sizeof(info), NULL );
if (info.ImageCharacteristics & IMAGE_FILE_DLL) {
    MESSAGE( "wine: %s is a dll, not an executable\n", debugstr_us(&params->ImagePathName) );
    NtTerminateProcess( GetCurrentProcess(), STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT );
}
```
This floor's `NtQueryInformationProcess` (`ntsvc.c`) only implements class `0` (`ProcessBasicInformation`) and class `26` (`ProcessWow64Information`); class `37` (`ProcessImageInformation`, `winternl.h:1931`) falls through to a silent `return STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` WITHOUT touching the caller's buffer. Donor's own local is declared uninitialized (no `={0}`), so `info.ImageCharacteristics` is guest-stack garbage — if that garbage happens to have the `IMAGE_FILE_DLL` bit set, `build_main_module` wrongly believes the main image (`hello8_x86.exe`, genuinely an EXE) is a DLL and self-terminates. A genuinely different bug CLASS from every C4-18 through C4-42 gap chased so far (not a TEB/PEB null-pointer crash at all — a missing `NtQueryInformationProcess` class feeding a donor-uninitialized local).

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`zoneA/scratch/c4-43_pe8_gate.log`/`c4-43_pe9_gate.log`/`c4-43_ramfb_gate.log`). **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` (`nt_build_wow64_teb32` + new `TEB32_ACTIVATIONCONTEXTSTACK*`/`TEB32_ACTIVATIONCONTEXTSTACKPOINTER_OFF` defines), commit `9104f4d`. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-42 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED: named + fixed the zero field C4-41 pinned but couldn't source. Chain confirmed byte-for-byte against `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll` (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -d`, ImageBase `0x7bc00000`) + vendored `wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c`:

```
7bc2ee90 <_alloc_module>:
7bc2eea1: call 7bc2b310 <_RtlImageNtHeader@4>   ; ecx(hModule) passed straight through
7bc2eea6: mov %eax,%esi                          ; <- retaddr, matches live caller=+0x2EEA6 exactly
```
`alloc_module(HMODULE hModule, ...)`'s first fastcall arg (`ecx`) feeds `RtlImageNtHeader(hModule)` directly (donor `loader.c:1531-1537`). Its own caller, disasm-confirmed at `syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x2BB06` (matches C4-41's live retaddr byte-for-byte):
```
7bc2bacc: xor %esi,%esi          ; <- build_ntdll_module's own body starts here (reached via a
                                  ;    backward jmp from build_main_module's success path, +0x2c14b)
7bc2badb..bb01:
  push $0x0            ; ReturnLength=NULL
  push $0x1c           ; MemoryInformationLength=sizeof(MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION32)
  lea -0xd4(%ebp),%eax; push %eax   ; &meminfo
  push $0x0            ; MemoryBasicInformation
  push $0x7bc4e0a0     ; <-- LdrInitializeThunk's OWN address (symbol-confirmed below)
  push $0xffffffff     ; NtCurrentProcess()
  call NtQueryVirtualMemory@24
7bc2baf4: mov -0xd0(%ebp),%ebx   ; ebx = meminfo.AllocationBase (offset+4, MBI32 layout)
7bc2bafd: mov %ebx,%ecx         ; ecx(hModule arg to alloc_module) = AllocationBase
7bc2bb01: call 7bc2ee90 <_alloc_module>
7bc2bb06: <- retaddr, matches live alloc_module's-own-retaddr=+0x2BB06 exactly
```
`push $0x7bc4e0a0` confirmed via symbol lookup to be `_LdrInitializeThunk@16`'s own address exactly. This is `build_ntdll_module()` (donor `loader.c:2312-2325`, confirmed identical: `NtQueryVirtualMemory(GetCurrentProcess(), LdrInitializeThunk, MemoryBasicInformation, &meminfo, sizeof(meminfo), NULL); module = meminfo.AllocationBase; wm = alloc_module(module, &nt_name, TRUE);`) — INLINED into `loader_init` by the compiler (no separate symbol), reached unconditionally right after `build_main_module()` succeeds (donor `loader.c:4489-4490`: `wm = build_main_module(); build_ntdll_module();` — confirmed via the `je 7bc2c13c` (build_main_module success) → `jmp 7bc2bace` (build_ntdll_module body) jump pair, disasm-traced).

**Root cause, one level earlier than C4-41's own MBI32-shaping hypothesis**: grepped C4-41's own 3542-line receipt for `BaseAddress==LdrInitializeThunk (0x486DE0A0)` in ANY of the 61 `NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY` lines — **zero matches, in any shape** (neither the raw 32-bit `0x1c` length nor a WOW64-thunked native `0x30` length). The genuine guest call, disasm-proven above to execute unconditionally, never reaches this floor's C `NtQueryVirtualMemory` at all. Traced why: `wow64.dll` here is a REAL, vendored, PE32+ AArch64 binary (`stock/wow64-chain/wow64.dll`) executing as genuine host-native code (not FEX-internal) — its exported `Wow64SystemServiceEx` (`wine/dlls/wow64/syscall.c:1202`, called by FEX's `WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`, `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:449`) dispatches guest syscall id 35 (`NtQueryVirtualMemory`, `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt:27`, confirmed matching this floor's own `NT_SVC_NTQUERYVIRTUALMEMORY=35`, `ntids.h:50` — the syscall NUMBER mapping is correct, ruling out a table-index mismatch) to `wow64_NtQueryVirtualMemory` (`wine/dlls/wow64/virtual.c:568-604`). That thunk gates `MemoryBasicInformation` queries on `(ULONG_PTR)addr > highest_user_address` (`virtual.c:585`) BEFORE ever calling the native `NtQueryVirtualMemory` this floor implements — an early `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` return with the caller's buffer never touched.

`highest_user_address` (`wow64.dll`'s own global, `syscall.c:40`, static default `0x7ffeffff`) is (re)populated exactly once, in `wow64.dll`'s own `process_init` (`syscall.c:996-997`):
```c
NtQuerySystemInformation( SystemEmulationBasicInformation, &info, sizeof(info), NULL );
highest_user_address = (ULONG_PTR)info.HighestUserAddress;   // NO status check
```
This floor's `NtQuerySystemInformation` (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c`) hard-rejected every class except `0` (`SystemBasicInformation`) — `SystemEmulationBasicInformation` is class `62` (`wine/include/winternl.h:2094`) — returning `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and leaving `info` untouched (host stack, effectively zero at this point in `wow64.dll`'s own init). `highest_user_address` therefore collapsed toward `~0`, and `(ULONG_PTR)addr > highest_user_address` is TRUE for essentially every real 32-bit guest address ever queried afterward — silently starving EVERY WOW64 `NtQueryVirtualMemory(MemoryBasicInformation)` guest call this boot, including `build_ntdll_module`'s own (which, per the donor source quoted above, never checks the call's return status either) — `meminfo.AllocationBase` stays at its guest-stack zero-init value, feeding `ecx=0` into `alloc_module` → `RtlImageNtHeader(0)` → the exact NULL `'MZ'`-check AV C4-40/C4-41 pinned.

**Fix, donor-exact** (`wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:7249-7254` — real Wine's own unix-side `NtQuerySystemInformation` treats `SystemEmulationBasicInformation`(62) as a PURE ALIAS of `SystemBasicInformation`(0): `case SystemBasicInformation: case SystemEmulationBasicInformation: ... return NtQuerySystemInformation( SystemBasicInformation, info, len, retlen );`): accept class `62` as class `0` in this floor's `NtQuerySystemInformation`. Also discovered + fixed, same struct writer: `HighestUserAddress@0x28` and `LowestUserAddress@0x20` (`SYSTEM_BASIC_INFORMATION`, `winternl.h:2860-2872`) were NEVER written even for the already-accepted class `0` (buffer zeroed, only `PageSize`/`NumberOfPhysicalPages`/`NumberOfProcessors` filled) — filled `HighestUserAddress=0x7ffeffff` (`wow64.dll`'s own static pre-`process_init` default, the exact value a real Windows 32-bit process reports — not a made-up constant) and `LowestUserAddress=0x10000` (donor-conventional null-page reservation).

Build/gate clean: `pe8` ret=78, `pe9` both backends ret=0, `ramfb` 7/7. Commit `4789ee0` (source-only, `ntsvc.c`).

`pe10 fex` re-run (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `/tmp/gaia-pe10-probe.log`, 3745 lines, archived `chalice/scratch/c4-42_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): **the fix engages exactly as diagnosed**:
```
CHALICE: NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY base=0x486DE0A0 class=0x0 buf=0x4116B390 len=0x30
CHALICE: NtQueryVirtualMemory addr=0x486DE0A0 branch=image allocbase=0x48690000 regionsize=0x1DEF60 state=0x1000 type=0x1000000
```
`build_ntdll_module`'s own call now reaches this floor's native implementation for the first time and answers correctly (`allocbase=0x48690000`, ntdll32's real base). The C4-40/41 terminal fault (`RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)` at `ntdll+0x2B34E`) is **GONE — 0 occurrences** in the new transcript (was the ONLY/terminal fault in C4-40/41).

**NEW, DIFFERENT, FURTHER-FORWARD frontier** (named, NOT chased, per instruction — a genuinely different field/struct from anything this atom touched): boot now runs measurably deeper (past `build_ntdll_module`, into real `kernel32.dll`/`load_dll`-era loader code) before terminating:
```
CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0xFF05
CHALICE: NtRaiseException FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating
pe10 ret=3221225477
```
Disasm (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump`, `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll+0xFF05`, near `_RtlFindActivationContextSectionString@20`):
```
7bc0fef9: mov %fs:0x18,%eax        ; TEB
7bc0feff: mov 0x1a8(%eax),%eax     ; TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer
7bc0ff05: mov (%eax),%eax          ; <- FAULTS: dereferences a NULL ActivationContextStackPointer
```
`0x1a8` matches `ACTIVATION_CONTEXT_STACK *ActivationContextStackPointer` in the 32-bit `_TEB32` layout byte-exact (`wine/include/winternl.h:1087`, `/* 01a8 */` comment in the donor struct itself). This floor's TEB32 builder (C4-29/C4-37) has only ever wired `ImageBaseAddress`/`ProcessParameters` on the PEB32 side — `ActivationContextStackPointer` on the TEB32 side is a DIFFERENT struct, DIFFERENT field, never touched by any prior atom. Candidate next atom: wire a real (or donor-correct empty-sentinel) `ACTIVATION_CONTEXT_STACK` and point `TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer@0x1a8` at it — NOT chased this atom (out of scope, per instruction: name + stop on a new fault).

## C4-40 — FIX APPLIED: `sync_try_guest_exception` (`kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c`) branch, exactly as C4-39's own recon named it:
```c
#define STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT 0x80000002U // wine/include/winnt.h:629
#define ESR_DFSC_ALIGN_FAULT 0x21U
...
if ((ec == 0x24 || ec == 0x25) && (esr & 0x3f) == ESR_DFSC_ALIGN_FAULT) {
    status = STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT;
    params = 0;
} else if (ec == 0x24 || ec == 0x25) {
    status = STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION;
    params = 2;

```
Checked BEFORE the generic Data-Abort branch, mirroring donor `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/signal_arm64.c` `segv_handler`'s own early-return `switch` order (`DFSC==ALIGN_FAULT` case sets `ExceptionCode` and calls `setup_raise_exception` directly, never reaching the `ExceptionInformation[0/1]=WnR/addr; NumberParameters=2;` lines a few lines further down in the SAME case — re-verified this atom against the live donor source, `signal_arm64.c:1123-1130`). `params=0` is therefore the DONOR-EXACT shape, not a simplification. Re-verified FEX's consumer, `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:926`, live source, this atom): `if (Exception->ExceptionCode == EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT && Context::HandleUnalignedAccess(TLS, Context)) return true;` — reads ONLY `ExceptionCode` for this branch (no `ExceptionInformation`/`NumberParameters` dependency), confirming the record-shape audit this atom's brief asked for needed no further change.

**`pe10 fex` receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, full transcript `chalice/scratch/c4-40_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, `3519` lines):

Six alignment faults, all silently self-healed (zero `NtRaiseException` for any of them — grepped the entire transcript, confirmed):
| # | esr | elr | far | wnr | status |
|---|-----|-----|-----|-----|--------|
| 1 | 0x96000061 | 0x2968DD634 | 0x5A060A8E | 1 | 0x80000002 (== C4-38/39's own named fault, byte-identical elr/far) |
| 2 | 0x96000021 | 0x2968E2514 | 0x5A060A4E | 0 | 0x80000002 |
| 3 | 0x96000061 | 0x2968E2524 | 0x488CB716 | 1 | 0x80000002 |
